II.  Software  Requirement  Specification  1.  Product  Overview The  Smart  Apartment  Management  System  (Apora)  is  a  mobile  "Super  App"  that  
completely
 
digitizes
 
workflows
 
among
 
the
 
Landlord,
 
Management
 
Board,
 
Residents,
 
and
 
Operational
 
Staff.
 
It
 
focuses
 
on
 
three
 
core
 
areas:
 
ensuring
 
transparent
 
financial
 
operations
 
via
 
automated
 
billing
 
and
 
secure
 
online
 
payments,
 
streamlining
 
facility
 
maintenance
 
through
 
digital
 
repair
 
tickets,
 
and
 
enhancing
 
the
 
resident
 
experience
 
with
 
seamless
 
tenancy
 
management
 
and
 
real-time
 
Live
 
Chat.
 
 Description  of  External  Entities:  ●  Payment  Gateway  (PayOS):  Receives  secure  checkout  requests  from  the  backend  
API
 
and
 
returns
 
transaction
 
results
 
via
 
Webhook,
 
allowing
 
the
 
system
 
to
 
automatically
 
clear
 
debts,
 
generate
 
digital
 
receipts,
 
and
 
update
 
invoice
 
statuses
 
in
 
real-time.
 ●  Cloudinary  (Image  Storage):  A  cloud  storage  service  that  receives,  optimizes,  and  
distributes
 
media
 
files
 
(Resident
 
identity
 
cards,
 
incident
 
scene
 
verification
 
photos,
 
task
 
completion
 
proofs
,
 
and
 
user
 
avatars)
 
directly
 
via
 
secure
 
HTTPS
 
URLs
 
to
 
minimize
 
database
 
payload
 
and
 
facilitate
 
ACID
 
checkout
 
data
 
purging.
 ●  Firebase  Cloud  Messaging  (FCM):  An  intermediary  service  utilized  to  reliably  push  
background
 
real-time
 
notifications
 
to
 
users'
 
mobile
 
devices
 
regarding
 
ticket
 
updates,
 
new
 
monthly
 
bills,
 
request
 
approval
 
statuses,
 
and
 
community
 
announcements.
 ●  Pusher  (WebSocket):  A  managed,  Serverless-compatible  WebSocket  broker  that  
handles
 
the
 
bidirectional
 
messaging
 
flow
 
(Live
 
Chat)
 
between
 
Residents
 
and
 
Management,
 
ensuring
 
seamless
 
real-time
 
communication
 
without
 
the
 
need
 
for
 
maintaining
 
persistent
 
stateful
 
servers.
 ●  Firebase  Authentication  (OTP  Gateway):  An  external  identity  service  responsible  
for
 
generating,
 
delivering
 
(via
 
SMS),
 
and
 
verifying
 
One-Time
 
Passwords
 
(OTPs)
 
during
 
the
 
password
 
recovery
 
flow.
 
It
 
securely
 
offloads
 
SMS
 
state
 
management
 
from
 
the
 
custom
 
backend
 
and
 
issues
 
verified
 
ID
 
Tokens
 
to
 
authorize
 
password
 
reset
 
requests.

Figure  1-1:  Apora  system  overview
 
As  described  in  Figure  1-1,  the  Apora  system  operates  through  the  following  core  
components
 
and
 
external
 
integrations:
 ●  Apartment  Management  Super  App:  The  central  processing  engine  that  handles  all  
business
 
logic,
 
database
 
operations,
 
and
 
orchestrates
 
data
 
exchange
 
with
 
external
 
cloud
 
services.
 ●  Resident  Module:  The  mobile  interface  for  tenants  to  manage  their  stay,  pay  monthly  
bills,
 
report
 
facility
 
issues,
 
register
 
roommates,
 
and
 
use
 
Live
 
Chat
 
to
 
communicate
 
with
 
the
 
management
 
board.
 ●  Landlord  Module:  The  high-level  management  interface  for  the  property  owner  to  
configure
 
apartment
 
structures
 
(room
 
parameters),
 
manage
 
Manager
 
accounts,
 
and
 
monitor
 
global
 
financial,
 
occupancy
 
data,
 
and
 
operational
 
statistics
 
via
 
the
 
centralized
 
dashboard.
 ●  Manager  /  Management  Module:  The  executive  dashboard  for  management  to  view  
statistics,
 
manage
 
operational
 
staff
 
(Security,
 
Janitor,
 
Technician),
 
process
 
billing,
 
approve
 
tenant/roommate
 
requests,
 
assign
 
maintenance
 
tickets,
 
and
 
broadcast
 
community
 
announcements.

workspace
 
for
 
facility
 
personnel
 
to
 
view
 
assigned
 
maintenance
 
or
 
service
 
tasks,
 
and
 
update
 
real-time
 
task
 
progress
 
along
 
with
 
completion
 
proof
 
photos.
 ●  PayOS  (Payment  Gateway):  Processes  secure  online  transactions  and  sends  
webhook
 
confirmations
 
to
 
the
 
core
 
system
 
for
 
automated
 
debt
 
clearance.
 ●  Cloudinary  (Image  Storage):  Securely  stores  uploaded  media  (Resident  ID  cards,  
incident
 
verification
 
photos,
 
and
 
task
 
completion
 
proofs)
 
and
 
returns
 
optimized
 
HTTPS
 
URLs
 
to
 
the
 
database.
 ●  Pusher  (WebSocket):  The  real-time  communication  broker  powering  instantaneous  
features
 
like
 
the
 
bidirectional
 
Live
 
Chat
 
between
 
residents
 
and
 
management.
 ●  Firebase  FCM  (Push  Notification):  Delivers  background  system  alerts  (new  bills,  
ticket
 
updates,
 
approval
 
decisions,
 
and
 
announcements)
 
directly
 
to
 
users'
 
mobile
 
screens.
 ●  Firebase  Auth  (OTP  Gateway):  Handles  the  secure  generation  and  verification  of  
SMS
 
OTPs
 
for
 
the
 
password
 
recovery
 
flow.
  2.  User  Requirements  2.1  Actors #  Actor  Description  
1  Landlord   
The  building  owner  who  holds  the  highest  authority.  Responsible  for  configuring  apartment  structures  (room  parameters),  managing  Manager  accounts,  and  monitoring  global  financial  and  operational  statistics  via  the  centralized  dashboard.  
2  Manager  
The  property  manager  responsible  for  day-to-day  operations.  Manages  the  apartment  lifecycle  (Check-in/Check-out),  generates  monthly  bills,  approves  resident  requests  (roommates,  stay  extensions),  assigns  maintenance  tasks  to  staff,  posts  community  announcements,  and  handles  resident  inquiries  via  live  chat.  
3  Resident   
The  primary  user  holding  the  apartment  lease.  Responsible  for  paying  monthly  bills,  reporting  facility  issues  (creating  repair  tickets),  updating  demographic  information  (registering  roommates),  requesting  stay  extensions,  and  communicating  with  the  management  board.  
4  Security  Guard  
The  on-site  personnel  handling  building  security,  safety  patrols,  and  access  control.  Within  the  system,  they  are  responsible  for  viewing  assigned  security-related  work  tickets  (e.g.,  broken  locks,  noise  complaints),  updating  task  lifecycle  progress,  and  uploading  completion  proof  photos  upon  resolving  the  issues.  
5  Janitor   The  on-site  facility  personnel  managing  building  hygiene,  cleaning  schedules,  and  sanitation.  Within  the  system,  they  are  responsible  for  
 6  |  Page  
--- PAGE 7 ---
 
viewing  assigned  cleaning  or  sanitation  work  tickets  (e.g.,  waste  disposal,  spillages),  updating  task  lifecycle  progress,  and  uploading  completion  proof  photos  upon  resolving  the  issues.  
 
6  Technician   
The  on-site  technical  personnel  specializing  in  facility  repairs  and  maintenance  (e.g.,  electrical,  plumbing,  HVAC).  Within  the  system,  they  are  responsible  for  viewing  assigned  technical  repair  tickets,  updating  task  lifecycle  progress,  and  uploading  completion  proof  photos  upon  resolving  the  issues.  
5  PayOS  System   
(External  system)  A  payment  gateway  that  processes  transactions  and  triggers  Webhooks  to  return  results  to  the  Backend  Server.   
6  
Firebase  Auth  System   
(External  system)  An  identity  platform  utilized  to  securely  generate  and  deliver  One-Time  Passwords  (OTPs)  via  SMS  during  password  recovery,  and  issue  verified  verification  tokens  back  to  the  central  system.   
7  Pusher  System   
(External  system)  A  managed  WebSocket  broker  that  handles  the  bidirectional  real-time  messaging  flow  between  Residents  and  the  Management  Board  for  the  Live  Chat  feature.   
8  Cloudinary  
(External  system)  A  cloud-based  media  storage  service  that  receives,  optimizes,  and  stores  uploaded  files  (ID  cards,  avatars,  and  incident  photos),  returning  secure  HTTPS  URLs  to  the  core  system  to  minimize  database  payload.   
9  Firebase  FCM   
(External  system)  An  intermediary  messaging  service  used  to  reliably  push  background  real-time  alerts  (Push  Notifications)  to  users'  mobile  OS  regarding  new  bills,  ticket  updates,  and  community  announcements.

 2.2.2  Descriptions   
ID  Group  function  Use  Case  Actors  Use  Case  Description  &  Main  Flow  
1  
Account  &  Profile  Management  Login  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Validates  user  credentials  using  their  phone  number  and  displays  a  role-selection  interface  if  the  account  possesses  multiple  roles.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  enters  Phone  Number  and  Password.  2.  System  validates  credentials.  3.  System  displays  role-selection  popup  (if  applicable).  4.  System  logs  user  in  and  redirects  to  dashboard.  
2  
Account  &  Profile  Management  Log  out  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Terminates  the  active  session,  clears  local  authentication  tokens,  and  disassociates  the  device's  push  notification  token  from  the  database.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  clicks  "Logout".  2.  System  terminates  the  active  session.  3.  System  clears  local  tokens  and  removes  FCM  token.  4.  System  redirects  to  the  Login  screen.  
3  
Account  &  Profile  Management  Forgot  Password  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Allows  users  to  reset  their  account  password  by  verifying  their  identity  via  a  secure  SMS  OTP  code.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  enters  registered  Phone  Number.  2.  System  sends  an  OTP  via  SMS.  3.  User  enters  the  valid  OTP.  4.  User  sets  a  new  password.  5.  System  saves  the  new  password  and  displays  a  success  message.  
4  
Account  &  Profile  Management  
View  Personal  Profile  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Displays  detailed  personal  information  of  the  logged-in  user,  including  identity  details,  contact  info,  and  assigned  system  roles.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  navigates  to  the  Profile  tab.  2.  System  retrieves  user  data  from  the  database.  3.  System  displays  personal  details,  contact  info,  and  roles.  
 11  |  Page  
--- PAGE 12 ---
 
5  
Account  &  Profile  Management  
Update  Personal  Profile  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Enables  users  to  update  non-sensitive  personal  details,  modify  contact  information,  and  upload  or  change  their  profile  avatar.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  clicks  "Edit  Profile".  2.  User  updates  contact  info  or  uploads  a  new  avatar.  3.  User  clicks  "Save".  4.  System  updates  the  data  and  refreshes  the  screen.  
6  
Tenancy  &  Roommate  Management  View  Contract  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  main  resident  to  view  their  legal  tenancy  timeline,  lease  start/end  dates,  and  the  remaining  days  of  their  staying  contract.  Main  Flow:  1.  Resident  accesses  the  Contract  section.  2.  System  retrieves  contract  details.  3.  System  displays  tenancy  timeline,  dates,  and  remaining  days.  
7  
Tenancy  &  Roommate  Management  
Request  Stay  Extension  Resident  
Description:  Enables  residents  to  submit  an  official  request  to  extend  their  staying  period,  specifying  the  desired  new  end  date  and  attaching  supporting  notes.  Main  Flow:  1.  Resident  opens  the  "Extend  Stay"  form.  2.  Resident  selects  the  desired  end  date  and  enters  a  reason.  3.  Resident  clicks  "Submit".  4.  System  saves  the  request  with  a  "Pending"  status.  
8  
Tenancy  &  Roommate  Management  
View  Stay  Extension  Request  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Provides  a  centralized  dashboard  for  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  view  all  pending,  approved,  and  rejected  stay  extension  requests.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager  /Landlord  navigates  to  the  Extension  Management  page.  2.  System  retrieves  all  requests.  3.  System  displays  the  list  categorized  by  status.  
9  
Tenancy  &  Roommate  Management  
Approve  Stay  Extension  Request  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  approve  or  reject  a  stay  extension  request,  which  automatically  updates  the  resident's  contract  duration  upon  approval.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager  /Landlord  selects  a  pending  request.  2.  Manager  /Landlord  clicks  "Approve"  (or  "Reject").  3.  System  updates  the  request  status.  4.  System  automatically  extends  contract  termination  date.  
 12  |  Page  
--- PAGE 13 ---
 
5.  System  dispatches  push  notification  to  resident.  
10  
Tenancy  &  Roommate  Management  
View  Roommate  List  Resident  
Description:  Displays  the  list  of  registered  roommates  (sub-tenants)  within  a  specific  apartment.  Sub-tenants  are  saved  as  data  records  without  independent  login  accounts.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  accesses  the  Roommate  list  for  an  apartment.  2.  System  retrieves  sub-tenant  records.  3.  System  displays  the  list  of  roommates.  
11  
Tenancy  &  Roommate  Management  
Register  Roommate  Resident  
Description:  Allows  the  primary  resident  to  register  a  new  roommate  by  providing  personal  details,  phone  number,  and  identity  card  (CCCD)  photos  for  legal  registration.  Main  Flow:  1.  Resident  opens  the  Registration  form.  2.  Resident  inputs  roommate's  details  and  uploads  CCCD  photos.  3.  Resident  clicks  "Submit".  4.  System  creates  a  new  registration  request.  
12  
Tenancy  &  Roommate  Management  
Approve  Roommate  Request  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  review,  verify  identity  documents,  and  approve  or  reject  roommate  registration  requests.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager  /Landlord  opens  a  roommate  registration  request.  2.  Manager  /Landlord  reviews  the  details  and  identity  documents.  3.  Manager  /Landlord  clicks  "Approve"  (or  "Reject").  4.  System  adds  the  valid  roommate  to  the  apartment's  occupancy  list.  
13  
Billing  &  Payment  Management  
Input  Monthly  Bills  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  generate  monthly  service,  electricity,  and  water  bills,  save  invoices  to  the  database,  and  send  push  notifications  to  residents.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager  /Landlord  accesses  the  Billing  page.  2.  Manager  /Landlord  inputs  utility  indicators  and  fees.  3.  Manager  /Landlord  clicks  "Generate  Bills".  4.  System  calculates  totals  and  saves  invoices.  5.  System  sends  push  notifications  to  residents.  
 13  |  Page  
--- PAGE 14 ---
 
14  
Billing  &  Payment  Management  
View  Transaction  History  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  Manager,  and  Landlord  to  view  a  list  of  completed  payments  and  access  specific  transaction  details.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  navigates  to  Transaction  History.  2.  System  displays  a  list  of  completed  payments.  3.  User  clicks  on  a  transaction  to  view  detailed  information.  
15  
Billing  &  Payment  Management  
View  Invoice  List  &  Details  Resident  
Description:  Allows  residents  to  view  their  paid  and  unpaid  invoices  along  with  detailed  charges.  Main  Flow:  1.  Resident  navigates  to  the  Invoices  tab.  2.  System  displays  lists  of  Paid  and  Unpaid  invoices.  3.  Resident  selects  an  invoice  to  view  the  fee  breakdown.  
16  
Billing  &  Payment  Management  Pay  Bills  Resident  
Description:  Enables  residents  to  pay  unpaid  invoices  via  the  PayOS  payment  gateway,  automatically  processing  the  transaction  and  updating  the  invoice  status  via  Webhook.  Main  Flow:  1.  Resident  selects  an  unpaid  invoice  and  clicks  "Pay".  2.  System  calls  the  PayOS  gateway.  3.  Resident  completes  the  transfer.  4.  Webhook  receives  the  success  signal.  5.  System  automatically  updates  the  invoice  status  to  "Paid".  
17  
Billing  &  Payment  Management  
View  Payment  Receipt  Resident  
Description:  Generates  and  displays  a  digital  receipt  containing  the  transaction  reference,  timestamp,  and  amount  after  a  successful  payment.  Main  Flow:  1.  Resident  clicks  "View  Receipt"  after  a  successful  payment.  2.  System  generates  a  digital  receipt  with  transaction  details.  3.  System  displays  the  receipt  on  the  screen.  
18  
Incident  &  Repair  Management  View  Ticket  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  review  the  status,  priority,  and  details  of  incident  tickets  reported  by  residents.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager/Landlord  accesses  the  Ticket  Management  page.  2.  The  system  displays  the  list  of  submitted  tickets.  3.  Manager/Landlord  clicks  a  ticket  to  review  its  details  and  priority.  
 14  |  Page  
--- PAGE 15 ---
 
19  
Incident  &  Repair  Management  Create  Ticket  Resident  
Description:  Allows  residents  to  submit  a  new  issue  ticket  by  providing  the  category,  description,  and  attached  images.  Main  Flow:  1.  The  resident  opens  the  "Report  Issue"  form.  2.  The  resident  selects  a  category,  enters  a  description,  and  uploads  images.  3.  The  resident  clicks  "Submit".  4.  The  system  creates  a  new  ticket  with  an  "PENDING"  status.  
20  
Incident  &  Repair  Management  
Update  Ticket  Status  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  update  the  current  status  of  a  ticket  and  add  internal  notes  or  feedback  for  the  resident.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager/Landlord  opens  an  existing  ticket.  2.  Manager/Landlord  changes  the  status  and  enters  internal  notes/feedback.  3.  Manager/Landlord  clicks  "Update".  4.  System  saves  the  new  status  and  notes.  
21  
Incident  &  Repair  Management  Assign  Task  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  assign  pending  repair  or  maintenance  tickets  to  specific  staff  members.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager/Landlord  opens  a  pending  ticket.  2.  Manager/Landlord  selects  a  staff  member  from  the  assignment  list.  3.  Manager/Landlord  clicks  "Assign".  4.  System  updates  the  ticket  and  notifies  the  assigned  staff.  
22  
Incident  &  Repair  Management  View  Task  
Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician  
Description:  Allows  Security  Guard,  Manager  and  Landlord to  view  the  list  of  assigned  repair  or  maintenance  tasks  that  require  action.   Main  Flow:  1.  Staff  accesses  the  "My  Tasks"  screen.  2.  System  retrieves  assigned  tickets.  3.  System  displays  the  list  of  pending  tasks.  
23  
Incident  &  Repair  Management  Update  Progress  
Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician  
Description:  Allows  staff  to  update  the  progress  of  an  assigned  task  by  uploading  completion  images  and  marking  the  task  as  complete.  Main  Flow:  1.  Staff  opens  an  assigned  task.  2.  Staff  performs  the  work  and  uploads  a  completion  image.  3.  Staff  clicks  "Mark  as  Complete".  4.  System  updates  the  task  progress  to  completed.  
 15  |  Page  
--- PAGE 16 ---
 
24  Communication  
Announce  Notification  Manager,  Landlord  
Description:  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  publish  general  building  announcements  with  titles,  content,  and  images  to  the  residents'  home  page.  Main  Flow:  1.  Manager/Landlord  opens  the  "Create  Announcement"  page.  2.  Manager/Landlord  inputs  the  title,  content,  and  attaches  images.  3.  Manager/Landlord  clicks  "Publish".  4.  System  posts  the  announcement  to  the  residents'  dashboard.  
25  Communication  
View  Notification  List  Resident  
Description:  Allows  residents  to  view  a  chronologically  sorted  list  of  general  building  announcements  and  personal  updates.  Main  Flow:  1.  Residents  access  the  Notifications  tab.  2.  System  retrieves  notification  history.  3.  The  system  displays  the  chronologically  sorted  list.  
26  Communication  
View  Notification  Detail  Resident  
Description:  Displays  the  full  content,  timestamp,  and  any  attached  images  or  links  of  a  specific  notification  for  residents.  Main  Flow:  1.  The  resident  clicks  on  a  specific  notification.  2.  The  system  displays  the  full  message  body,  timestamp,  and  attachments.  
27  Communication  
Receive  Notification  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Automatically  triggers  and  delivers  real-time  push  notifications  to  users'  mobile  devices  when  important  events  occur  (e.g.,  new  invoices,  announcements,  ticket  updates).  Main  Flow:  1.  System  detects  a  critical  event.  2.  System  generates  a  push  notification  payload.  3.  System  sends  the  payload  via  FCM.  4.  User's  device  receives  and  displays  the  notification  in  real-time.  
 16  |  Page  
--- PAGE 17 ---
 
28  Communication  Use  Live  Chat  
Resident,  manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Enables  real-time  messaging  between  residents  and  the  Manager/Landlord  for  support  and  inquiries.  Main  Flow:  1.  User  opens  the  Support  Chat  interface.  2.  User  types  a  message  and  hits  "Send".  3.  System  transmits  the  message  via  WebSocket.  4.  Message  appears  instantly  on  the  recipient's  screen.  
29  
Statistics  &  Administration  
View  Apartment  List  
Resident,  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard,  Technician,  Janitor  
Description:  Allows  the  Landlord,  Manager  to  view  an  overview  of  all  apartment  room  numbers,  their  current  occupancy  status,  and  owner  names.  Main  Flow:  1.  Landlord,  Manager  navigates  to  the  Apartment  Management  page.  2.  System  retrieves  all  apartment  records.  3.  System  displays  the  list  with  room  numbers,  status,  and  owner  names.  
30  
Statistics  &  Administration  
View  Apartment  Detail  
Landlord,  Manager,  Security  Guard,  Technician  
Description:  Allows  the  Landlord,  Manager  to  view  comprehensive  data  for  a  specific  apartment,  including  owner  info,  registered  roommates,  and  billing/ticket  history.  Main  Flow:  1.  Landlord,  Manager  clicks  on  a  specific  apartment.  2.  System  queries  the  database  for  detailed  records.  3.  System  displays  owner  info,  roommates,  and  history.  
31  
Statistics  &  Administration  
Create  New  Apartment  Landlord

Landlord  clicks  "Add  New  Apartment".  2.  Landlord  enters  room  number,  floor,  size,  and  rent.  3.  Landlord  clicks  "Save".  4.  System  creates  a  new  apartment  record  with  an  "Empty"  status.

Landlord  clicks  "Edit"  on  an  apartment.  2.  Landlord  updates  the  desired  parameters.  3.  Landlord  clicks  "Save".  4.  System  updates  the  record  in  the  database.

3.  Software  Features  3.1  Functional  Overview 3.1.1  Screens  Flow   
  
 
 
 
  
 
  22  |  Page  

--- PAGE 23 ---
 
3.1.2  Screen  Descriptions #  Feature  Screen  Description  1  Authentication  Login  The  initial  entry  screen  for  users  when  their  token  is  invalid  or  expired.  Users  input  credentials  to  access  the  app  or  click  "Forgot  Password"  to  initiate  recovery.  2  Authentication  Forgot  Password  Allows  users  to  request  a  password  reset.  Clicking  the  "Send  OTP"  button  triggers  an  authorization  code  and  navigates  to  the  next  step.  3  Authentication  Reset  Password  The  screen  where  users  input  the  received  OTP  and  set  up  a  new  password.  Clicking  "Submit  /  Change  Password"  finalizes  the  process  and  redirects  to  Login.  4  Profile  Profile  A  globally  shared  interface  accessible  from  various  dashboards  allowing  users  (Resident,  Security,  Manager)  to  view,  manage,  and  edit  their  personal  information.  5  Communication Live  Chat  The  real-time  messaging  interface  where  residents  and  management  can  communicate  directly  for  support,  inquiries,  and  assistance.  6  Communication Notification  List  A  centralized  hub  accessed  via  the  "Notification"  tab,  displaying  a  list  of  all  system  alerts,  messages,  and  updates  sent  to  the  user.  7  Communication Notification  Detail  
Displays  the  full,  detailed  content  of  a  specific  notification  when  the  user  selects  it  from  the  Notification  List.  8  Resident  Resident  Home  The  central  dashboard  for  residents.  It  provides  quick  access  navigation  to  the  news  feed,  billing,  visitor  management,  ticket  tracking,  roommate  status,  live  chat,  and  notifications.  9  Resident  News  Feed  A  dedicated  feed  accessed  from  the  Resident  Home,  displaying  announcements,  articles,  and  general  updates  broadcasted  by  the  management.  10  Resident  Billing  List  Displays  a  historical  list  of  all  bills  (electricity,  water,  management  fees)  issued  to  the  apartment.  Residents  can  select  a  specific  bill  to  view  details.  11  Resident  Billing  Detail  Shows  the  itemized  breakdown  of  the  selected  bill.  Features  a  "Pay  Now"  button  to  forward  the  user  to  the  payment  gateway.  12  Resident  PayOS  Payment  The  secure  online  payment  interface  powered  by  PayOS  where  residents  complete  their  financial  transactions.  13  Resident  Contract  Management  
Manages  contract  details  and  registered  visitors  for  the  apartment.  Residents  can  trigger  guest  registration  or  request  a  contract  extension  from  here.  14  Resident  Request  Extension  
A  form  interface  where  residents  can  formally  request  to  extend  their  apartment  lease  or  tenancy  agreement.  
 23  |  Page  
--- PAGE 24 ---
 
15  Resident  Ticket  List  A  tracking  screen  where  residents  can  view  the  list  and  real-time  status  of  their  submitted  issues,  complaints,  or  maintenance  requests.  16  Resident  Create  Ticket  A  form  where  residents  fill  out  details,  describe  issues,  and  upload  pictures  regarding  maintenance  or  complaints  for  the  management  team.  17  Resident  Roommate  List  Displays  all  current  occupants  registered  under  the  resident's  apartment.  Features  a  "Register  Roommate"  button  to  add  a  new  member.  18  Resident  Register  Roommate  
A  registration  form  used  to  input  personal  details  and  legal  documentation  to  add  a  new  roommate  to  the  apartment  record.  19  Staff  Security  Task  The  main  landing  dashboard  for  the  staff/Security  staff.  It  showcases  active  tasks  and  automatically  flashes  urgent  alerts  when  a  resident  triggers  an  SOS.  20  Staff  Task  List  A  dedicated  view  gathering  all  active  maintenance  and  security  tickets  assigned  to  the  staff.  Selecting  a  task  reveals  its  details.  21  Staff  Task  Detail  Displays  specific  information  regarding  an  assigned  problem.  Contains  buttons  like  "Process  Task"  or  "Update  Status"  to  log  progress  in  real-time.  22  Manager  Management  Dashboard  
The  primary  operations  command  board  for  the  Manager.  It  links  to  staff  management,  financial  billing,  news  posting,  apartment  configurations,  and  global  tickers.  23  Manager  Manager  Management  
A  centralized  control  board  accessed  from  the  Manager  Dashboard  for  monitoring,  tracking,  and  managing  all  Manager  accounts.  24  Manager  Manager  Detail  Displays  the  specific  profile  information,  historical  activity,  and  access  levels  of  an  individual  Manager  account.  25  Manager  Create  Manager  An  onboarding  form  used  to  generate  a  new  Manager  account  and  configure  its  operational  permissions.  26  Manager  Manage  Staff  A  management  hub  where  the  Manager  can  add,  monitor,  review,  and  configure  system  permissions  for  building  staff  (including  staffs).  27  Manager  Create  Staff  A  form  interface  enabling  the  Manager  to  input  details  and  generate  new  system  accounts  for  building  staff  or  security  personnel.  28  Manager  Staff  Detail  Shows  the  specific  profile,  assigned  permissions,  and  active  status  of  an  individual  staff  member  selected  from  the  staff  management  list.  
 24  |  Page  
--- PAGE 25 ---
 
29  Manager  Manage  Transaction  
A  comprehensive  overview  of  the  building's  financial  logs  and  transaction  histories.  Features  a  "Generate  Monthly  Bills"  button  to  automate  billing  cycles.  30  Manager  Input  Bills  A  data  entry  interface  for  the  Manager  to  manually  input  utility  meters  (electricity,  water)  or  extra  service  charges  to  generate  monthly  resident  statements.  31  Manager  View  Ticket  A  global  monitoring  screen  where  Manager  reviews  all  user-reported  tickets.  Manager  can  delegate  work  by  clicking  the  "Assign  Ticket  to  Staff"  button.  32  Manager  Assign  Task  A  dispatch  screen  enabling  Manager  to  assign  or  forward  an  open  resident  ticket  to  a  specific  staff  or  technical  team  member.  33  Manager  Post  News  An  announcement  creation  studio  allowing  Manager  to  write  articles  for  the  News  Feed  or  push  real-time  broadcasts  via  the  "Post  Notification"  option.  34  Manager  Manage  Apartment  
An  operational  dashboard  to  manage  apartment  statuses,  coordinate  check-ins/check-outs,  and  view  pending  resident  approvals.  35  Manager  Create  Apartment  
A  data  entry  form  allowing  the  Manager  to  define  unit  parameters  and  add  a  new  apartment  to  the  building's  system.  36  Manager  Check  in  An  onboarding  screen  used  to  process  newly  arriving  residents,  register  their  basic  entry  data,  and  initialize  their  system  accounts.  37  Manager  Check-out  and  Anonymize  
A  move-out  processing  screen  that  checks  out  departing  residents  and  runs  personal  data  anonymization  routines  to  comply  with  data  privacy  regulations.  38  Manager  Apartment  Detail  Displays  descriptive  records  for  a  specific  unit,  including  size  metrics,  historical  bills,  active  tenants,  and  maintenance  timelines.  39  Manager  View  Request  A  centralized  approval  queue  where  Manager  handles  pending  requests  submitted  by  residents,  splitting  into  extension  or  roommate  approvals.  40  Manager  View  Extension  Request  
A  specific  review  interface  displaying  detailed  parameters  of  a  resident's  contract  extension  request  for  Manager  validation  (Approve/Reject).  41  Manager  View  Roommate  Request  
A  vetting  interface  showing  personal  files  of  a  proposed  new  roommate,  allowing  Manager  to  grant  or  deny  apartment  residency.   3.1.3  Screen  Authorization #  Screen  /  Action   Resident  Security   Manager  Landlord  1  Login  X  X  X  X

3.1.4  Non-Screen  Functions   
#  
Feature  System  Function  
Description  
1  
Billing   PayOS  Webhook  Listener   
Backend  automatically  listens  to  Server-to-Server  requests  from  PayOS,  verifies  the  HMAC  hash  signature,  and  updates  the  invoice  status  to  PAID  —  preventing  transaction  loss  due  to  premature  app  closure.   
2  
Manager Data  Anonymization  Job   
When  an  manager  performs  Check-out,  the  system  silently  calls  the  Cloudinary  API  to  delete  the  resident's  ID  card  photos,  masks  all  ID  numbers  in  the  database,  and  unassigns  the  owner  from  the  unit.   
3  
Communication   
Push  Notification  
The  system  extracts  Device  Tokens  from  the  database  and  calls  the  Firebase  Cloud  Messaging  API  to  push  real-time  notifications  when  key  events  occur  (e.g.,  ticket  assigned,  invoice  issued,  roommate  request  submitted,  visitor  checked  in).   
4  
Communication   Assign  Notification   
When  an  manager  assigns  a  ticket  to  a  staff  member,  the  system  automatically  triggers  a  push  notification  to  that  staff  member's  device  via  FCM.   
 
3.1.5  Entity  Relationship  Diagram  
 
Entities  Description  
#  Entity  Description  1  User  Stores  account  information  of  all  system  users,  including  Landlords,  Managers,  Security  Guards,  and  Residents.  It  is  used  for  authentication,  role-based  access  control,  account  status  
 27  |  Page  

--- PAGE 28 ---
 
management,  profile  information,  notification  delivery,  chat  messages,  repair  ticket  handling,  task  assignment,  payment  records,  and  stay  extension  review  actions.  
2  Apartment  Stores  physical  and  occupancy  information  of  apartments,  including  unit  number,  floor,  current  owner  or  main  resident,  and  apartment  status.  Each  apartment  can  be  linked  to  contracts,  roommates,  repair  tickets,  and  historical  residential  data.  
3  Contract  Stores  tenancy  or  residential  contract  information  between  a  resident  and  an  apartment.  It  records  the  apartment,  resident,  contract  start  and  end  dates,  rent  snapshot,  and  contract  status  to  preserve  historical  rental  data  for  auditing  and  invoice  generation.  
4  Invoice  Stores  monthly  billing  information  generated  from  a  contract,  including  billing  month,  electricity  and  water  consumption,  rent  snapshot,  management  fee  snapshot,  total  amount,  and  payment  status.  It  is  used  by  residents  to  view  and  pay  bills.  
5  Roommate  Stores  information  of  additional  residents  living  in  an  apartment,  including  personal  details,  phone  number,  citizen  identification  number,  CCCD  image  URLs,  and  approval  status.  Roommates  are  linked  to  apartments  but  do  not  necessarily  have  independent  login  accounts.  
6  Repair  Ticket  Stores  maintenance  or  incident  reports  submitted  by  residents  for  apartments.  It  includes  issue  category,  description,  before-repair  images,  ticket  status,  internal  notes,  and  timestamps  for  ticket  creation  and  updates.  
7  News  Stores  general  building  announcements  created  by  Managers  or  Landlords.  It  includes  announcement  title,  content,  optional  image,  author  information,  publication  status,  and  timestamps  for  creation  and  updates.  
8  Chat  Room  Stores  chat  session  information  between  a  resident  and  assigned  support  staff,  such  as  Manager,  Landlord,  or  Security  Guard.  It  keeps  the  latest  message  preview  and  timestamps  for  chat  room  creation  and  updates.  
9  Message  Stores  individual  chat  messages  sent  inside  chat  rooms.  Each  message  belongs  to  a  chat  room  and  is  linked  to  the  user  who  sent  it.  
10  Task  Stores  work  assignments  created  from  repair  tickets.  It  records  the  related  ticket,  assigned  staff  member,  assigner,  task  title,  task  description,  progress  notes,  completion  images,  task  status,  assigned  time,  and  completion  time.  
11  Device  Token  Stores  Firebase  Cloud  Messaging  tokens  of  user  devices  for  push  notification  delivery.  It  records  the  device  token,  device  type,  
 28  |  Page  
--- PAGE 29 ---
 
device  name,  token  status,  last  used  time,  creation  time,  and  revocation  time.  
12  Password  Reset  OTP  Stores  OTP  records  used  for  the  forgot  password  process.  It  includes  the  phone  number,  OTP  code,  expiration  time,  usage  status,  failed  attempt  count,  and  creation  time.  
13  Stay  Extension  Stores  stay  extension  requests  submitted  by  residents.  It  records  the  related  contract,  requester,  current  end  date,  requested  new  end  date,  reason,  approval  status,  reviewer,  review  time,  rejection  reason,  and  creation  time.  
14  Payment  Stores  payment  transaction  records  for  invoices.  It  includes  the  related  invoice,  paying  resident,  PayOS  order  ID,  transaction  code,  paid  amount,  payment  method,  payment  status,  payment  time,  and  creation  time.  
15  Notification  Stores  personal  or  system  notifications  delivered  to  users.  It  includes  receiver  information,  title,  body,  notification  type,  related  reference  ID,  read  status,  creation  time,  and  read  time.   
3.1.6  Entity  Details 1.  User  
#  Attribute  name  PK  Type  Mandatory  Description  1  id  x  int  Yes  Unique  identifier  of  the  user.  
2  phone_number   String  Yes  User  phone  number  used  as  the  login  username.  Must  be  unique.  
3  password_hash   String  Yes  Hashed  password  used  for  authentication.  
4  full_name   String  Yes  Full  name  of  the  user.  
5  avatar_url   String  No  URL  of  the  user  avatar  image.  
6  roles   String  Array  
Yes  List  of  roles  assigned  to  the  user.  Possible  values:  LANDLORD,  MANAGER,  SECURITY_GUARD,  RESIDENT,  JANITOR,  TECHNICIAN.  
7  status   String  Yes  Account  status.  Possible  values:  ACTIVE,  INACTIVE.  
8  created_at   Timestamp  Yes  Date  and  time  when  the  user  account  was  created.   2.  Apartment   
 29  |  Page  
--- PAGE 30 ---
 
#  Attribute  name  PK  Type  Mandatory  Description  1  id  x  int  Yes  Unique  identifier  of  the  apartment.  
2  unit_number   String  Yes  Apartment  unit  number.  Must  be  unique.

3.2  Account  &  Profile  Management  3.2.1  FID-01  -  Mobile  Login  /  UC01  Role-based  Login  
 
 
Table  3.2.1.1:  Screen  Definition  
 
#  Field  Name  Type  Mandatory  
Max  Length  
Description  
1  Phone  Number  (Username)  
Text  Field  Yes  15  The  unique  phone  number  used  as  the  login  username  
2  Password  Password  Field  
Yes  50  The  user's  plain-text  password,  which  the  system  verifies  against  the  stored  hash.  
3  Login  Button  Button  -  -  Triggers  the  account  and  role  validation  process.  
 39  |  Page  

--- PAGE 40 ---
 
4  Forgot  Password  Link  
Link  -  -  Redirects  the  user  to  the  Forgot  Password  recovery  flow  (UC03).  
5  Error  Message  Text  -  -  Displays  an  error  notification  if  the  Phone  Number  or  Password  entered  is  incorrect  (MSG02).1 
 
3.2.1.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC01  Use  Case  Name  Role-based  Login  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  Version  1.0  Actor  Resident,  Landlord,  Manager,  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician  
Description  Allows  all  authorized  users  (Resident,  Landlord,  Manager,  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician)  to  securely  access  the  mobile  application  using  their  Phone  Number  and  Password.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  Manager  has  created  the  account  and  provided  the  default  password.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  User  opens  the  application,  enters  credentials,  and  taps  the  "Login"  button.  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  validates  the  user  and  redirects  them  to  the  appropriate  home  screen  based  on  their  role.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
 40  |  Page  
--- PAGE 41 ---
 
1  User  Opens  the  app,  enters  Phone  number  and  Password.  
2  System  Validates  the  user's  account  and  role.  
3  System  If  first  login  with  default  password,  redirects  the  user  to  the  Change  Password  flow  (UC05).  
4  System  Redirects  Resident  to  Resident  Home,  Landlord  and  Manager  to  Dashboard,  and  staff  (Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician)  to  Task  List.  
 
 
Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Phone  number  or  Password  is  incorrect:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Incorrect  Phone  number  or  password.  Please  check  again."  (MSG02) 
2.2  System  Remains  on  the  Login  screen  and  waits  for  the  user  to  correct  the  input.  
AT2  At  Step  1  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  mandatory  Phone  number  field  is  left  empty:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error  message:  "Please  enter  Phone  number."  (MSG01)   
1.2  System  Blocks  the  login  submission  process.   
AT3  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  account  status  is  INACTIVE  (BR-04):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
 41  |  Page  
--- PAGE 42 ---
 
2.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Your  account  has  been  deactivated.  Please  contact  management."  
2.2  System  Remains  on  the  Login  screen.  
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Account  Provisioning:  The  system  does  not  allow  users  to  freely  register.  Accounts  are  exclusively  provisioned  by  the  Management  Board  with  a  default  password.  Users  must  change  their  password  upon  their  first  login.  
BR-02  Subject  Identification:  The  Phone  number  used  as  the  login  username  must  be  unique  across  the  entire  system.  
BR-03  Security:  User  passwords  must  be  one-way  encrypted  using  the  bcrypt  algorithm  before  being  saved  to  the  PostgreSQL  database.  
BR-04  Inactive  Account:  Accounts  with  status  INACTIVE (e.g.,  following  a  checkout  process)  are  not  permitted  to  log  into  the  system.  
 42  |  Page  
--- PAGE 43 ---
 
3.2.2  FID-02  -  Logout  /  UC02  Logout  
 
 
Table  3.2.2.1:  Screen  Definition  
 
#  Field  Name  Type  Mandatory  
Max  Length  
Description  
1  Logout  Button  /  Link  
Button  /  Link  
-  -  Button  or  link  (typically  located  on  the  Profile/Settings  screen)  that  initiates  the  logout  process.  
2  Confirmation  Dialog  
System  Popup  
Yes  -  A  modal  dialog  displayed  to  confirm  the  user's  intention  to  log  out  
 43  |  Page  

--- PAGE 44 ---
 
(Example:  "Are  you  sure  you  want  to  log  out?").  
3  Confirm  Button  Button  -  -  Activates  the  session  termination  and  redirects  to  the  Login  screen.  
 
3.2.2.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC02  Use  Case  Name  Log  out   Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  
Version  1.0  Actor  Resident,  Landlord,  Manager,  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician  
Description  Terminates  the  user's  current  session,  clears  local  authentication  tokens,  and  disassociates  the  device's  FCM  Token  to  prevent  receiving  push  notifications.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  User  has  successfully  logged  in  and  has  an  active  session  (JWT  Token).   
Trigger  TRG-01:  User  clicks  the  "Log  out"  button  within  the  application.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  User  session  is  terminated.  POS-02:  FCM  Token  is  removed.  POS-03:  User  is  redirected  to  Login  screen.    
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Clicks  the  "Log  out"  button  or  link.   
2  System  Displays  a  confirmation  dialog.   
 44  |  Page  
--- PAGE 45 ---
 
3  User  Clicks  the  'Confirm'  button  on  the  confirmation  dialog.    
4  System  Calls  the  logout  API  to  delete  the  FCM  Token  from  the  system.   
5  System  Clears  local  storage  (authentication  tokens,  session  data).   
6  System  Redirects  the  user  to  the  Login  Screen  (UC01).   
 
 
Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  cancels  the  logout  action:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  User  Clicks  the  "Cancel"  button  on  the  confirmation  dialog.  
3.2  System  Closes  the  dialog,  and  the  user  remains  logged  in  on  the  current  screen.  
AT2  At  Step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  fails  to  remove  the  FCM  token  from  the  server  (network/server  error):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Logs  the  server-side  error  but  continues  with  client-side  actions.   
4.2  System  Clears  local  storage  and  redirects  the  user  to  the  Login  Screen  (UC01),  displaying  a  temporary  error  notification.   
AT3  At  Step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  logout  API  call  fails  because  the  JWT  has  already  expired  or  is  invalid  (HTTP  401):  
 45  |  Page  
--- PAGE 46 ---
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Treats  the  session  as  already  terminated  and  proceeds  gracefully,  without  surfacing  the  401  error  to  the  user.  
4.2  System  Clears  local  storage  (authentication  tokens,  session  data)  and  redirects  the  user  to  the  Login  Screen  (UC01).  
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Session  Invalidation:  The  logout  process  must  explicitly  invalidate  the  user's  active  JSON  Web  Token  (JWT)  on  the  server  side  to  prevent  unauthorized  reuse.  
BR-02  Push  Notification  Disassociation  (FCM  Disassociation):  The  FCM  Token  linked  to  the  logging-out  device  must  be  removed  from  the  database  to  stop  sending  push  notifications  to  that  device.  
BR-03  Client-side  Security:  All  locally  stored  login  information  and  tokens  on  the  device  must  be  completely  erased  upon  successful  logout.  
BR-04  Redirection:  Upon  successful  logout,  the  system  must  redirect  the  user  back  to  the  Login  Screen  (UC01).  
 46  |  Page  
--- PAGE 47 ---

 48  |  Page  
--- PAGE 49 ---
 
Precondition  
PRE-01:  The  user  account  exists  in  the  database.  PRE-02:  The  account  status  is  'ACTIVE'.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  User  clicks  the  "Forgot  Password"  link  on  the  Login  Screen  (UC01).   
Post-Condition  
POS-01:  The  user's  password  is  successfully  updated  in  the  database.  POS-02:  The  user  is  redirected  to  the  Login  screen  (UC01).   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Enters  their  registered  Phone  Number  on  the  recovery  screen  and  taps  "Send  OTP".  
2  System  Validates  the  phone  number  (must  exist  and  be  ACTIVE)  and  generates  a  temporary  OTP  code.  
3  System  Sends  the  OTP  code  via  SMS  to  the  registered  number.  
4  User  Receives  the  OTP,  enters  the  code,  New  Password,  and  Confirm  Password  fields.  
5  System  Validates  the  OTP,  checks  if  the  New  Password  matches  the  Confirm  Password,  and  hashes  it.  
6  System  Updates  the  user's  ```  
password_hash  
```  in  the  database.  
7  System  Displays  a  success  message  and  redirects  the  user  to  the  Login  screen  (UC01).  
 
 49  |  Page  
--- PAGE 50 ---
 
 
Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  At  step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Phone  Number  is  not  found  in  the  database  or  the  account  status  is  'INACTIVE':  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "The  phone  number  entered  is  not  associated  with  an  active  account."  (Custom  MSG).  
2.2  System  Blocks  OTP  generation  and  remains  on  the  Identification  screen.  
AT2  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  entered  OTP  code  is  expired  or  invalid:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Invalid  or  expired  OTP.  Please  try  requesting  a  new  code."  (Custom  MSG).   
5.2  System  Blocks  the  password  reset  process.   
AT3  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  New  Password  and  Confirm  Password  do  not  match  or  fail  validation  (e.g.,  minimum  length):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  inline  error  messages  (e.g.,  "Passwords  do  not  match" )  under  the  password  fields.  
5.2  System  Stops  the  update  process  and  waits  for  the  user  to  correct  the  input.  
AT4  At  any  point  after  Step  1,  if  the  user  taps  "Resend  OTP"  because  the  previous  code  expired  (per  BR-08):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System  Invalidates  the  previously  issued  OTP,  generates  a  new  OTP,  and  resends  it  via  SMS  to  the  registered  phone  number.  
 50  |  Page  
--- PAGE 51 ---
 
1.2  System  Restarts  the  5-minute  OTP  validity  countdown  (BR-08)  and  displays  a  confirmation  that  a  new  code  has  been  sent.  
AT5  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  enters  an  incorrect  OTP  more  than  the  maximum  allowed  attempts  (e.g.,  3  times):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Invalidates  the  current  OTP  and  blocks  further  verification  attempts  for  that  code  (tracked  via  attempt_count).  
5.2  System  Displays  an  error  message  instructing  the  user  to  request  a  new  OTP  via  "Resend  OTP".  
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-05  OTP  Validation:  The  system  must  validate  that  the  account  status  is  'ACTIVE'  before  generating  and  sending  an  OTP  for  password  recovery  (BR-04).  
BR-06  Password  Hashing:  The  new  password  must  be  one-way  encrypted  using  the  bcrypt  algorithm  before  being  saved  to  the  database.  
BR-07  Session  Invalidation:  If  the  user  performs  a  password  reset  while  currently  logged  in  on  another  device,  the  system  must  automatically  invalidate  all  active  JWT  tokens  for  that  user,  forcing  a  re-login  after  the  password  change.  
BR-08  OTP  Expiration:  The  One-Time  Password  (OTP)  generated  by  the  system  must  be  valid  for  a  limited  time  (e.g.,  5  minutes)  to  ensure  security  and  prevent  replay  attacks.  
BR-09  Password  Complexity:  The  new  password  must  be  at  least  8  characters  long  and  contain  at  least  one  uppercase  letter  and  one  number.  
  
 51  |  Page  
--- PAGE 52 ---

3.2.4.2  Use  Case  Description  
 53  |  Page  
--- PAGE 54 ---
 
Use  Case  ID  UC04  Use  Case  Name  View  Personal  Profile   
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  
Version  1.0  Actor  All  Actors  (Resident,  Landlord,  Manager,  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician)    
Description  Displays  detailed  personal  information  of  the  logged-in  user,  including  identity  details,  contact  info,  and  roles.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  User  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  User  navigates  to  the  "Profile"  tab  or  screen.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  securely  retrieves  and  displays  the  user's  profile  details.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Navigates  to  the  "Profile"  tab   
2  System  Fetches  the  user  record  using  the  active  session  token.   
3  System  Renders  the  profile  page  showing  Full  Name,  Phone,  Avatar,  CCCD  (masked),  and  active  roles.   
4  System  Renders  the  user's  contact  group:  Email,  Emergency  Contact,  and  current  Apartment  Info.  
 54  |  Page  
--- PAGE 55 ---
 
5  System  Renders  role-specific  action  shortcuts  (e.g.,  "My  Roommates"  for  Residents)  along  with  Settings  and  Logout.  
 
 
Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  At  Step  2,  if  the  system  fails  to  fetch  the  user  record  (e.g.,  session  expired,  database  error):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  an  error  message  (e.g.,  "Failed  to  load  profile  data")  and  prompts  the  user  to  refresh  or  log  out.  
AT2  At  Step  3,  if  the  user  does  not  have  an  avatar  uploaded  (avatar_url is  null):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  a  default  placeholder  avatar  icon  instead  of  a  profile  picture.   
AT3  At  Step  5,  the  available  action  shortcuts  differ  by  role:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  For  a  Resident,  displays  the  "My  Roommates"  shortcut  (UC10)  alongside  Settings  and  Logout.  
5.2  System  For  Manager,  Landlord,  and  staff  roles  (Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician),  hides  the  "My  Roommates"  shortcut.  
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Data  Filtering:  The  profile  view  must  strictly  hide  sensitive  fields,  such  as  the  password_hash and  internal  id.  
 55  |  Page  
--- PAGE 56 ---
 
BR-02  Read-Only  Enforcement:  All  displayed  demographic  fields  (Full  Name,  Phone  Number,  Roles)  must  be  read-only  and  immutable  on  this  screen,  with  modifications  requiring  explicit  navigation  to  the  Update  Profile  screen  (UC05).  
BR-03  Data  Masking:  If  a  Citizen  ID  (CCCD)  number  is  displayed,  it  must  be  partially  masked  (e.g.,  only  the  last  4  digits  visible)  for  security  and  privacy.  
BR-04  Self-Service  Restriction:  The  system  must  use  the  user  ID  extracted  exclusively  from  the  active  JWT  token  to  fetch  the  profile  data,  ensuring  the  user  can  only  view  their  own  information.

3.2.5.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC05  Use  Case  Name  
Update  Personal  Profile   
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  
Version  1.0  Actor  All  Actors  (Resident,  Landlord,  Manager,  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician)  )   
 57  |  Page  
--- PAGE 58 ---
 
Description  Allows  users  to  view  and  update  their  personal  details,  including  their  full  name,  phone  number,  and  avatar  image.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  accesses  the  View  Personal  Profile  screen  (UC04).   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  user's  profile  details  (full\_name,  phone,  avatar\_url)  are  updated  in  the  database.  POS-02:  A  success  confirmation  is  displayed.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Navigates  to  the  Profile  screen.  
2  System  Displays  the  current  profile  data  loaded  from  the  users table.  
3  User  Modifies  one  or  more  editable  fields  (Full  Name,  Phone  Number,  and/or  Avatar).  
4  User  Clicks  the  "Save  Changes"  button.  
5  System  Validates  the  input  data  (mandatory  fields,  unique  phone  number).  
6  System  If  a  new  avatar  is  uploaded,  compresses  the  image  and  saves  the  URL  to  Cloudinary.  
 58  |  Page  
--- PAGE 59 ---
 
7  System  Updates  the  user  record  in  the  users table  with  the  new  data.  
8  System  Displays  a  success  toast  message  and  refreshes  the  profile  view.  
 
 
Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  leaves  a  mandatory  field  (Full  Name  or  Phone  Number)  empty:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error  message  (e.g.,  "Tr ườ ng  b ắ t  bu ộ c  không  đượ c  để  tr ố ng." )  under  the  empty  field.  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  update  process  and  waits  for  the  user  to  correct  the  input.  
AT2  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  entered  Phone  Number  already  exists  in  the  database  (violating  BR-02/BR-09):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Phone  number  already  exists.  Please  enter  a  different  number."   
5.2  System  Blocks  the  update  process  and  waits  for  the  user  to  enter  a  unique  phone  number.   
AT3  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  uploaded  Avatar  image  fails  the  client-side  compression  or  size  limit  (violating  BR-06  in  Appendix  5.1):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Image  size  exceeds  the  limit.  Please  choose  a  smaller  file."  (MSG09)  and  cancels  the  Cloudinary  upload.1 
 59  |  Page  
--- PAGE 60 ---
 
6.2  System  Stops  the  image  saving  process,  keeps  the  existing  avatar  (if  any),  and  allows  the  user  to  correct  other  profile  fields  (Full  Name,  Phone  Number).  
AT4  At  Step  4,  if  the  user  clicks  "Save  Changes"  without  modifying  any  field:  
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Detects  that  no  editable  field  was  changed  and  displays  the  message  "No  changes  to  save."  
4.2  System  Skips  the  update  API  call  and  keeps  the  user  on  the  Profile  screen.  
AT5  At  Step  5,  if  the  user  changed  their  Apartment  Number:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Verifies  the  selected  apartment  exists  and  is  not  already  OCCUPIED;  if  it  does  not  exist  or  is  occupied,  displays  an  error  and  blocks  the  change  (BR-13).  
5.2  System  If  valid,  records  the  apartment  change  as  PENDING  manual  verification  by  the  property  manager  (Manager)  and  notifies  the  user  that  approval  is  required  (BR-13).  
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-09  Phone  Uniqueness:  The  Phone  Number  used  by  the  user  must  remain  unique  across  the  entire  system,  even  when  performing  an  update.  
BR-10  Image  Compression:  Any  uploaded  avatar  image  must  be  compressed  at  the  client  side  (Mobile  Application)  to  a  maximum  defined  threshold  (e.g.,  <  500KB)  before  being  transmitted  to  the  Cloudinary  storage  (BR-06  in  Appendix  5.1).1 
 60  |  Page  
--- PAGE 61 ---
 
BR-11  User  Role  Access:  Only  users  with  the  role  RESIDENT  are  authorized  to  view  and  update  their  personal  profiles  through  this  functionality.  Manager  profiles  are  managed  separately  (implied  by  Screen  Authorization  table  3.1.3).1 
BR-12  Change  Auditing:  For  security  purposes,  any  modification  to  the  user's  phone number  must  be  logged,  as  this  field  serves  as  the  unique  login  username.  
 61  |  Page  
--- PAGE 62 ---

3.2.6.4  Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-19   No  Account  Creation:  Room  members  are  registered  as  demographic  data  only  in  the  Room  Member table.  The  system  must  not  create  a  corresponding  user  account  (User  entity)  or  provide  login  credentials.  
BR-10   Image  Compression:  Any  Citizen  ID  image  uploaded  must  be  compressed  at  the  client  side  (Mobile  Application)  to  a  maximum  defined  threshold  (e.g.,  <  500KB)  before  being  transmitted  to  the  Cloudinary  storage  (BR-06).  
BR-20   Data  Privacy:  If  the  apartment  owner  performs  a  Checkout  (UC18),  the  system  must  automatically  delete  the  roommate’s  Citizen  ID  image  from  Cloudinary  and  mask  the  identification  numbers  in  the  database  (BR-04).  
BR-21   Status  Flow:  The  initial  status  of  the  request  must  be  'PENDING'.  Only  the  manager  is  authorized  to  modify  the  status  to  either  'APPROVED'  or  'REJECTED'  via  the  manager  Module  (Screen  4.4).

3.2.7.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  
UC10  Use  Case  Name  
View  Roommate  List   
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Khu ấ t  Quang  H ư ng  Version  1.0  Actor  Resident   
Description  
Displays  a  comprehensive  list  of  all  registered  roommates  (sub-tenants)  currently  assigned  to  the  primary  resident's  apartment,  including  their  contact  details  and  manager  approval  statuses.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  Resident  has  successfully  logged  into  the  mobile  application.  
 
PRE-02:  Resident  is  officially  linked  to  an  active  apartment  in  the  database.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  Resident  navigates  to  the  "My  Roommates"  section  from  the  app's  main  menu  or  dashboard.   
 67  |  Page  
--- PAGE 68 ---
 
Post-Condition  
POS-01:  The  system  securely  retrieves  and  displays  the  list  of  roommates  without  modifying  any  database  records.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  
Resident  
Taps  the  "My  Roommates"  menu  item.   
2  System  
Extracts  the  user's  apartment_id from  the  active  session  token.   
3  
System  
Queries  the  database  for  all  resident  records  associated  with  that  specific  apartment_id.   
4  
System  
Calculates  the  total  count  of  occupants  to  populate  the  "Total  Residents  Summary"  card.   
5  
System  
Renders  the  vertical  list  of  Roommate  Cards,  dynamically  displaying  their  Name,  Phone  Number,  and  current  Status  (e.g.,  Pending,  Approved).   
6  
Resident  
Scrolls  through  the  list  to  review  the  current  household  members.   
7  
Resident  
(Optional)  Taps  the  "Invite  New  Roommate"  button  to  initiate  UC11.   
 
 3.2.7.3  Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  Empty  Roommate  List   
 68  |  Page  
--- PAGE 69 ---
 
At  step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  primary  resident  is  the  only  person  registered  in  the  apartment:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Queries  the  database  and  detects  no  additional  sub-tenants  linked  to  the  apartment_id.   
3.2  System  Updates  the  Total  Residents  Summary  to  display  "1  Roommate"  (the  primary  owner).   
3.3  System   Displays  a  user-friendly  empty  state  illustration  and  message  (e.g.,  "You  currently  have  no  roommates  registered.")  in  place  of  the  list.   
3.4  System   Keeps  the  "Invite  New  Roommate"  button  visible  for  future  registrations.   
 
3.2.7.4  Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-23   Data  Isolation  (Privacy):  The  system  must  strictly  enforce  data  isolation.  A  Resident  can  only  view  the  profiles  and  statuses  of  roommates  assigned  to  their  exact  apartment_id.  Cross-apartment  querying  is  strictly  prohibited.   
BR-21   Status  Transparency:  The  system  must  accurately  reflect  the  real-time  administrative  status  of  each  roommate.  Users  marked  as  PENDING  are  currently  awaiting  Manager  approval  and  may  have  restricted  building  access  until  their  status  is  updated  to  APPROVED.   
BR-24   Primary  Owner  Visibility:  The  primary  resident  (the  account  holder)  should  also  be  counted  in  the  "Total  Residents"  calculation  to  accurately  reflect  the  total  physical  occupancy  of  the  apartment.

--- PAGE 70 ---

3.2.8.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  
UC12  Use  Case  Name  Approve  Roommate  Request   
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Khu ấ t  Quang  H ư ng  Version  1.0  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  
Allows  the  Management  Board  to  review  pending  roommate  registrations  submitted  by  primary  residents,  verify  their  legal  identity  documents  (CCCD),  and  either  approve  or  reject  the  sub-tenant's  stay.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  Manager  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
 
PRE-02:  There  is  at  least  one  roommate  registration  record  in  the  database  with  the  status  'PENDING'.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  Manager  navigates  to  the  "Roommate  Requests"  or  "Approvals"  module  from  the  dashboard.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  roommate  record's  status  is  permanently  updated  to  'APPROVED'  or  'REJECTED'.  
 
POS-02:  A  push  notification  is  sent  to  the  primary  resident  confirming  the  decision.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager  
Navigates  to  the  "Roommate  Approval"  screen.   
2  System  
Fetches  the  list  of  pending  requests  and  renders  the  "Pending  Requests"  list  on  the  left  side  of  the  screen.   
 72  |  Page  
--- PAGE 73 ---
 
3  Manager  
Taps/clicks  on  a  specific  Request  Card  (e.g.,  "Alex  Johnson  -  Apt:  A-102").   
4  System  
Loads  the  "Identity  Verification"  panel,  displaying  the  applicant's  metadata  (Name,  Phone,  ID  Number)  and  fetching  the  secure  image  URLs  from  Cloudinary.   
5  Manager  
Inspects  the  text  information  and  visually  cross-checks  it  against  the  loaded  ID  Card  Front  and  Back  images.   
6  Manager  
Clicks  the  "Approve"  button  at  the  bottom  of  the  panel.   
7  System  
Updates  the  roommate's  record  status  from  'PENDING'  to  'APPROVED'  in  the  database.   
8  System  
Triggers  Firebase  FCM  to  send  a  success  notification  to  the  primary  resident  who  submitted  the  request.   
9  System  
Removes  the  approved  card  from  the  "Pending  Requests"  list  and  auto-selects  the  next  pending  item.   
 
 
3.2.8.3  Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  AT1:  Manager  Rejects  the  Request  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Manager  detects  mismatched  information,  blurry  images,  or  invalid  ID  documents:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  Manager  Clicks  the  "Reject"  button.  
6.2  System  
(Optional)  Displays  a  small  modal  asking  the  Manager  to  select  or  type  a  rejection  reason  (e.g.,  "Blurry  ID  images",  "Information  mismatch").  
6.3  Manager  Submits  the  rejection  reason.  
 73  |  Page  
--- PAGE 74 ---
 
6.4  System  Updates  the  record  status  to  'REJECTED'  in  the  database.  
6.5  System  
Sends  a  Firebase  FCM  notification  to  the  primary  resident  explaining  the  rejection  reason  and  advising  them  to  re-submit.  
6.6  System  Removes  the  rejected  card  from  the  pending  list.   
3.2.8.4  Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-21   Strict  Sequential  Workflow:  The  system  must  enforce  that  a  sub-tenant  (roommate)  cannot  gain  access  to  digital  building  amenities  or  receive  gate  pass  permissions  until  their  account  transitions  completely  from  the  PENDING state  to  the  APPROVED state.   
BR-22   Sensitive  Data  Handling:  The  ID  Card  images  displayed  in  the  verification  panel  are  classified  as  highly  sensitive  PII  (Personally  Identifiable  Information).  The  system  must  load  these  images  via  secure,  expiring  HTTPS  URLs  and  prevent  caching  on  the  Manager's  local  device  to  comply  with  data  protection  standards.   
BR-11   Audit  Logging:  Every  approval  or  rejection  action  must  be  logged  in  the  system's  audit  trail,  recording  the  manager_id,  the  timestamp,  and  the  action_taken to  ensure  operational  accountability.

Use  Case  ID  UC06  Use  Case  Name  View  Contract  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Resident,  Manager,  Security  Guard,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  user  to  view  their  legal  tenancy  timeline,  including  the  apartment  information,  lease  start/end  dates,  base  rent,  and  the  dynamically  calculated  remaining  days  of  the  staying  contract.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  PRE-02:  The  user's  account  is  associated  with  an  apartment  that  has  an  existing  contract  record  in  the  database.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  navigates  to  the  Contract  Details  screen  from  the  application  menu  or  dashboard.  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  retrieves  and  displays  the  apartment  information,  contract  details,  and  remaining  duration  on  the  screen.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Navigates  to  the  Contract  Details  screen  from  the  app  menu  or  dashboard.   
2  System  Retrieves  the  user's  apartment  data  and  the  associated  contract  data  from  the  database.   
3  System  Calculates  the  remaining  days  by  subtracting  the  current  system  date  from  the  contract  end  date.   
4  System  Renders  the  screen  with  three  sections:  Apartment  Info,  Contract  Details  (with  status  badge),  and  Remaining  Duration  (with  progress  bar  and  remaining  days  count).   
5  User  Views  the  contract  information  on  the  screen.   
 
 
 
Alternative  flows  
 77  |  Page  
--- PAGE 78 ---
 
 
 
AT1  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  contract  status  is  EXPIRED:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  the  Apartment  Info  and  Contract  Details  sections  with  the  Contract  Status  badge  showing  "EXPIRED"  (gray).  
2.2  System  Hides  the  "REMAINING  DURATION"  section  entirely,  as  remaining  days  are  not  applicable  for  expired  contracts.  
2.3  System  Hides  the  "[  REQUEST  STAY  EXTENSION  ]"  button,  as  extensions  cannot  be  requested  for  expired  contracts.  
AT2  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  no  contract  record  is  found  for  the  user's  apartment:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  the  Apartment  Info  section  with  available  apartment  data.  
2.2  System  Displays  a  message  in  the  Contract  Details  area:  "No  active  contract  found  for  this  apartment."  
2.3  System  Hides  the  Remaining  Duration  section  and  the  "[  REQUEST  STAY  EXTENSION  ]"  button.  
AT3  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  taps  "[  REQUEST  STAY  EXTENSION  ]":  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  User  Taps  the  "[  REQUEST  STAY  EXTENSION  ]"  button.  
5.2  System  Navigates  to  the  Request  Stay  Extension  screen   ,  passing  the  current  contract's  id  and  end_date  as  parameters.  
  78  |  Page  
--- PAGE 79 ---
 
Business  rule  
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-12  Active  Lease  Requirement:  The  system  shall  only  calculate  and  display  stay  duration  (remaining  days  and  progress  bar)  for  contracts  with  an  ACTIVE  status  in  the  database.  Expired  contracts  must  not  show  remaining  duration.  
BR-13  Dynamic  Stay  Calculation:  Remaining  days  of  tenancy  must  be  calculated  dynamically  on  each  page  load  by  subtracting  the  current  system  date  from  contracts.end_date.  This  value  is  never  stored;  it  is  always  computed  in  real-time.  
BR-23  Data  Isolation:  A  Resident  can  only  view  the  contract  details  associated  with  their  exact  apartment_id.  Cross-apartment  querying  is  strictly  prohibited  by  the  system's  access  control  layer.  
BR-09  Profile  Edit  Access:  Only  users  with  the  role  RESIDENT  can  see  the  "[  REQUEST  STAY  EXTENSION  ]"  button.  Manager,  Security  Guard,  and  Landlord  can  view  contract  details  but  cannot  submit  extension  requests  from  this  screen.

Use  case  description  
 
 
Use  Case  ID  UC07  Use  Case  Name  Request  Stay  Extension  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Resident  
Description  Enables  residents  to  submit  an  official  request  to  extend  their  staying  period  by  specifying  the  desired  new  end  date  and  providing  a  mandatory  reason.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  RESIDENT  role.  PRE-02:  The  resident  has  an  apartment  with  an  ACTIVE  contract.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  resident  taps  the  "[  REQUEST  STAY  EXTENSION  ]"  button  on  the  Contract  Details  screen  (UC#6).  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  stay  extension  request  is  created  with  status  PENDING.  POS-02:  The  resident  is  redirected  back  to  the  Contract  Details  screen.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Pre-fills  the  "Current  Contract"  section  with  the  resident's  apartment  info  and  current  contract  end  date.  
2  Resident  Selects  a  new  desired  end  date  using  the  date  picker.  
3  System  Validates  that  the  selected  date  is  strictly  after  the  current  contract  end  date.  Calculates  and  displays  the  Extension  Period  in  days.  
4  Resident  Enters  a  reason  for  the  extension  request  in  the  text  area.  
 82  |  Page  
--- PAGE 83 ---
 
5  Resident  Taps  the  "[  SUBMIT  REQUEST  ]"  button.  
6  System  Validates  all  mandatory  fields  and  creates  the  stay  extension  request  with  status  PENDING.  
7  System  Displays  a  success  message  and  navigates  the  resident  back  to  the  Contract  Details  screen  (UC#6).   
Alternative  flows  
 
 
AT1  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  date  is  before  or  equal  to  the  current  contract  end  date:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Highlights  the  date  picker  input  with  a  red  border  and  displays  the  inline  error:  "(!)  New  date  must  be  after  the  current  contract  end  date."  
3.2  System  Keeps  the  Extension  Period  showing  "—  days"  and  blocks  form  submission.  
AT2  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Reason  field  is  empty  or  contains  fewer  than  10  characters:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Highlights  the  Reason  text  area  with  a  red  border  and  displays  the  inline  error:  "(!)  Reason  must  be  at  least  10  characters."  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  form  submission  process  until  the  validation  is  satisfied.  
AT3  At  Step  2,  4,  or  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  resident  taps  "[  CANCEL  ]":  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
X.1  Resident  Taps  the  "[  CANCEL  ]"  button.  
 83  |  Page  
--- PAGE 84 ---
 
X.2  System  Discards  all  entered  data  without  saving  and  navigates  back  to  the  Contract  Details  screen  (UC#6).  
AT4  At  Step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  network  error  or  server  error  occurs:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  error  message:  "An  error  occurred.  Please  try  again  later."  (MSG10).  
6.2  System  Remains  on  the  form  screen  with  all  entered  data  preserved,  allowing  the  resident  to  retry.  
 
Business  Rules  
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-14  Extension  Date  Validation:  The  proposed  new  end  date  in  a  stay  extension  request  must  be  in  the  future  and  strictly  after  the  current  lease's  end  date.  The  system  must  reject  any  date  that  is  before  or  equal  to  the  current  contract  end  date.  
BR-15  Extension  Request  Reason  Constraint:  The  "Reason"  input  for  requesting  a  stay  extension  must  be  strictly  mandatory,  containing  a  minimum  of  10  characters  and  a  maximum  of  500  characters.  
BR-12  Active  Lease  Requirement:  Stay  extension  requests  can  only  be  submitted  for  contracts  with  an  ACTIVE  status.  The  "[  REQUEST  STAY  EXTENSION  ]"  button  is  only  accessible  when  the  contract  is  ACTIVE.  
BR-23  Data  Isolation:  The  system  must  only  display  and  allow  extension  requests  for  the  resident's  own  apartment.  Cross-apartment  requests  are  strictly  prohibited.  
 
3.2.11  FID-11  -  View  Stay  Extension  Request    /  UC08  View  Stay  Extension  Request

Use  Case  ID  UC08  Use  Case  Name  View  Stay  Extension  Requests  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  authorized  management  staff  to  view  a  list  of  all  stay  extension  requests  submitted  by  residents,  filter  them  by  status,  and  select  individual  requests  for  review.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  navigates  to  the  Extension  Requests  list  screen  from  the  dashboard  or  bottom  navigation  menu.  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  retrieves  and  displays  the  list  of  stay  extension  requests.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Navigates  to  the  Extension  Requests  list  screen.  
2  System  Retrieves  all  stay  extension  requests  from  the  database,  including  the  associated  resident  and  apartment  details.  
3  System  Renders  the  screen  with  a  list  of  request  cards,  sorted  by  submission  date  (newest  first)  by  default.  
4  User  Selects  a  specific  filter  tab  (e.g.,  Pending,  Approved,  Rejected)  to  narrow  down  the  list.  
5  System  Updates  the  displayed  list  to  show  only  requests  matching  the  selected  status.  
6  User  Taps  on  a  specific  request  card.  
7  System  Navigates  to  the  Approve/Reject  Extension  Request  screen  (UC#9)  for  the  selected  request.   
 
Alternative  flows  
 
 87  |  Page  
--- PAGE 88 ---
 
AT1  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  there  are  no  stay  extension  requests  in  the  system:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  an  empty  state  message:  "No  extension  requests  found."  
2.2  System  Hides  the  list  of  request  cards.  
AT2  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  there  are  no  requests  matching  the  selected  filter  status:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  an  empty  state  message  corresponding  to  the  filter:  "No  [Status]  requests  found."   
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-16  Extension  Request  Access  Control:  Only  users  with  the  role  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  are  allowed  to  view,  approve,  or  reject  stay  extension  requests.  Residents  cannot  access  this  administrative  list  view.
