Use  Case  ID  UC09  Use  Case  Name  
Approve/Reject  Stay  Extension  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  authorized  management  staff  to  review  a  resident's  stay  extension  request,  evaluate  the  provided  reason,  and  decide  whether  to  approve  (extending  the  contract)  or  reject  it  (with  a  mandatory  reason).  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role.  PRE-02:  The  selected  extension  request  is  currently  in  the  PENDING  status.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  taps  on  a  specific  PENDING  request  card  from  the  Extension  Requests  list  screen  (UC#8).  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  request  status  is  updated  to  APPROVED  or  REJECTED.  POS-02:  If  approved,  the  associated  contract's  end  date  is  updated.  POS-03:  A  push  notification  is  sent  to  the  resident.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Retrieves  and  displays  the  full  details  of  the  extension  request,  including  Resident  Info,  Contract  Info,  and  Extension  Request  details.  
2  User  Reviews  the  current  contract  status,  the  requested  new  end  date,  and  the  resident's  reason.  
3  User  Taps  the  "[  APPROVE  ]"  button  to  grant  the  extension.  
 91  |  Page  
--- PAGE 92 ---
 
4  System  Initiates  a  database  transaction  to  process  the  approval.  
5  System  Updates  the  extension  request  record:  sets  status  to  APPROVED,  records  the  current  user  as  the  reviewer,  and  sets  the  review  timestamp.  
6  System  Updates  the  associated  contract  record:  changes  the  end  date  to  match  the  requested  end  date  within  the  same  transaction.  
7  System  Commits  the  transaction  and  triggers  a  push  notification  to  the  resident  informing  them  of  the  approval.  
8  System  Displays  a  success  message  and  navigates  the  user  back  to  the  Extension  Requests  list  screen.  
  Alternative  flow  
 
 
AT1  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  decides  to  REJECT  the  request:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  User  Enters  a  rejection  reason  in  the  "Reject  Reason"  text  area.  
3.2  User  Taps  the  "[  REJECT  ]"  button.  
3.3  System  Validates  that  the  "Reject  Reason"  field  is  not  empty.  
3.4  System  Updates  the  extension  request  record:  sets  status  to  REJECTED,  records  the  rejection  reason,  the  reviewer,  and  the  review  timestamp.  
 92  |  Page  
--- PAGE 93 ---
 
3.5  System  Triggers  a  push  notification  to  the  resident  informing  them  of  the  rejection  and  the  reason  provided.  
3.6  System  Displays  a  success  message  and  navigates  the  user  back  to  the  Extension  Requests  list  screen.  
AT2  At  Sub-step  3.3  of  AT1,  if  the  "Reject  Reason"  field  is  left  empty  when  tapping  "[  REJECT  ]":  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.3.1  System  Highlights  the  "Reject  Reason"  text  area  with  a  red  border  and  displays  the  inline  error:  "(!)  A  rejection  reason  is  required."  
3.3.2  System  Blocks  the  rejection  process  and  waits  for  the  user  to  provide  a  reason.  
AT3  At  Step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  associated  contract  is  no  longer  ACTIVE  (e.g.,  terminated  manually  by  another  Manager):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Cancels  the  transaction  and  displays  an  error  message:  "Cannot  approve  request.  The  associated  contract  is  no  longer  active."  
4.2  System  Remains  on  the  detail  screen,  allowing  the  user  to  reject  the  request  or  navigate  back.  
 
Business  Rules  
  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-16  Extension  Request  Access  Control:  Only  users  with  the  role  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  are  allowed  to  view,  approve,  or  reject  stay  extension  requests.  
 93  |  Page  
--- PAGE 94 ---
 
BR-17  ACID  Transaction  on  Extension  Approval:  The  stay  extension  approval  process  must  execute  within  a  single  database  transaction  block.  If  updating  either  stay_extensions.status  to  APPROVED  or  contracts.end_date  to  proposed_end_date  fails,  the  entire  transaction  must  rollback.  
BR-18  FCM  Notification  Requirement:  Upon  successful  approval  or  rejection  of  a  stay  extension  request,  the  system  must  immediately  dispatch  a  Firebase  FCM  push  notification  to  the  resident's  registered  device  token.

Use  Case  ID  UC13  Use  Case  Name  Input  Monthly  Bills  Date  03/06/2026  
Author  Khu ấ t  Quang  H ư ng  Version  2.0  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
--- PAGE 97 ---
 
3.3.1.2  Use  Case  Description Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager  Navigates  to  the  "Input  Monthly  Bills"  screen.  
2  System  Displays  the  utility  index  input  form.  
3  Manager  Selects  the  target  apartment  code  (e.g.,  Room  101).  
4  System  Queries  and  displays  the  previous  index  readings  (prev_electricity_index,  prev_water_index)  from  the  database.  
5  Manager  Inputs  the  current  electricity  index  (curr_electricity_index)  and  current  water  index  (curr_water_index)  read  from  the  physical  meters.  
6  Manager  Inputs  an  optional  ad-hoc  extra  fee  (extraFee)  and  description  (extraFeeDescription)  if  any  additional  charges  apply  for  the  month.  
7  Manager  Clicks  the  "ISSUE  BILL"  button.  
 97  |  Page  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  to  input  current  electricity  and  water  indices,  along  with  optional  ad-hoc  extra  fees,  directly  on  their  mobile  device.  The  system  retrieves  previous  index  readings  and  fixed  fees  from  the  active  lease  contract  to  compute  the  final  bill  amount  and  instantly  issue  the  monthly  invoice.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  Manager  has  successfully  logged  into  the  app.  
PRE-02:  There  is  an  active  lease  contract  (contracts)  for  the  selected  apartment  containing  the  base  rent,  management  fees,  and  initial  meter  indices.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  Manager  accesses  the  "Input  Monthly  Bills"  screen,  selects  an  apartment,  inputs  the  meter  readings  and  optional  fees,  and  clicks  "ISSUE  BILL".  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  invoice  record  is  generated  in  the  invoices  table  with  a  status  of  'UNPAID'.  
POS-02:  A  real-time  push  notification  (via  Firebase  Cloud  Messaging)  is  successfully  sent  to  the  resident's  mobile  device.  
--- PAGE 98 ---
 
8  System  Validates  the  entered  indices  to  ensure  that  the  current  index  is  greater  than  or  equal  to  the  previous  index.  
9  System  Calculates  the  total  invoice  amount:  Total  =  Room  Rent  +  Management  Fee  +  (Electricity  Consumption  *  Price)  +  (Water  Consumption  *  Price)  +  Extra  Fee.  
10  System  Saves  the  new  invoice  record  in  the  database  invoices  table  with  the  status  'UNPAID'.  
11  System  Invokes  Firebase  FCM  API  to  send  a  "New  Bill  Issued"  push  notification  to  the  resident.  
12  System  Clears  the  input  fields,  displays  a  success  toast  message  (MSG03),  and  updates  the  UI  status.  
3.3.1.3  Alternative  flows  
 
AT1:  Index  Validation  Fails  (New  Index  <  Previous  Index)  -  At  step  8  of  the  main  flow:  
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
8.1  System  System  detects  that  the  entered  current  index  is  lower  than  the  previous  month's  final  index  (curr  <  prev).  
8.2  System  System  blocks  invoice  generation  and  displays  an  error  message  on  the  screen:  "New  index  cannot  be  less  than  the  previous  month's  index  (Previous:  XXX)".  
8.3  Manager  Manager  verifies  the  meter  reading,  inputs  the  corrected  value,  and  clicks  "ISSUE  BILL"  again  (returns  to  step  7).  
AT2:  Bill  for  Current  Month  Already  Exists  -  At  step  8  of  the  main  flow:  
 
 98  |  Page  
--- PAGE 99 ---
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
8.1  System  System  detects  that  an  invoice  has  already  been  generated  for  the  selected  apartment  for  the  current  billing  cycle.  
8.2  System  System  rejects  the  creation  request  and  displays  an  error:  "An  invoice  has  already  been  generated  for  this  room  in  [Month/Year]".  
8.3  System  System  redirects  the  Manager  back  to  the  apartment  selection  list.  
 
3.3.1.4  Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-25   (Index  Validation):  The  input  current  electricity  and  water  indices  must  be  non-negative  real  numbers  and  must  be  greater  than  or  equal  to  their  respective  previous  month's  indices  (curr  >=  prev).  
BR-26   Positive  Consumption  Logic:  The  calculated  consumption  (New  Index  -  Old  Index)  for  both  water  and  electricity  must  be  strictly  ≥  0.  The  system  must  reject  any  row  violating  this  mathematical  rule.  
BR-27   Duplication  Prevention:  The  system  enforces  a  unique  composite  constraint  on  apartment_id  and  month_year.  It  must  reject  the  creation  of  a  bill  if  an  invoice  for  that  exact  month  and  apartment  already  exists  in  the  database.  
BR-28   ACID  Transaction:  The  bulk  insert  process  must  run  within  a  database  transaction.  If  one  row  fails  critical  validation  during  the  insert  phase,  the  entire  batch  must  roll  back  to  prevent  partial  billing  states.

3.3.2.2  Use  Case  Description  
 
Use  Case  ID  UC14  Use  Case  Name  View  Transaction  History   
Author  Khu ấ t  Quang  H ư ng  Version  2.0   
Date  01/06/2026  Actor  Manager  
Description  Provides  a  straightforward,  read-only  data  table  displaying  all  building-wide  resident  payment  transactions,  allowing  the  Manager  to  easily  track  payment  statuses  using  basic  filters.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  Manager  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  There  are  existing  payment  transaction  records  in  the  database  (generated  via  PayOS  webhooks).  
Trigger  TRG-01:  Manager  navigates  to  the  "Transaction  History"  module  from  the  main  menu.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  accurately  displays  the  paginated  list  of  transactions  without  altering  any  database  records.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager  Navigates  to  the  "Transaction  History"  screen.   
2  System  Queries  the  database  for  all  payment  transactions,  sorted  by  the  most  recent  date.   
3  System   Renders  the  data  table  showing  Apartment  ID,  Payment  Date,  and  Status,  along  with  the  pagination  controls.   
4  Manager  Selects  a  specific  month  (e.g.,  "June  2026")  from  the  "Select  Month"  dropdown.   
 102  |  Page  
--- PAGE 103 ---
 
5  Manager  Selects  a  specific  state  (e.g.,  "Success")  from  the  "Status"  dropdown.   
6  System   Dynamically  queries  the  database  applying  the  selected  filters.   
7  System   Refreshes  the  data  table  to  display  only  the  transactions  matching  the  selected  month  and  status.   
3.3.2.3  Alternative  flows  
AT1  No  Records  Found  (Empty  State)   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Executes  the  query  based  on  Manager’s  selected  filters  (Month/Status).   
6.2   System   Detects  that  0  records  match  the  criteria.   
6.3  System   Displays  a  user-friendly  empty  state  message  inside  the  table  area  (e.g.,  "No  transactions  found  for  the  selected  criteria.")  instead  of  data  rows.   
6.4  System   Clears  or  changes  the  dropdown  filters  to  view  other  data.   
 
3.3.2.4  Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-29   Read-Only  Enforcement:  The  Transaction  History  module  is  strictly  read-only.  Managers  cannot  manually  add,  edit,  or  delete  any  transaction  records  from  this  interface  to  ensure  absolute  financial  transparency  and  auditability.   
BR-30   Default  Sorting :  Upon  initial  page  load  (without  filters),  the  system  must  query  and  display  the  records  in  descending  order  based  on  the  Payment  Date  (newest  transactions  appear  at  the  top).   
 103  |  Page  
--- PAGE 104 ---
 
BR-30  Pagination  Limit :  To  optimize  database  querying  and  UI  rendering  performance,  the  system  must  limit  the  number  of  records  fetched  per  page  (e.g.,  10  rows  per  page  as  depicted  in  the  UI),  requiring  the  use  of  pagination  controls  to  view  older  data.

 105  |  Page

3.3.3.4  Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-31   Data  Isolation:  The  backend  API  must  query  the  bills  table  using  the  user_id  extracted  from  the  JWT  token,  NEVER  by  the  apartment_id.  This  ensures  the  current  resident  cannot  view  the  financial  history  of  previous  tenants.

3.3.4.3  Alternative  flows

 3.3.4.4Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-33   Live  Transaction  Precaution:  Because  the  system  processes  real  financial  transactions  via  VietQR,  all  testing  and  demonstration  scenarios  must  strictly  use  minimal  denominations  (e.g.,  under  50,000  VND)  to  ensure  financial  safety.  
 109  |  Page  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  The  Flutter  App  calls  the  backend  API  (/payments/payos/create-url)  to  sign  the  payload  (Checksum)  and  generate  a  secure  PayOS  payment  link.  
2  System  Opens  an  in-app  WebView  rendering  the  PayOS  Checkout  screen  with  a  dynamic  VietQR  code.  
3  Resident  Uses  another  banking  application  to  scan  the  QR  code  OR  uses  the  "Copy"  buttons  to  manually  enter  the  transfer  details  (Account  Number,  Amount,  Message).  
4  Resident  Completes  the  bank  transfer  on  their  personal  banking  app.  
5  PayOS  (Background)  Detects  the  successful  transaction  and  redirects  the  WebView  to  the  Success  deep  link.  
--- PAGE 110 ---
 
BR-32   Status  Update  Restriction:  The  mobile  application  must  not  directly  update  the  database  bill  status  to  'PAID'  after  this  screen.  Status  updates  are  strictly  reserved  for  the  server-to-server  Webhook  to  prevent  frontend  manipulation  or  spoofing.

Description  Displays  a  comprehensive  digital  e-receipt  to  the  Resident  immediately  following  a  successful  PayOS  transaction,  or  when  viewing  historical  paid  invoices.

Main  flows

 112  |  Page

3.3.5.4  Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-34   Immutability:  The  data  displayed  on  the  e-receipt  (Total  Amount,  Timestamp,  Reference  ID)  must  be  strictly  read-only  and  immutable,  reflecting  the  exact  payload  confirmed  by  the  payment  gateway  webhook.   
BR-35   Session  Security :  If  the  receipt  is  accessed  via  a  URL  or  deep  link,  the  system  must  verify  that  the  requesting  user's  owner_id  or  resident_id  matches  the  apartment  associated  with  the  invoice  to  prevent  unauthorized  access  to  financial  record s.

Usecase  description  
 
 
Use  Case  ID  UC18  Use  Case  Name  View  Ticket  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  review  the  list  of  incident  tickets  reported  by  residents,  filter  them  by  status,  and  select  individual  tickets  to  view  their  full  details  and  priority.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role.  
 116  |  Page  
--- PAGE 117 ---
 
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  navigates  to  the  Ticket  Management  screen  from  the  dashboard  or  bottom  navigation  menu.  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  retrieves  and  displays  the  list  of  repair  tickets  sorted  by  creation  date  (newest  first).  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Navigates  to  the  Ticket  Management  screen.  
2  System  Retrieves  all  repair  tickets  from  the  database,  including  the  associated  resident  and  apartment  details.  
3  System  Renders  the  screen  with  a  list  of  ticket  cards,  sorted  by  creation  date  (newest  first).  Each  card  displays  the  ticket  ID,  category,  title,  apartment,  reporter  name,  status  badge,  and  created  date.  
4  User  Optionally  selects  a  filter  tab  (e.g.,  Pending,  Assigned,  Processing,  Resolved)  to  narrow  down  the  list.  
5  System  Updates  the  displayed  list  to  show  only  tickets  matching  the  selected  status.  
6  User  Taps  on  a  specific  ticket  card.  
7  System  Navigates  to  the  Ticket  Detail  screen  (UC#20)  for  the  selected  ticket.   
Alternative  flow  
 
AT1  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  there  are  no  repair  tickets  in  the  system:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action

2.2  System  Hides  the  ticket  card  list  area.

Sub  step  Actor  Action  
 117  |  Page  
--- PAGE 118 ---
 
5.1  System  Displays  a  filtered  empty  state  message:  "No  [Status]  tickets  found."  
AT3  At  Step  1  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  taps  the  search  icon:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  User  Taps  the  search  icon  on  the  header.  
1.2  System  Expands  a  search  bar  below  the  header.  
1.3  User  Enters  a  keyword  (ticket  ID,  resident  name,  or  apartment  number).  
1.4  System  Filters  and  displays  only  matching  ticket  cards  in  real-time.  
 
Business  rule  
 
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-39  Role-Based  Ticket  Filtering:  The  database  query  must  restrict  ticket  results  based  on  roles.  Managers  and  Landlords  have  read  access  to  all  tickets  across  all  apartments.  
BR-40  Ticket  Assignment  Constraints:  Only  users  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role  are  authorized  to  assign  or  reassign  repair  tickets.  A  ticket  can  only  be  assigned  if  its  current  status  is  PENDING.  
BR-18  FCM  Notification  Requirement:  Upon  successful  status  updates  or  task  assignments,  the  system  must  dispatch  a  push  notification  to  the  relevant  resident  or  staff  member.

Usecase  description  
 
Use  Case  ID  UC19  Use  Case  Name  Create  Ticket  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Resident  
Description  Allows  residents  to  submit  a  new  issue  ticket  by  selecting  a  category,  providing  a  detailed  description,  and  optionally  attaching  photos  of  the  problem.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  RESIDENT  role.  PRE-02:  The  resident  is  associated  with  an  OCCUPIED  apartment.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  resident  taps  the  "Report  Issue"  button  from  the  Resident  Home  screen  or  Ticket  List.  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  repair  ticket  is  created  with  status  PENDING.  POS-02:  The  resident  is  redirected  back  to  the  Ticket  List  screen.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Pre-fills  the  "Apartment  Info"  section  with  the  resident's  apartment  unit  number  and  floor.  
 121  |  Page  
--- PAGE 122 ---
 
2  Resident  Selects  a  category  from  the  dropdown  or  chip  buttons  (Electricity,  Water,  Furniture,  or  Other).  
3  Resident  Enters  a  detailed  description  of  the  issue  in  the  text  area.  
4  Resident  Optionally  attaches  up  to  3  photos  by  tapping  the  "+"  placeholder.  Photos  are  compressed  on  the  client  side  before  upload.  
5  Resident  Taps  the  "[  SUBMIT  TICKET  ]"  button.  
6  System  Validates  all  mandatory  fields,  uploads  attached  images  to  cloud  storage,  creates  a  new  repair  ticket  with  status  PENDING,  and  displays  a  success  message.   
Alternative  flow  
 
AT1  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Category  field  is  not  selected:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Highlights  the  Category  selector  with  a  red  border  and  displays  the  inline  error:  "(!)  Please  select  a  category."  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  form  submission  process.  
AT2  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Description  field  is  empty  or  contains  fewer  than  10  characters:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Highlights  the  Description  text  area  with  a  red  border  and  displays  the  inline  error:  "(!)  Description  must  be  at  least  10  characters."  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  form  submission  process  until  the  validation  is  satisfied.  
AT3  At  Step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  resident  attempts  to  attach  a  photo  that  exceeds  5MB  or  is  not  JPG/PNG:  
 122  |  Page  
--- PAGE 123 ---
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  error  message:  "Image  size  exceeds  the  limit.  Please  choose  a  smaller  file."  (MSG09).  
4.2  System  Rejects  the  file  and  does  not  add  it  to  the  upload  queue.  
AT4  At  any  step,  if  the  resident  taps  "[  CANCEL  ]":  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
X.1  Resident  Taps  the  "[  CANCEL  ]"  button.  
X.2  System  Discards  all  entered  data  without  saving  and  navigates  back  to  the  previous  screen.  
 
Business  rule  
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-36  Mandatory  Report  Fields:  A  repair  ticket  cannot  be  submitted  without  a  selected  category  and  description  text  (minimum  10  characters  and  maximum  500  characters).  
BR-37  Report  Image  Limits:  The  Resident  can  attach  at  most  3  images  showing  the  issue  before  repair.  Each  image  must  be  in  JPG/PNG  format  and  must  not  exceed  5MB.  
BR-10  Resource  Optimization:  All  media  files  captured  or  selected  by  users  must  be  compressed  on  the  client  side  to  a  maximum  defined  threshold  before  being  transmitted  to  cloud  storage.  
 123  |  Page  
--- PAGE 124 ---
 
BR-39  Role-Based  Ticket  Filtering:  The  created  ticket  is  automatically  associated  with  the  resident's  apartment.  Only  the  resident  and  authorized  management  staff  can  view  this  ticket.

Usecase  description  
 
Use  Case  ID  UC20  Use  Case  Name  Update  Ticket  Status  
Date  01/06/2026  
 126  |  Page  
--- PAGE 127 ---
 
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  review  the  full  details  of  a  repair  ticket,  update  its  current  status,  and  add  internal  notes  or  feedback  for  the  resident.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role.  PRE-02:  The  selected  ticket  exists  in  the  database.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  taps  on  a  specific  ticket  card  from  the  Ticket  Management  list  screen  (UC#18).  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  ticket  status  is  updated  in  the  database.  POS-02:  A  push  notification  is  sent  to  the  resident  informing  them  of  the  status  change.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Retrieves  and  displays  the  full  details  of  the  ticket,  including  Ticket  Info,  Reported  By,  Issue  Description,  and  Attached  Photos.  
2  User  Reviews  the  ticket  details,  the  issue  description,  and  attached  photos.  
3  User  Selects  a  new  status  from  the  "Update  Status"  dropdown.  
4  User  Optionally  enters  internal  notes  or  feedback  in  the  text  area.  
5  User  Taps  the  "[  SAVE  CHANGES  ]"  button.  
6  System  Validates  the  status  transition,  updates  the  ticket  record  in  the  database,  records  the  update  timestamp,  and  triggers  a  push  notification  to  the  resident.   
Alternative  flow  
 
AT1  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  selects  an  invalid  status  transition  (e.g.,  PENDING  →  RESOLVED  directly):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
 127  |  Page  
--- PAGE 128 ---
 
3.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Invalid  status  transition.  Please  follow  the  allowed  workflow."  
3.2  System  Resets  the  dropdown  to  the  current  status  and  blocks  the  save  action.  
AT2  At  Step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  network  error  or  server  error  occurs:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  error  message:  "An  error  occurred.  Please  try  again  later."  (MSG10).  
6.2  System  Remains  on  the  detail  screen  with  all  entered  data  preserved.  
AT3  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  selects  status  CANCELLED:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  a  confirmation  dialog:  "Are  you  sure  you  want  to  cancel  this  ticket?  This  action  cannot  be  undone."  
3.2  User  Taps  "Confirm"  to  proceed  or  "Cancel"  to  go  back.  
3.3  System  If  confirmed,  updates  the  ticket  status  to  CANCELLED  and  purges  associated  images  from  cloud  storage.  
AT4  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  has  not  changed  the  status  and  has  not  entered  any  notes:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
 128  |  Page  
--- PAGE 129 ---
 
5.1  System  Displays  a  message:  "No  changes  to  save."  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  save  action.  
 
Business  rule  
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-40  Ticket  Status  Workflow:  The  system  must  enforce  a  valid  status  transition  flow:  PENDING  →  ASSIGNED  →  PROCESSING  →  RESOLVED.  Direct  skipping  of  intermediate  statuses  is  not  allowed.  Tickets  can  be  CANCELLED  from  PENDING  status  only.  
BR-38  Image  Purge  on  Ticket  Cancellation:  If  a  ticket  is  cancelled,  all  associated  image  URLs  must  be  purged  from  cloud  storage  to  comply  with  data  privacy  standards.  
BR-39  Role-Based  Access:  Only  users  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role  are  authorized  to  update  ticket  statuses  and  add  internal  notes.  Residents  have  read-only  access  to  their  own  ticket  details.  
BR-18  FCM  Notification  Requirement:  Upon  successful  status  update,  the  system  must  dispatch  a  push  notification  to  the  resident  informing  them  of  the  change.

Usecase  description  
 
 
Use  Case  ID  UC21  Use  Case  Name  Assign  Task  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  assign  a  pending  repair  ticket  to  a  specific  security  guard  or  technician  by  creating  a  task  with  a  title,  description,  and  selecting  a  staff  member  based  on  their  current  workload.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role.  PRE-02:  The  selected  ticket  has  a  current  status  of  PENDING.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  navigates  to  the  Assign  Task  screen  from  the  Ticket  Detail  screen  (UC#20)  or  directly  from  the  Ticket  Management  list  (UC#18).  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  task  record  is  created  with  status  ASSIGNED.  POS-02:  The  associated  ticket  status  is  updated  to  ASSIGNED.  POS-03:  A  push  notification  is  sent  to  the  assigned  staff  member.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
 132  |  Page  
--- PAGE 133 ---
 
1  System  Displays  the  Ticket  Summary  card  with  the  ticket  details  and  retrieves  the  list  of  available  staff  members  with  their  real-time  workload  indicators.  
2  User  Enters  a  task  title  in  the  "Task  Title"  field.  
3  User  Optionally  enters  detailed  instructions  in  the  "Task  Description"  field.  
4  User  Selects  a  staff  member  from  the  staff  selection  list.  
5  User  Taps  the  "[  ASSIGN  TASK  ]"  button.  
6  System  Validates  all  mandatory  fields,  creates  a  new  task  record  linked  to  the  ticket,  updates  the  ticket  status  to  ASSIGNED,  and  dispatches  a  push  notification  to  the  selected  staff  member.   
Alternative  flow  
 
AT1  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Task  Title  field  is  left  empty:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Highlights  the  Task  Title  field  with  a  red  border  and  displays  the  inline  error:  "(!)  Task  title  is  required."  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  assignment  process.  
AT2  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  no  staff  member  has  been  selected:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  error  message:  "Please  select  a  staff  member  to  assign  this  task."  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  assignment  process.  
AT3  At  Step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  ticket  status  has  already  been  changed  by  another  Manager  (e.g.,  no  longer  PENDING):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
 133  |  Page  
--- PAGE 134 ---
 
6.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Cannot  assign  task.  This  ticket  is  no  longer  in  PENDING  status."  
6.2  System  Remains  on  the  screen  and  suggests  the  user  navigate  back  to  the  Ticket  Management  list.  
AT4  At  Step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  network  error  or  server  error  occurs:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  error  message:  "An  error  occurred.  Please  try  again  later."  (MSG10).  
6.2  System  Remains  on  the  form  screen  with  all  entered  data  preserved,  allowing  the  user  to  retry.  
 
Business  rule  
 
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-40  Ticket  Assignment  Constraints:  Only  users  with  the  MANAGER  or  LANDLORD  role  are  authorized  to  assign  repair  tickets.  A  ticket  can  only  be  assigned  if  its  current  status  is  PENDING.  
BR-41  Workload  Indicator:  The  system  must  fetch  real-time  workload  counts  (number  of  currently  active  tasks  in  status  ASSIGNED  or  IN_PROGRESS)  for  all  staff  members  and  display  correct  status  tags  (Available  /  Busy  X  task(s))  to  aid  decision-making.  
 134  |  Page  
--- PAGE 135 ---
 
BR-18  FCM  Notification  Requirement:  Upon  successful  task  assignment,  the  system  must  immediately  dispatch  a  push  notification  to  the  assigned  staff  member's  device.  
BR-42  Technician  Task  Access:  Only  the  specific  staff  member  assigned  to  the  task  (or  an  Manager)  is  authorized  to  view  and  update  the  task  progress.

Use  Case  ID  UC22  Use  Case  Name  View  Task  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician,  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Security  Guard,  Manager,  and  Landlord  to  view  the  list  of  assigned  repair  or  maintenance  tasks  that  require  action,  filter  them  by  status,  and  select  individual  tasks  for  detailed  review.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  SECURITY_GUARD,  MANAGER,  or  LANDLORD  role.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  navigates  to  the  "My  Tasks"  screen  from  the  bottom  navigation  bar  or  dashboard.  
 137  |  Page  
--- PAGE 138 ---
 
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  retrieves  and  displays  the  list  of  tasks  assigned  to  the  current  user.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Navigates  to  the  "My  Tasks"  screen.  
2  System  Retrieves  all  tasks  assigned  to  the  logged-in  user  from  the  database,  including  the  associated  ticket  and  apartment  details.  
3  System  Renders  the  screen  with  a  list  of  task  cards,  sorted  by  assigned  date  (newest  first).  Each  card  displays  the  task  ID,  title,  category,  apartment,  status  badge,  assigned  by,  and  relevant  date.  
4  User  Optionally  selects  a  filter  tab  (e.g.,  Active,  Completed)  to  narrow  down  the  list.  
5  System  Updates  the  displayed  list  to  show  only  tasks  matching  the  selected  filter.  
6  User  Taps  on  a  specific  task  card.  
7  System  Navigates  to  the  Task  Detail  /  Update  Progress  screen  (UC#23)  for  the  selected  task.   
Alternative  flow  
 
AT1  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  there  are  no  tasks  assigned  to  the  current  user:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  an  empty  state  message:  "No  tasks  assigned  to  you."  
2.2  System  Hides  the  task  card  list  area.  
AT2  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  there  are  no  tasks  matching  the  selected  filter:  
 138  |  Page  
--- PAGE 139 ---
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  a  filtered  empty  state  message:  "No  [Active/Completed]  tasks  found."  
 
Business  rule  
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-42  Technician  Task  Access:  Only  the  specific  staff  member  assigned  to  the  task  is  authorized  to  view  and  update  their  own  task  list.  The  task  list  must  only  display  tasks  that  have  been  explicitly  assigned  to  the  logged-in  user.  managers  and  Landlords  can  view  all  tasks.  
BR-39  Role-Based  Data  Filtering:  The  database  query  must  restrict  task  results  based  on  roles.  Security  Guards  can  only  see  tasks  assigned  to  their  user  ID.  managers  and  Landlords  have  read  access  to  all  tasks  across  all  staff.

Usecase  description  
 
 
Use  Case  ID  UC23  Use  Case  Name  
Update  Progress  
Date  01/06/2026  
Author  Vo  Minh  Tai  Version  1.0  Actor  Security  Guard,  Janitor,  Technician   
Description  Allows  the  assigned  security  guard  member  to  update  the  progress  of  a  repair  task  by  entering  progress  notes,  uploading  completion  photos  as  proof,  and  marking  the  task  as  complete.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  logged  in  with  the  SECURITY_GUARD  role.  PRE-02:  The  task  is  currently  in  ASSIGNED  or  IN_PROGRESS  status  and  is  assigned  to  the  current  user.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  security  guard  member  taps  on  a  specific  task  card  from  the  My  Tasks  list  screen  (UC#22).  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  task  status  is  updated  to  COMPLETED.  POS-02:  Completion  images  and  progress  notes  are  saved.  POS-03:  The  associated  ticket  status  is  updated  to  RESOLVED.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Retrieves  and  displays  the  full  task  details,  including  Task  Info,  Ticket  Reference  card,  and  Before  Photos  from  the  original  repair  ticket.  
2  Security  Guard  Reviews  the  task  instructions,  ticket  details,  and  before  photos  to  understand  the  scope  of  work.  
 143  |  Page  
--- PAGE 144 ---
 
3  Security  Guard  Enters  progress  notes  describing  the  work  performed  in  the  text  area.  
4  Security  Guard  Uploads  at  least  1  completion  photo  by  tapping  the  "+"  placeholder.  Photos  are  compressed  on  the  client  side  before  upload.  
5  Security  Guard  Taps  the  "[  MARK  AS  COMPLETE  ]"  button.  
6  System  Validates  that  at  least  1  completion  photo  is  uploaded,  saves  progress  notes  and  images,  updates  the  task  status  to  COMPLETED  with  the  current  timestamp,  and  updates  the  linked  ticket  status  to  RESOLVED.   
Alternative  flow  
 
AT1  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  no  completion  photo  has  been  uploaded:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Highlights  the  Completion  Photos  section  with  a  red  border  and  displays  the  inline  error:  "(!)  At  least  1  completion  photo  is  required."  
5.2  System  Blocks  the  completion  process  until  at  least  one  photo  is  uploaded.  
AT2  At  Step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  staff  member  attempts  to  upload  a  photo  that  exceeds  5MB  or  is  not  JPG/PNG:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  error  message:  "Image  size  exceeds  the  limit.  Please  choose  a  smaller  file."  (MSG09).  
4.2  System  Rejects  the  file  and  does  not  add  it  to  the  upload  queue.  
 144  |  Page  
--- PAGE 145 ---
 
AT3  At  Step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  staff  member  attempts  to  upload  more  than  3  photos:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  message:  "Maximum  3  photos  allowed."  
4.2  System  Prevents  the  additional  photo  from  being  added.  
AT4  At  Step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  network  error  or  server  error  occurs:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  a  SnackBar  error  message:  "An  error  occurred.  Please  try  again  later."  (MSG10).  
6.2  System  Remains  on  the  detail  screen  with  all  entered  data  preserved,  allowing  the  staff  member  to  retry.  
 
Business  rule  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-43  Resolution  Proof  Requirement:  A  staff  member  cannot  mark  a  task  as  COMPLETED  without  uploading  at  least  one  photo  under  the  Completion  Photos  section  to  prevent  fraudulent  completion  logs.  
BR-42  Technician  Task  Access:  Only  the  specific  staff  member  assigned  to  the  task  is  authorized  to  update  the  task  progress  and  mark  it  as  complete.  
 145  |  Page  
--- PAGE 146 ---
 
BR-37  Image  Limits:  The  staff  member  can  attach  at  most  3  completion  photos.  Each  image  must  be  in  JPG/PNG  format  and  must  not  exceed  5MB.  
BR-10  Resource  Optimization:  All  media  files  captured  or  selected  by  users  must  be  compressed  on  the  client  side  before  being  transmitted  to  cloud  storage.

3.5.1.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC24  Use  Case  Name  Announce  Notification  
Author  Võ  Chiên  Th ắ ng  Version  1.0  Date  01/06/2026  
Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  Manager  to  create  and  publish  important  announcements.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  Manager  has  successfully  authenticated  and  logged  into  the  Manager  Dashboard  system.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Manager  accesses  the  "Community  Board"  section  and  clicks  the  "Create  New  Post"  button.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  post  is  saved  in  the  PostgreSQL  database.  
POS-02:  All  residents  can  instantly  view  the  new  post  on  their  Flutter  app's  home  feed.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager  Enters  the  post  title,  content,  and  uploads  an  optional  attached  image.  
2  Manager  Clicks  the  "Publish"  button.  
 148  |  Page  
7  Attachment  Zone  
Upload  Container  
File  /  Binary  No  Labeled  as  "ATTACHMENT".  Formatted  with  a  dashed  line  border  block  placeholder  enclosing  an  explicit  "Camera"  icon  element  accompanied  by  the  command  string  label  "ADD  IMAGE".  
--- PAGE 149 ---
 
3  Backend  Receives  the  request,  validates  the  input  payload  data,  and  commits  the  post  to  the  PostgreSQL  database.  
4  System  Automatically  triggers  the  real-time  notification  workflow  to  broadcast  to  all  residents.  
5  System  Refreshes  the  Manager  Dashboard  feed  and  displays  a  "Post  Published  Successfully"  toast  notification.  
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  3,  the  entered  data  is  invalid  or  missing  required  fields.  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Keeps  the  input  form  active,  highlights  the  missing  inputs  in  red,  and  displays  an  error  message:  "Title  and  Content  fields  cannot  be  empty."  
3.2  Manager  Corrects  the  information  and  returns  to  step  2  of  the  main  flow.   
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Content  Authorization:  Only  system  accounts  explicitly  assigned  the  role  =  'Manager'  hold  the  permissions  required  to  create,  update,  or  publish  posts  on  the  Community  Board.   
BR-10  Resource  Optimization:  All  media  files  (e.g.,  incident  scene  photos,  ID  cards,  avatars)  captured  or  selected  by  users  must  be  compressed  at  the  client  side  (Mobile  Application)  to  a  maximum  defined  threshold  (e.g.,  <  500KB)  before  being  transmitted  to  the  Cloudinary  storage.

3.5.2.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC25  Use  Case  Name  View  Notification  List  
Author  Võ  Chiên  Th ắ ng  Version  1.0  Date  01/06/2026  
Actor  Resident   
Description  Allows  residents  to  access  and  view  a  historical  list  of  all  system  alerts,  updates,  and  direct  messages  sent  to  their  account.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  authenticated  and  logged  into  the  Flutter  mobile  application.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  clicks  or  taps  on  the  "Community  Board"  tab  from  the  main  navigation  menu.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  retrieves  and  displays  the  notification  list  on  the  user's  screen.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Taps  on  the  "Notification"  tab/icon  on  the  interface.  
2  System  Sends  an  API  request  to  the  backend  to  fetch  the  user's  notification  history.  
3  Backend  Validates  the  user's  token,  queries  the  PostgreSQL  database  for  the  notification  records  associated  with  the  user  ID,  and  returns  the  data  payload.  
 152  |  Page  
10  Create  Post  FAB  
Floating  Action  Button  (FAB)  
-  Conditional  A  bold  circle  container  containing  a  +  symbol  at  the  bottom  right.  Visible  only  to  users  with  Manager  privileges.   
--- PAGE 153 ---
 
4  System  Receives  the  data  and  renders  the  "Notification  List"  screen,  sorting  the  items  chronologically  (newest  first).  
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  3,  the  user  has  not  received  any  notifications.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  Backend  Returns  an  empty  array/list  payload  to  the  client.  
3.2  System  Displays  an  empty  state  graphic  on  the  screen  along  with  the  message:  "You  don't  have  any  notifications  yet."  
 
 
AT2  At  step  2,  the  client  cannot  reach  the  server  or  the  server  encounters  an  error.    
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Detects  a  network  timeout  or  receives  an  error  HTTP  status  code  (e.g.,  500  Internal  Server  Error).  
2.2  System  Halts  the  loading  process  and  displays  a  snackbar/toast  error  message:  "Unable  to  load  notifications.  Please  check  your  connection  and  try  again."  along  with  a  "Retry"  button.  
2.3  Resident  Clicks  "Retry"  to  return  to  step  1  of  the  main  flow.  
 
Business  Rules  
 153  |  Page  
--- PAGE 154 ---
 
#  Rule  Description  
BR-51  Notification  Pagination:  To  optimize  database  queries  and  client  rendering  performance,  the  notification  list  must  implement  pagination  (e.g.,  fetching  20  records  per  request)  utilizing  lazy  loading  or  infinite  scrolling  as  the  user  scrolls  down  the  list.  
BR-52  Notification  Sorting  Priority:  Notifications  must  always  be  sorted  by  their  creation  timestamp  in  descending  order,  ensuring  the  most  recent  alerts  appear  at  the  top.  
BR-53  Notification  Data  Isolation:  Users  can  only  retrieve  and  view  notifications  that  are  explicitly  addressed  to  their  specific user_id  or  broadcasted  to  their  assigned  role/apartment.  Cross-user  notification  querying  is  strictly  prohibited.

--- PAGE 157 ---
 
Author  Võ  Chiên  Th ắ ng  Version  1.0  Date  01/06/2026  
Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  residents  to  view  the  full  content  and  attachments  of  a  specific  notification.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  user  has  successfully  authenticated  and  logged  into  the  application.  
PRE-02:  The  user  is  currently  on  the  "Notification  List"  screen  and  has  at  least  one  notification  available.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  user  clicks  or  taps  on  a  specific  notification  item  from  the  list.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  renders  the  detailed  content,  timestamp,  and  any  attachments  of  the  selected  notification.  
POS-02:  The  system  updates  the  notification's  status  to  'Read'  in  the  database.  
POS-03:  The  global  unread  notification  counter  badge  is  decreased  accordingly.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  User  Taps  on  a  specific  notification  card  from  the  Notification  List.  
2  System  Immediately  navigates  to  the  "Notification  Detail"  screen  and  displays  the  locally  cached  summary  data  while  sending  an  API  request  to  fetch  full  details  and  update  the  read  status.  
3  Backend  Validates  the  request,  retrieves  the  full  notification  payload  from  the  PostgreSQL  database,  and  executes  an  update  to  set  is_read  =  true for  this  specific  record.  
 157  |  Page  
--- PAGE 158 ---
 
4  System  Renders  the  complete  information  including  the  full  body  text,  sender  details,  precise  timestamp,  and  any  embedded  action  buttons  (e.g.,  deep  links  to  tickets/bills).  
5  System  Updates  the  unread  notification  badge  count  on  the  main  navigation  bar  to  reflect  the  newly  read  item.  
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  3,  the  notification  has  been  deleted  or  recalled  by  the  Manager  before  the  user  clicked  it.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  Backend  Fails  to  find  the  record  and  returns  a  404  Not  Found  error.  
4.1  System  Displays  an  error  graphic  with  the  message:  "This  notification  is  no  longer  available  or  has  been  removed  by  the  management."  
4.2  System  Provides  a  "Go  Back"  button  to  return  the  user  to  the  Notification  List  and  removes  the  dead  item  from  the  local  list.  
 
 
AT2  At  step  3,  the  notification  has  been  deleted  or  recalled  by  the  Manager  before  the  user  clicked  it.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Detects  a  network  timeout  or  receives  an  error  HTTP  status  code.  
 158  |  Page  
--- PAGE 159 ---
 
2.2  System  Displays  the  locally  available  cached  data  (Title  and  partial  content)  but  shows  a  warning  banner:  "Offline  mode:  Cannot  load  full  details  or  mark  as  read.  Please  check  your  connection."  
2.3  User  Reconnects  to  the  internet  and  pulls  to  refresh,  returning  to  step  2  of  the  main  flow.  
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-54  Asynchronous  Read  Update:  The  action  of  marking  a  notification  as  'Read'  must  be  processed  asynchronously  by  the  backend  to  ensure  it  does  not  block  or  delay  the  UI  rendering  of  the  detail  screen  for  the  user.  
BR-55  Notification  Immutability:  The  content  of  a  notification  displayed  in  the  detail  view  (Title,  Body,  Timestamp,  Sender)  is  strictly  read-only  for  the  receiving  user.  Users  cannot  edit,  reply  directly,  or  forward  the  system  notification.  
BR-56  Actionable  Deep  Linking:  If  a  notification  is  linked  to  a  specific  system  entity,  the  Notification  Detail  screen  must  provide  an  actionable  button  or  deep  link  that  routes  the  user  directly  to  the  corresponding  entity's  detail  screen.

3.5.4.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC27  Use  Case  Name  Receive  Notification  
Author  Võ  Chiên  Th ắ ng  Version  1.0  Date  01/06/2026  
Actor  System  
 160  |  Page  
#  Field  Name  Type  Mandatory  Max  Length  Description  
1  Notification  Icon  
Icon  Indicator  -  Yes  Enclosed  within  a  simple  squared  frame  box.  Renders  a  generic  Bell  icon  graphic  to  signify  a  system  alert  or  announcement  event.  
2  Notification  Title  
Typography  String  Yes  Displays  the  message  header  or  main  subject  line  in  bold,  capitalized  text.  Dynamically  mapped  from  the  Pusher  event  payload.  
3  Notification  Body  
Typography  String  Yes  Displays  the  message  detail  snippet.  Supports  multi-line  wrapping  up  to  250  characters  max.  
4  Dismiss  Button  Icon  Button  -  Yes  An  'X'  symbol  marked  at  the  top-right  corner  of  the  overlay  container.  Tapping  this  button  explicitly  triggers  a  close  transition,  animating  the  banner  out  of  view.  
--- PAGE 161 ---
 
Description  A  server-side  background  process  that  automatically  dispatches  instant  push  notifications  directly  to  end-user  mobile  devices  via  Firebase  Cloud  Messaging  (FCM)  whenever  major  system  milestones  occur,  ensuring  delivery  even  if  the  application  is  completely  closed  or  the  device  screen  is  locked.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  target  user's  mobile  device  has  granted  application  notification  permissions  and  successfully  registered  its  unique  FCM_Token  into  the  PostgreSQL  database  upon  authentication.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Triggered  programmatically  by  specific  system  actions   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  Target  mobile  devices  receive  and  display  the  push  notification  overlay  banner  via  the  native  operating  system  tray  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Invokes  the  notification  engine  corresponding  to  the  specific  business  event  that  just  occurred.  
2  Backend  Queries  the  PostgreSQL  database  to  fetch  the  active  list  of  FCM_Token  strings  mapped  to  the  target  recipient  user_id  accounts.  
3  Backend  Packages  the  notification  data  payload  and  sends  an  HTTP  POST  request  to  the  Firebase  Cloud  Messaging  API.  
4  Firebase  Processes  the  incoming  data  package  from  the  backend,  handles  cross-platform  routing,  and  pushes  the  alert  down  to  active  target  device  connection.  
5  System  The  user's  mobile  hardware  receives  the  transmission,  triggers  a  native  sound/vibration  alert,  and  renders  the  popup  notification  banner  on  the  OS  status  bar.  
 161  |  Page  
--- PAGE 162 ---
 
6  User  Taps  the  notification  banner;  the  native  OS  wakes  up  or  switches  focus  to  the  APORA  app,  reads  the  embedded  Deep  Link  Target  Route  string,  and  automatically  redirects  the  user  straight  to  the  relevant  detail  screen.  
 Alternative  flows  
 
AT1  At  step  2,  if  a  target  user  account  has  no  active  FCM  Token  registered  in  the  database   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  Backend  Skips  the  notification  process  for  that  specific  user  ID,  writes  a  "delivery  failed"  record  into  the  system  event  log,  and  gracefully  proceeds  with  processing  the  queue  for  the  remaining  users.   
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-44  Notification  Accuracy:  The  system  must  capture  and  store  the  device's  unique  FCM  Token  upon  a  successful  user  login  and  strictly  destroy  this  token  entry  in  the  PostgreSQL  database  upon  logout.  This  ensures  sensitive  notifications  are  not  pushed  to  the  wrong  recipient  after  an  account  or  resident  profile  switch  on  the  same  physical  smartphone  device.

Precondition  PRE-01:  Both  the  Resident  and  Manager  are  connected  to  the  Internet  and  have  navigated  to  their  respective  active  Chat  screens.

Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Sender  Types  text  content  into  the  message  bar  field  and  presses  the  "Send"  button.

3  Backend  Receives  the  incoming  message  and  logs  the  row.  
4  Backend  Instructs  the  Pusher  WebSocket  service  to  broadcast  an  event  containing  the  message  payload  to  the  dedicated  room  channel..  
5  System  The  receiver's  client  application  captures  the  socket  event  and  smoothly  appends  the  new  message  bubble  to  the  interface.  
 Alternative  flows

3.1  System   The  local  client  app  fails  to  receive  an  HTTP  200  OK  acknowledgment  from  the  API,  updating  the  local  message  status  to  "Failed  to  send"  with  a  red  warning  icon  for  manual  retry.

Text  /  Badge  Yes  50  Displays  the  apartment  unit  number  and  current  status,  such  as  OCCUPIED,  EMPTY,  or  INACTIVE.  
9  Owner  Information  Text  Conditional  100  Displays  the  current  owner  or  main  resident  name  if  the  apartment  is  occupied.  If  the  apartment  is  empty,  displays  “No  owner”.  
10  Apartment  Indicators  
Text  /  Icon  Group  
Yes  -  Displays  key  operational  indicators  of  the  apartment,  such  as  unpaid  invoice  count  and  unresolved  repair  ticket  count.  
11  View  Detail  Button  Icon  Button  Yes  -  Allows  Manager  or  Landlord  to  open  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  of  the  selected  apartment.  
12  Add  Apartment  Button  
Floating  Action  Button  
Conditional  -  Allows  authorized  users  to  open  the  Create  Apartment  
 170  |  Page  
--- PAGE 171 ---
 
screen.  This  button  is  displayed  only  if  the  user  has  permission  to  create  apartment  records.  
 
3.6.1.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC29  Use  Case  Name  View  Apartment  List  
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Manager  ,  Landlord,   Security  Guard   
Description  Manager  ,  Landlord   or  Security  Guard  views  the  structural  list  of  all  apartments  in  the  building.  The  system  allows  users  to  search  and  filter  apartments  by  unit  number,  owner  name,  occupancy  status,  and  unpaid  invoice  status  so  that  they  can  quickly  monitor  apartment  conditions  and  access  apartment  details  when  needed.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  User  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  User  account  has  the  role  MANAGER,  LANDLORD   or  SECURITY_GUARD.  
PRE-03:  Apartment  records  already  exist  in  the  database.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  User  accesses  the  Apartment  Management  screen  from  the  main  dashboard  or  workspace  menu.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  apartment  list  is  displayed  successfully.  
POS-02:  Matching  apartment  records  are  displayed  according  to  the  selected  search  keyword  or  filter.  
POS-03:  User  can  select  an  apartment  from  the  list  to  view  detailed  apartment  information.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
 171  |  Page  
--- PAGE 172 ---
 
1  Manager  /  Landlord  /  Security  Guard  
On  the  dashboard,  clicks  the  “Apartment  Management”  menu.   
2  System  Retrieves  apartment  records  from  the  database,  including  unit  number,  floor,  owner  or  main  resident,  apartment  status,  unpaid  invoice  count,  and  unresolved  ticket  count.   
3  System  Displays  the  Apartment  Management  screen  with  summary  cards,  search  bar,  status  filters,  and  apartment  list.   
4  Manager  /  Landlord  /  Security  Guard  
Searches  by  unit  number  or  owner  name,  or  filters  apartments  by  status  such  as  All,  Empty,  Occupied,  Inactive,  or  Has  Debt.   
5  System  Queries  apartment  records  from  the  database  based  on  the  entered  keyword  or  selected  filter.   
6  System  Displays  the  matching  apartment  list  in  grid  or  list  view.   
7  Manager  /  Landlord  /  Security  Guard  
Reviews  apartment  information  such  as  unit  number,  owner  name,  occupancy  status,  unpaid  invoice  count,  and  unresolved  ticket  count.   
8  Manager  /  Landlord  /  Security  Guard  
Selects  an  apartment  from  the  list  to  view  more  detailed  information.   
9  System   Redirects  the  user  to  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  for  the  selected  apartment.   
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  no  apartment  matches  the  search  keyword  or  selected  filter.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  an  empty-state  message,  such  as  “No  apartment  found.”  
 172  |  Page  
--- PAGE 173 ---
 
5.2  System  Keeps  the  search  bar  and  filters  available  so  the  user  can  search  again.  
 
AT2  At  step  1  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  logged-in  user  does  not  have  permission  to  access  Apartment  Management:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System  Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  access  this  function.”  
1.2  System  Blocks  access  to  the  Apartment  Management  screen  and  keeps  the  user  on  their  current  screen.  
 
AT3  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  cannot  load  apartment  data  due  to  a  server  or  database  error:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Unable  to  load  apartment  list.  Please  try  again.”  
5.2  System  Provides  a  retry  option  for  the  user  to  reload  the  apartment  list.  
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  users  with  role  MANAGER,  LANDLORD,  or  SECURITY_GUARD  are  allowed  to  access  the  Apartment  Management  screen.   
BR-61  Apartment  UI  Display  &  Indicators :  The  list  must  display  essential  information  and  operational  indicators  (unpaid  invoices,  unresolved  tickets)  based  on  the  latest  database  state.   
BR-62  UI  Navigation  &  Read-Only  Control :  Searching,  filtering,  and  navigating  to  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  or  Create  screen  must  not  modify  any  apartment  data.

3.6.2.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC30  Use  Case  Name  View  Apartment  Detail   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Manager,  Landlord,  Security  Guard  
Description  Manager,  Landlord,  or  Security  Guard  views  detailed  information  of  a  selected  apartment,  including  apartment  physical  information,  current  owner  or  main  resident  information,  approved  roommates,  recent  repair  tickets,  and  related  operational  indicators.  This  use  case  helps  authorized  users  understand  the  current  residential  and  operational  status  of  an  apartment.  For  Security  Guard,  the  screen  supports  room  inspection  and  on-site  verification,  while  management-only  actions  such  as  checkout  remain  restricted  to  Manager  or  Landlord.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  User  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  User  account  has  the  role  MANAGER,  LANDLORD,  or  SECURITY_GUARD.  
PRE-03:  Apartment  records  already  exist  in  the  database.  
PRE-04:  User  has  accessed  the  Apartment  Management  screen  and  selected  a  valid  apartment.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  User  selects  an  apartment  from  the  Apartment  List  screen  to  view  its  detailed  information.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  Detailed  apartment  information  is  displayed  successfully.  
POS-02:  Related  data  such  as  owner  information,  approved  roommate  list,  recent  invoices,  and  recent  repair  tickets  is  displayed  based  on  the  user’s  role.  
 177  |  Page  
--- PAGE 178 ---
 
POS-03:  No  apartment  data  is  modified  by  this  use  case.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager  /  Landlord  /  Security  Guard   
On  the  Apartment  Management  screen,  selects  an  apartment  from  the  apartment  list.  
2  System  Receives  the  selected  apartment  ID  and  queries  apartment  information  from  the  database.  
3  System  Retrieves  physical  apartment  information,  including  unit  number,  floor,  area  size,  base  rent,  and  occupancy  status.   
4  System  Checks  whether  the  apartment  currently  has  an  assigned  owner.  
5  System  Retrieves  current  owner  information  if  the  apartment  status  is  ‘OCCUPIED’.  
6  System  Retrieves  approved  roommate  information  linked  to  the  selected  apartment.   
7  System  Retrieves  recent  invoices  related  to  the  apartment’s  active  contract  based  on  the  user’s  role  permission.   
8  System   Retrieves  recent  repair  tickets  related  to  the  selected  apartment.   
9  System   Displays  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  with  apartment  information,  owner  information,  roommate  list,  recent  invoices,  and  recent  repair  tickets.  
10  Manager  /  Landlord  /  Security  Guard   
Reviews  the  displayed  apartment  information  for  management  or  room  inspection  purposes.   
 Alternative  flows  
 178  |  Page  
--- PAGE 179 ---
 
AT1  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  apartment  has  status  ‘EMPTY’  and  has  no  current  owner:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  owner  information  as  empty  or  “No  current  owner.”  
5.2  System  Continues  displaying  the  remaining  apartment  information.  
 
AT2  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  apartment  has  no  registered  roommates:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  an  empty-state  message  in  the  roommate  section,  such  as  “No  roommates  registered.”  
6.2  System  Continues  displaying  other  apartment  details.  
 
AT3  At  step  7  or  step  8  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  apartment  has  no  recent  invoices  or  repair  tickets.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  empty-state  message  in  the  corresponding  section,  such  as  “No  recent  invoices  found”  or  “No  recent  repair  tickets  found.”  
7.2  System  Continues  displaying  the  Apartment  Detail  screen.  
 
AT4  At  step  1  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  apartment  record  no  longer  exists  or  has  been  deactivated.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Apartment  not  found.”  
 179  |  Page  
--- PAGE 180 ---
 
1.2  System  Redirects  the  user  back  to  the  Apartment  Management  screen.  
 
AT5  At  step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  cannot  load  apartment  details  due  to  a  server  or  database  error:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Unable  to  load  apartment  details.  Please  try  again.”  
2.2  System  Provides  a  retry  option  or  allows  the  user  to  return  to  the  Apartment  Management  screen.  
 
AT6  At  step  1  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  logged-in  user  does  not  have  permission  to  view  apartment  details:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System  Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  view  apartment  details.”  
1.2  System  Blocks  access  to  the  Apartment  Detail  screen.  
 
AT7  At  step  9  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  logged-in  user  is  Security  Guard.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
9.1   System  Displays  only  information  required  for  room  inspection  and  hides  management-only  actions  such  as  Checkout,  Edit  Apartment,  or  Check-in.   
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
 180  |  Page  
--- PAGE 181 ---
 
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  authorized  users  can  view  details.  Manager/Landlord  sees  full  data;  Security  Guard  sees  limited  data  for  room  inspection  only.   
BR-08  Profile  View  Data  Masking :  Sensitive  resident  and  roommate  data  (e.g.,  CCCD  numbers)  must  be  masked  unless  full  visibility  is  required  by  an  Manager/Landlord  role.   
BR-31  Billing  History  Isolation :  The  current  owner  must  not  be  able  to  view  previous  owner  data.  Operational  history  of  previous  residents  must  be  protected.   
BR-47  Apartment  Status  Constraints :  If  the  apartment  is  "EMPTY",  it  must  display  "No  current  owner"  and  management  actions  like  Checkout  must  be  hidden.   
BR-62  UI  Navigation  &  Read-Only  Control :  Viewing  apartment  details  is  strictly  read-only.

10  Create  Button  Button  Yes  -  Submits  the  form  to  create  a  new  apartment  after  all  required  fields  pass  validation.  
 
3.6.3.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC31  Use  Case  Name  Create  New  Apartment   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord   
Description  Landlord  creates  a  new  physical  apartment  unit  in  the  system  by  entering  required  apartment  information  such  as  floor  number,  room  number  or  unit  number,  area  size,  and  base  rent.  After  successful  creation,  the  apartment  is  saved  into  the  database  with  the  default  status  “EMPTY”  and  becomes  available  for  future  resident  check-in.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
 183  |  Page  
--- PAGE 184 ---
 
PRE-02:  User  account  has  the  role  LANDLORD.  
PRE-03:  The  apartment  room  number  or  unit  number  to  be  created  must  not  already  exist  in  the  system.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Landlord  accesses  the  Apartment  Management  screen  and  clicks  the  “Add  New  Apartment”  button.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  apartment  record  is  created  successfully  in  the  database.  
POS-02:  The  new  apartment  status  is  set  to  “EMPTY”  by  default.  
POS-03:  The  newly  created  apartment  appears  in  the  Apartment  Management  list.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Landlord   On  the  Dashboard,  clicks  the  “Apartment  Management”  menu.   
2  System  Displays  the  Apartment  Management  screen  with  apartment  list,  search  bar,  status  filters,  and  the  “Add  New  Apartment”  button.   
3  Landlord   Clicks  the  “Add  New  Apartment”  button.   
4  System  Displays  the  Create  New  Apartment  form.   
5  Landlord   Enters  apartment  information,  including  floor  number,  room  number,  area  size,  and  base  rent.   
6  Landlord   Clicks  the  “Create”  button.   
7  System  Validates  mandatory  fields  and  checks  whether  the  room  number  is  unique.   
8  System   Creates  a  new  apartment  record  in  the  database.   
9  System   Sets  the  apartment  status  to  ‘EMPTY’  by  default.   
 184  |  Page  
--- PAGE 185 ---
 
10  System   Displays  a  success  message  and  redirects  Landlord  back  to  the  Apartment  Management  screen.   
11  System   Refreshes  the  apartment  list  and  displays  the  newly  created  apartment.   
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  Landlord  leaves  one  or  more  mandatory  fields  empty:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  inline  validation  messages  beside  the  missing  fields.  
7.2  System  Blocks  the  apartment  creation  process  and  waits  for  Landlord  to  complete  all  required  fields.   
 
AT2  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  entered  room  number  already  exists  in  the  system:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Room  number  already  exists.”  
7.2  System  Stops  apartment  creation  and  keeps  the  entered  data  on  the  form  for  Landlord  to  edit.   
 
AT3  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  area  size,  base  rent,  or  deposit  value  is  invalid:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error,  such  as  “Area  size  must  be  greater  than  0”  or  “Base  rent  must  be  a  positive  number.”  
7.2  System  Blocks  apartment  creation  until  valid  values  are  entered.  
 
 185  |  Page  
--- PAGE 186 ---
 
AT4  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  Landlordclicks  the  “Cancel”  or  “Back”  button:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  a  confirmation  popup  if  the  form  has  unsaved  changes.  
6.2  Landlord  Confirms  leaving  the  form.  
6.3  System  Discards  entered  data  and  returns  to  the  Apartment  Management  screen.  
 
AT5  At  step  8  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  fails  to  save  the  apartment  due  to  a  server  or  database  error:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
8.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Failed  to  create  apartment.  Please  try  again.”  
8.2  System  Keeps  the  entered  data  on  the  form  so  Landlord  can  retry  the  creation  process.   
 
AT6  At  step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  non-Landlord  user  tries  to  access  the  Create  New  Apartment  function.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  perform  this  action.”  
3.2  System  Blocks  access  to  the  Create  New  Apartment  form.  
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
 186  |  Page  
--- PAGE 187 ---
 
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  users  with  role  LANDLORD  are  allowed  to  execute  the  Create  New  Apartment  function.   
BR-63  Form  Data  Validation  &  Integrity :  Floor,  room  number,  area  size,  and  base  rent  are  mandatory.  Numeric  values  must  be  positive.  Room  numbers  must  be  absolutely  unique.   
BR-47  Apartment  Status  Constraints :  A  newly  created  apartment  must  be  strictly  assigned  the  status  "EMPTY"  by  default.

10  Update  Scope  Notice  
Information  Message  
No  -  Informs  Landlord  that  only  physical  apartment  information  and  rental  values  will  be  updated.  
11  Cancel  Button  Button  Yes  -  Discards  unsaved  changes  and  returns  Landlord  to  the  previous  Apartment  Detail  screen.  
 189  |  Page  
--- PAGE 190 ---
 
12  Save  Changes  Button  
Button  Yes  -  Submits  the  updated  apartment  information  after  all  required  fields  pass  validation.  
 
3.6.4.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC32  Use  Case  Name  Update  Apartment  Information   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord   
Description  Landlord  updates  the  physical  information  of  an  existing  apartment,  such  as  floor  number,  room  number  or  unit  number,  area  size,  and  base  rent.  This  use  case  allows  Landlord  to  correct  apartment  data  or  adjust  rental  information  while  keeping  the  apartment  lifecycle  status  and  owner  assignment  unchanged.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  User  account  has  the  role  LANDLORD.  
PRE-03:  The  selected  apartment  already  exists  in  the  database.  
PRE-04:  Landlord  has  opened  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  of  the  selected  apartment.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Landlord  clicks  the  “Edit  Info”  button  on  the  Apartment  Detail  screen.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  Apartment  information  is  updated  successfully  in  the  database.  
POS-02:  The  updated  apartment  information  is  displayed  on  the  Apartment  Detail  screen.  
POS-03:  Apartment  status  and  owner  assignment  remain  unchanged  unless  handled  by  a  separate  check-in  or  checkout  use  case.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
 190  |  Page  
--- PAGE 191 ---
 
1  Landlord   On  the  Apartment  Management  screen,  selects  an  apartment  from  the  apartment  list.   
2  System  Displays  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  with  current  apartment  information.   
3  Landlord   Clicks  the  “Edit  Info”  button.   
4  System  Displays  the  Update  Apartment  Information  form  with  existing  apartment  data  pre-filled.   
5  Landlord   Updates  apartment  information,  such  as  floor  number,  room  number,  area  size,  base  rent,  or  deposit  value.   
6  Landlord   Clicks  the  “Save  Changes”  button.   
7  System  Validates  mandatory  fields,  numeric  values,  and  room  number  uniqueness  if  the  room  number  is  changed.   
8  System   Updates  the  apartment  record  in  the  database.   
9  System   Displays  a  success  message  and  returns  Landlord  to  the  Apartment  Detail  screen.   
10  System   Refreshes  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  with  the  latest  apartment  information.   
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  Landlord  leaves  one  or  more  mandatory  fields  empty.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  inline  validation  messages  beside  the  missing  fields.  
7.2  System  Blocks  the  update  process  and  waits  for  Landlord  to  complete  all  required  fields.   
 
 191  |  Page  
--- PAGE 192 ---
 
AT2  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  updated  room  number  already  exists  in  another  apartment  record:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Room  number  already  exists.”  
7.2  System  Stops  the  update  process  and  keeps  the  entered  data  on  the  form  for  Landlord  to  edit.  
 
AT3  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  area  size,  base  rent,  or  deposit  value  is  invalid:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error,  such  as  “Area  size  must  be  greater  than  0”  or  “Base  rent  must  be  a  positive  number.”  
7.2  System  Blocks  the  update  process  until  valid  values  are  entered.  
 
AT4  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  Landlord  clicks  “Cancel”  or  the  back  button:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  a  confirmation  popup  if  there  are  unsaved  changes.  
6.2  Landlord  Confirms  leaving  the  form.  
6.3  System  Discards  unsaved  changes  and  returns  to  the  Apartment  Detail  screen.  
 
AT5  At  step  8  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  fails  to  update  the  apartment  due  to  a  server  or  database  error:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
 192  |  Page  
--- PAGE 193 ---
 
8.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Failed  to  update  apartment  information.  Please  try  again.”  
8.2  System  Keeps  the  entered  data  on  the  form  so  Landlord  can  retry  the  update  process.  
 
AT6  At  step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  non-Landlord  user  tries  to  access  the  Update  Apartment  Information  function:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  perform  this  action.”  
3.2  System  Blocks  access  to  the  Update  Apartment  Information  form.  
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  Landlords  can  execute  the  Update  function.   
BR-63  Form  Data  Validation  &  Integrity :  Updated  room  numbers  must  not  conflict  with  existing  records.  All  monetary  and  area  inputs  must  be  valid  positive  numbers.   
BR-48  Historical  Billing  Rent  Protection :  Updating  the  base  rent  of  an  apartment  must  not  modify  old  invoices  or  old  contract  rent  snapshots  created  prior  to  the  update.   
BR-64  Lifecycle  Separation :  Updating  basic  physical  information  must  not  alter  the  apartment  status,  owner  assignment,  or  active  contracts.
