3.6.6.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC33  Use  Case  Name  Check-in  Apartment   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord,  Manager   
 196  |  Page  
--- PAGE 197 ---
 
Description  Landlord  or  Manager  performs  the  check-in  process  for  a  new  resident  owner  by  assigning  the  owner  to  an  empty  apartment.  The  system  creates  a  new  Resident  account  using  the  provided  phone  number  as  the  login  username,  creates  an  active  contract  for  the  selected  apartment,  links  the  owner  to  the  apartment,  and  changes  the  apartment  status  from  “EMPTY”  to  “OCCUPIED”.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  User  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  User  account  has  the  role  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER.  
PRE-03:  The  selected  apartment  already  exists  in  the  database.  
PRE-04:  The  selected  apartment  must  have  status  “EMPTY”.  
PRE-05:  The  new  owner’s  phone  number  must  not  already  exist  in  the  system.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Landlord  or  Manager  selects  an  empty  apartment  and  clicks  the  “Assign  New  Owner”  or  “Check-in”  button.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  Resident  account  is  created  successfully  with  status  “ACTIVE”.  
POS-02:  A  new  active  contract  is  created  for  the  selected  apartment  and  resident.  
POS-03:  The  selected  apartment  is  assigned  to  the  new  owner.  
POS-04:  The  selected  apartment  status  is  changed  from  “EMPTY”  to  “OCCUPIED”.  
POS-05:  The  new  owner  can  use  the  registered  phone  number  and  default  password  to  log  into  the  system.  
POS-06:  The  Apartment  Management  screen  is  refreshed  with  the  updated  apartment  status.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Landlord  /  Manager   On  the  Apartment  Management  screen,  filters  or  selects  an  apartment  with  status  ‘EMPTY’.   
2  System  Displays  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  for  the  selected  apartment.   
3  Landlord  /  Manager   Clicks  the  “Assign  New  Owner”  or  “Check-in”  button.   
 197  |  Page  
--- PAGE 198 ---
 
4  System  Displays  the  Assign  New  Owner  form  with  the  selected  apartment  information.   
5  Landlord  /  Manager   Enters  the  new  owner’s  full  name  and  phone  number.   
6  Landlord  /  Manager   Clicks  the  “Confirm  Check-in”  button.   
7  System  Validates  mandatory  fields,  phone  number  format,  and  phone  number  uniqueness.   
8  System   Creates  a  new  user  account  with  role  ‘RESIDENT’,  status  ‘ACTIVE’,  and  a  default  password.   
9  System   Creates  a  new  active  contract  linked  to  the  selected  apartment  and  the  new  Resident  account.   
10  System   Saves  the  contract  start  date,  contract  end  date,  base  rent  snapshot,  and  deposit  value  if  provided.   
11  System   Updates  the  selected  apartment  by  setting  the  new  Resident  account  as  the  current  owner.   
12  System   Changes  the  apartment  status  from  ‘EMPTY’  to  ‘OCCUPIED’.   
13  System   Displays  a  check-in  success  message.   
14  System   Redirects  Manager  back  to  the  Apartment  Management  or  Apartment  Detail  screen  and  refreshes  the  latest  apartment  information.   
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  apartment  is  not  “EMPTY”.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “This  apartment  is  already  occupied.”  
 198  |  Page  
--- PAGE 199 ---
 
3.2  System  Blocks  the  check-in  process.  
3.3  System  Does  not  create  a  new  Resident  account  and  does  not  update  apartment  data.  
 
AT2  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  Landlord  or  Manager  leaves  one  or  more  mandatory  fields  empty.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  inline  validation  messages  beside  the  missing  fields.  
7.2  System  Blocks  the  check-in  process  and  waits  for  the  user  to  complete  all  required  fields.   
 
AT3  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  phone  number  format  is  invalid:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error,  such  as  “Invalid  phone  number  format.”  
7.2  System  Stops  account  creation  and  waits  for  the  user  to  enter  a  valid  phone  number.   
 
AT4  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  phone  number  already  exists  in  the  system:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error,  such  as  “Phone  number  already  exists.”  
7.2  System  Stops  account  creation  and  keeps  the  entered  data  on  the  form  for  the  user  to  edit.   
 
 199  |  Page  
--- PAGE 200 ---
 
AT5  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  Landlord  or  Manager  clicks  “Cancel”  or  the  back  button.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  a  confirmation  popup  if  the  form  has  unsaved  changes.  
6.2  Manager   Confirms  leaving  the  form.  
6.3  System  Discards  entered  data  and  returns  to  the  previous  screen.  
6.4  System  Makes  no  changes  to  user  account  or  apartment  data.  
 
AT6  At  step  8  or  step  9  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  fails  to  create  the  Resident  account  or  update  the  apartment:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
8.1  System  Rolls  back  all  check-in  changes  to  maintain  data  consistency.  
8.2  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Check-in  failed.  Please  try  again.”  
8.3  System  Keeps  the  apartment  status  as  ‘EMPTY’  and  does  not  assign  any  owner.  
 
AT7  At  step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  non-authorized  user  tries  to  access  the  Check-in  function.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  perform  this  action.”  
3.2  System  Blocks  access  to  the  Assign  New  Owner  form.  
 
 200  |  Page  
--- PAGE 201 ---
 
AT8  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  contract  end  date  is  earlier  than  or  equal  to  the  contract  start  date.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error,  such  as  “Contract  end  date  must  be  later  than  start  date.”   
7.2  System  Blocks  the  check-in  process  until  valid  contract  dates  are  entered.   
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  can  perform  apartment  check-in.   
BR-47  Apartment  Status  Constraints :  Check-in  can  only  be  performed  on  an  "EMPTY"  apartment.  After  a  successful  check-in,  the  status  must  strictly  change  to  "OCCUPIED"  with  exactly  one  active  owner  assigned.   
BR-01  Account  Provisioning :  The  system  provisions  a  new  RESIDENT  account  using  the  phone  number  as  the  username,  applying  a  default  password  policy.   
BR-02  Phone  Number  Uniqueness :  The  phone  number  used  for  the  new  resident  must  not  exist  in  any  other  active  or  inactive  account  in  the  system.   
BR-65  Transaction  Safety :  The  check-in  process  (account  creation,  contract  creation,  apartment  update)  must  be  handled  as  a  single  ACID  transaction.  If  one  fails,  all  must  roll  back.   
 3.6.6  FID-34  -  Checkout  Apartment  3.6.6.1  Screen  Mock-up  
 201  |  Page  
--- PAGE 202 ---
 
 
Figure  3-6-6 :  Screen  Design  of  Checkout  Confirmation  
Table  3-6-6:  Screen  Definition  -  Checkout  Confirmation  Screen   
#  Field  Name  Type  Mandatory  Max  Length  Description  
1  Confirmation  Dialog  
Modal  /  Popup  Yes  -  Displays  a  warning  popup  before  processing  
 202  |  Page  

--- PAGE 203 ---
 
checkout  for  the  current  owner.  The  background  screen  is  dimmed  to  focus  Landlord  or  Manager  attention  on  the  confirmation  action.  
2  Warning  Title  Area  Icon  /  Text  Yes  50  Displays  a  warning  icon  and  the  title  “Confirm  Checkout”  to  indicate  that  this  is  a  sensitive  apartment  lifecycle  action.  
3  Checkout  Warning  Message  
Text  Yes  255  Explains  that  checkout  will  deactivate  the  current  resident  account,  remove  the  owner  assignment  from  the  apartment,  end  the  active  contract,  and  anonymize  sensitive  roommate  data.  
4  Checkout  Impact  Checklist  
Read-only  Checklist  
Yes  -  Displays  the  list  of  system  operations  that  will  be  performed  after  confirmation,  including  setting  apartment  status  to  EMPTY,  removing  owner  assignment,  setting  owner  account  to  INACTIVE,  ending  the  active  contract,  deleting  CCCD  
 203  |  Page

images  from  cloud  storage,  and  masking  CCCD  numbers  in  the  database.  
5  Cancel  Button  Button  Yes  -  Closes  the  confirmation  dialog  and  returns  Landlord  or  Manager  to  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  without  making  any  changes.  
6  Confirm  Checkout  Button  
Button  Yes  -  Confirms  the  checkout  operation.  The  system  starts  a  transaction,  updates  apartment  status  to  EMPTY,  removes  the  owner  reference,  deactivates  the  old  owner  account,  ends  the  active  contract,  anonymizes  roommate  data,  and  refreshes  the  apartment  list  after  success.  
 
3.6.6.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC34  Use  Case  Name  Checkout  Apartment   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord,  Manager   
 204  |  Page  
--- PAGE 205 ---
 
Description  Landlord  or  Manager  performs  the  checkout  process  for  the  current  resident  owner  of  an  occupied  apartment.  The  system  removes  the  current  owner  assignment,  changes  the  apartment  status  to  “EMPTY”,  deactivates  the  old  owner  account  instead  of  deleting  it,  ends  the  active  contract,  and  anonymizes  sensitive  roommate  information  while  preserving  invoices,  payments,  and  audit-related  history.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  User  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  User  account  has  the  role  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER.  
PRE-03:  The  selected  apartment  already  exists  in  the  database.  
PRE-04:  The  selected  apartment  must  have  status  “OCCUPIED”.  
PRE-05:  The  selected  apartment  must  have  a  valid  current  owner.  
PRE-06:  The  selected  apartment  must  have  an  active  contract  linked  to  the  current  owner.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Landlord  or  Manager  opens  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  of  an  occupied  apartment  and  clicks  the  “Checkout”  or  “Checkout  Tenant”  button.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  selected  apartment  status  is  changed  from  “OCCUPIED”  to  “EMPTY”.  
POS-02:  The  current  owner  reference  is  removed  from  the  apartment.  
POS-03:  The  old  owner  account  status  is  changed  to  “INACTIVE”.  
POS-04:  The  active  contract  is  ended  and  marked  as  “EXPIRED”  or  “TERMINATED”.  
POS-05:  Sensitive  roommate  data  is  anonymized,  including  deleting  CCCD  image  files  and  masking  CCCD  numbers.  
POS-06:  Billing  history,  payment  history,  repair  ticket  history,  and  audit-related  records  are  preserved  in  the  system.  
POS-07:  The  Apartment  Management  screen  is  refreshed  with  the  updated  apartment  status.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Landlord  /  Manager   On  the  Apartment  Management  screen,  selects  an  apartment  with  status  ‘OCCUPIED’.   
 205  |  Page  
--- PAGE 206 ---
 
2  System  Displays  the  Apartment  Detail  screen,  including  current  owner  information,  roommate  list,  invoices,  and  repair  history.   
3  Landlord  /  Manager   Clicks  the  “Checkout”  or  “Checkout  Tenant”  button.   
4  System  Displays  a  confirmation  warning  popup  explaining  that  the  owner  account  will  be  deactivated,  the  active  contract  will  be  ended,  and  sensitive  roommate  data  will  be  anonymized.   
5  Landlord  /  Manager   Clicks  the  “Confirm”  button.   
6  System   Validates  that  the  selected  apartment  is  ‘OCCUPIED’  and  has  a  valid  current  owner.  
7  System   Validates  that  the  selected  apartment  has  an  active  contract  linked  to  the  current  owner.   
8  System   Starts  a  database  transaction  to  ensure  data  consistency  during  checkout.   
9  System   Removes  the  current  owner  reference  from  the  selected  apartment.   
10  System   Changes  the  apartment  status  from  ‘OCCUPIED’  to  ‘EMPTY’.   
11  System   Updates  the  old  owner  account  status  to  ‘INACTIVE’  instead  of  deleting  the  account.   
12  System   Updates  the  active  contract  status  to  “EXPIRED”  or  “TERMINATED”.   
13  System   Deletes  roommate  CCCD  images  from  cloud  storage  and  masks  sensitive  identity  numbers  in  the  database.   
14  System   Preserves  invoices,  payments,  repair  tickets,  and  historical  contract  records  for  audit  purposes.   
 206  |  Page  
--- PAGE 207 ---
 
15  System   Commits  the  transaction  after  all  checkout  operations  are  completed  successfully.   
16  System   Displays  a  checkout  success  message.   
17  System   Redirects  Landlord  or  Manager  back  to  the  Apartment  Management  or  Apartment  Detail  screen  and  refreshes  the  latest  apartment  information.   
 
Alternative  Flows  
AT1  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  apartment  is  not  “OCCUPIED”.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Only  occupied  apartments  can  be  checked  out.”   
6.2  System  Blocks  the  checkout  process  and  makes  no  data  changes.   
 
AT2  At  step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  apartment  has  no  valid  current  owner.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “No  current  owner  found  for  this  apartment.”   
6.2  System  Blocks  the  checkout  process  and  keeps  the  apartment  data  unchanged.   
 
AT3  At  step  7  of  the  main  flow,  if  no  active  contract  is  found.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
7.1  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “No  active  contract  found  for  this  apartment.”   
 207  |  Page  
--- PAGE 208 ---
 
7.2  System  Blocks  the  checkout  process  and  keeps  the  apartment  data  unchanged.   
 
AT4  At  step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  Landlord  or  Manager  clicks  “Cancel”.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Closes  the  confirmation  popup.   
5.2  System  Returns  to  the  Apartment  Detail  screen  without  making  any  changes.   
 
AT5  At  step  13  of  the  main  flow,  if  Cloudinary  image  deletion  fails.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
13.1  System  Records  the  failure  for  retry  or  cleanup  processing.   
13.2  System  Continues  masking  sensitive  identity  numbers  in  the  database  if  the  checkout  transaction  can  still  be  safely  completed.   
 
AT6  At  step  9,  10,  11,  or  12  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  fails  to  update  apartment,  user,  or  contract  data.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
9.1  System  Rolls  back  all  checkout  changes  to  maintain  data  consistency.   
9.2  System  Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Checkout  failed.  Please  try  again.”   
9.3  System  Keeps  the  apartment  status  as  “OCCUPIED”  and  keeps  the  owner  and  contract  unchanged.   
 
 208  |  Page  
--- PAGE 209 ---
 
AT7  At  step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  non-authorized  user  tries  to  access  the  Checkout  function.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  perform  this  action.”   
3.2  System  Blocks  access  to  the  checkout  confirmation  dialog.   
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  can  execute  the  checkout  process.   
BR-47  Apartment  Status  Constraints :  Checkout  is  strictly  limited  to  apartments  currently  marked  as  "OCCUPIED".   
BR-49  Data  Retention  Policy :  The  resident's  account  status  is  changed  to  INACTIVE  (not  hard-deleted)  to  preserve  historical  billing  and  ticket  references.   
BR-20  Data  Privacy  on  Checkout :  Sensitive  roommate  data  (CCCD  images,  full  ID  numbers)  must  be  anonymized  or  deleted  upon  the  termination  of  the  apartment  contract.   
BR-65  Transaction  Safety :  The  checkout  process  must  be  handled  as  a  single  ACID  transaction  to  prevent  partial  data  corruption.

3.6.7.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC36  Use  Case  Name  View  Staff  List  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  view  a  list  of  all  security  guards  and  maintenance  staff  along  with  their  current  employment  status.  
Precondition  PRE-01:Manager/Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  PRE-02:  Staff  accounts  (Security  Guard,  etc.)  exist  in  the  database.  
Trigger  TRG-01:Manager/Landlord  navigates  to  the  Staff  Management  module.  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  retrieves  and  displays  the  list  of  staff  accounts  and  their  statuses.  
 212  |  Page  
--- PAGE 213 ---
 
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager/Landlord  Navigates  to  Staff  Management.  
2  System  Retrieves  staff  accounts  
3  System   Displays  the  list  of  staff  and  their  statuses.  
4  System  Renders  the  Staff  Statistics  Summary  (total  staff,  active,  inactive,  and  open  assigned  tickets).  
5  Manager/Landlord  Enters  a  keyword  in  the  Search  Bar  to  find  staff  by  name  or  phone  number.  
6  System  Filters  the  staff  list  in  real  time  based  on  the  keyword  and  the  selected  Status  Filter  tab  (All  /  Active  /  Inactive).  
7  Manager Clicks  "Add  Staff"  to  open  the  Add  Staff  Account  form  (UC38).  
Alternative  flows  
AT1  Empty  Staff  List  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  no  staff  accounts  are  found  in  the  database:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System   Detects  zero  records  matching  the  staff  role  criteria.  
2.2  System   Displays  an  empty  state  message  (e.g.,  "No  staff  members  currently  registered.")  
AT2  Data  Retrieval  Failure  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  fails  to  fetch  the  staff  records  (e.g.,  network  timeout,  database  error):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  a  temporary  error  message  (e.g.,  "Failed  to  load  staff  list.  Please  try  again.")  
 213  |  Page  
--- PAGE 214 ---
 
2.2  System  Retains  the  user  on  the  current  screen  and  prompts  for  a  refresh  action.  
AT3  Search/Filter  No  Match:  At  Step  6,  if  no  staff  account  matches  the  entered  keyword  or  the  selected  status  filter:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Detects  zero  records  matching  the  current  search  keyword  and  status-filter  combination.  
6.2  System  Displays  a  "No  matching  staff  found."  message  while  keeping  the  Search  Bar  and  Status  Filter  controls  active.  
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Access  Control:  Access  to  the  Staff  Management  module  is  strictly  restricted  to  users  with  the  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  role.  
BR-02  Data  Scope:  The  system  must  only  retrieve  and  display  user  accounts  explicitly  designated  with  staff-related  roles,  specifically  SECURITY_GUARD,  JANITOR,  and  TECHNICIAN.  (This  excludes  RESIDENT  accounts).  
BR-03  Default  Sorting:  Upon  initial  load,  the  staff  list  must  be  sorted  by  a  logical  field  such  as  the  staff  member's  Full  Name  (alphabetically)  or  Account  Status  (e.g.,  Active  first).  
BR-04  Data  Masking:  Sensitive  user  data,  including  the  password_hash and  internal  id,  must  be  filtered  out  and  never  transmitted  to  the  client  for  display  in  this  list.

3.6.8.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC37  Use  Case  Name  View  Staff  Detail  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Manager  ,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager and  Landlord  to  view  the  detailed  profile,  contact  information,  and  assigned  permissions  of  a  specific  staff  member.  
 217  |  Page  
--- PAGE 218 ---
 
Precondition  PRE-01:  Manager/Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.   
PRE-02:  A  staff  account  exists  with  a  staff  role  (SECURITY_GUARD,  JANITOR,  or  TECHNICIAN).  
Trigger  TRG-01:  Manager/Landlord  clicks  on  a  staff  member's  name  from  the  Staff  List  screen  (UC36).   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  retrieves  and  displays  the  selected  staff  member's  detailed  profile  and  permissions.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager/Landlord  Clicks  on  a  staff  member's  name  from  the  Staff  List  (UC36).   
2  System  Retrieves  detailed  staff  information  using  the  selected  staff  member's  id.  
3  System   Displays  the  profile  details,  contact  information,  and  assigned  roles/permissions.  
4  System  Counts  and  displays  the  number  of  open  tickets  currently  assigned  to  the  staff  member.  
5  System  If  the  staff  member  has  open  assigned  tickets,  renders  the  Open  Assigned  Tickets  list  and  shows  the  "Reassign  Tickets  First"  button;  otherwise  hides  both  sections.  
6  Manager Clicks  "Edit  Profile"  to  open  the  Update  Staff  Account  screen  (UC39).  
Alternative  flows  
AT1  Staff  Member  Not  Found  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  staff  member  ID  does  not  exist  in  the  database  or  the  account  has  been  deleted:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
 218  |  Page  
--- PAGE 219 ---
 
2.1  System   Displays  an  error  message  (e.g.,  "Error:  Staff  account  not  found  or  access  denied.").  
2.2  System   Redirects  the  user  back  to  the  Staff  List  screen  (UC36).  
AT2  Data  Retrieval  Failure  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  fails  to  fetch  the  staff  records  (e.g.,  database  connection  error):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action

2.2  System  Retains  the  user  on  the  current  screen  and  allows  a  refresh  action.   
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Access  Control:  Access  to  the  Staff  Detail  view  (Screen  28)  is  strictly  restricted  to  users  with  the  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  role.  
BR-02  Data  Hiding:  Sensitive  user  data,  including  the  password_hash and  internal  security  identifiers,  must  be  strictly  filtered  out  and  never  displayed  on  the  UI.  
BR-03  View-Only  Enforcement:  All  displayed  data  fields  on  this  screen  must  be  read-only,  with  modifications  explicitly  requiring  navigation  to  the  Update  Staff  Account  screen  (UC39).  
BR-04  Data  Scope:  The  system  must  only  retrieve  user  records  that  possess  a  staff-related  role  (e.g.,  SECURITY_GUARD)  for  this  detail  view.

3.6.9.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC38  Use  Case  Name  Create  Staff  Account  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  create  a  new  staff  account  by  entering  the  required  details  and  setting  an  initial  password  and  assigning  a  role.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  Manager/Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  PRE-02:  The  Phone  Number  to  be  used  is  unique  and  not  already  registered  in  the  system.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Manager/Landlord  clicks  "Add  Staff"  from  the  Manage  Staff  screen  (UC36).  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  user  record  is  saved  in  the  database  with  the  assigned  role  and  a  default  password  hash.  POS-02:  Account  status  is  set  to  'ACTIVE'.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
 222  |  Page  
--- PAGE 223 ---
 
1  Manager/Landlord  Navigates  to  the  Create  Staff  screen  and  inputs  Full  Name,  Phone  Number,  Initial  Password,  Confirm  Password,  and  selects  a  Role.   
2  Manager/Landlord  Clicks  the  "Save  Account"  button.  
3  System  Validates  all  mandatory  fields,  ensures  the  Phone  Number  is  unique  (BR-02/BR-09),  and  confirms  passwords  match.   
4  System  Hashes  the  Initial  Password  (BR-03)  and  creates  a  new  user  record   
5  System  Displays  a  success  message  and  redirects  to  the  Manage  Staff  screen  (UC36).   
Alternative  flows  
AT1  Phone  Number  Already  Exists  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  entered  Phone  Number  is  already  associated  with  an  existing  user  account  (Resident,  Manager ,  or  Staff):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System   Displays  an  error  message:  "Phone  number  already  exists.  Please  enter  a  different  number."  (AT2  under  UC05)  
3.2  System   Blocks  the  account  creation  process  and  retains  the  user  on  the  current  screen  until  the  Phone  Number  is  corrected.  
AT2  Password  Mismatch  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  Initial  Password  and  Confirm  Password  do  not  match:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  an  inline  error  message:  "Passwords  do  not  match."  (AT3  under  UC03)   
3.2  System  Blocks  the  account  creation  process.   
AT3  Mandatory  Field  Missing  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  mandatory  field  (Full  Name,  Phone,  Password,  or  Role)  is  left  empty:  
 223  |  Page  
--- PAGE 224 ---
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error  message  (e.g.,  "Tr ườ ng  b ắ t  bu ộ c  không  đượ c  để  tr ố ng."  or  "Field  cannot  be  empty.")  (AT1  under  UC05)    
3.2  System  Blocks  the  account  creation  process.   
AT4  At  any  step,  if  the  Manager  clicks  "Cancel"  after  entering  data  into  the  form:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System  Displays  a  confirmation  dialog  warning  that  unsaved  data  will  be  lost.  
1.2  Manager  Confirms;  the  system  discards  the  data  and  returns  to  the  Staff  Management  screen  without  creating  an  account.  
AT5  At  Step  4,  if  a  database  error  occurs  while  saving  the  new  user  record:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Aborts  creation  without  persisting  a  partial  record  and  displays:  "Unable  to  create  account.  Please  try  again."  
4.2  System  Retains  the  entered  form  data  so  the  Manager  can  retry.  
AT6  At  Step  1,  if  the  logged-in  user  does  not  have  permission  to  create  staff  accounts:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System  Blocks  access  to  the  Add  Staff  form  and  displays  an  authorization  error.  
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Role  Assignment:  The  system  must  ensure  that  the  new  account  is  assigned  one  of  the  designated  staff  roles  (```SECURITY_GUARD```,  etc.)  and  the  status  is  set  to  ACTIVE.  
BR-02  Uniqueness:  The  phone_number  field  used  as  the  login  username  must  be  unique  across  all  existing  user  records  in  the  database  (BR-02  under  UC01).  
 224  |  Page  
--- PAGE 225 ---
 
BR-03  Password  Security:  The  initial  password  must  be  one-way  encrypted  using  the  bcrypt  algorithm  before  being  saved  to  the  database  (BR-03  under  UC01).  
BR-04  First  Login  Policy:  Upon  the  new  staff  member's  first  successful  login,  the  system  must  automatically  redirect  them  to  the  Change  Password  flow  (implied  by  BR-01  under  UC01).

3.6.10.3  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC39  Use  Case  Name  Update  Staff  Account  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  modify  a  staff  member's  personal  information,  contact  details  (phone  number),  or  assigned  system  roles/permissions.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  Manager/Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  PRE-02:  The  target  staff  account  exists  and  is  selected  from  the  Staff  Detail  screen  (UC37).   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Manager/Landlord  clicks  the  "Edit  Profile"  button  on  the  Staff  Detail  screen  (UC37).  
 227  |  Page  
--- PAGE 228 ---
 
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  staff  member's  profile  details  (full_name,  phone,  roles,  avatar_url)  are  successfully  updated  in  the  database.  POS-02:  A  success  message  is  displayed.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager/Landlord  Clicks  "Edit  Profile"  on  the  Staff  Detail  screen.  
2  System  Displays  the  editable  profile  data  for  the  selected  staff  member.  
3  Manager/Landlord  Modifies  one  or  more  fields  (Name,  Phone  Number,  Roles,  Avatar).  
4  Manager/Landlord  Clicks  the  "Save  Changes"  button.  
5  System  Validates  input  data  (mandatory  fields,  unique  phone  number  -  BR-02).   
6  System  Updates  the  staff  record  in  the  ```  users  ```  table  with  the  new  data.   
7  System  Displays  a  success  message  and  redirects  back  to  the  Staff  Detail  view  (UC37).   
Alternative  flows  
AT1  Phone  Number  Already  Exists  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  entered  Phone  Number  is  already  associated  with  another  active  user  account  (violating  BR-02):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System   Displays  an  error  message:  "Phone  number  already  exists.  Please  enter  a  different  number."  
5.2  System   Blocks  the  update  process  and  retains  the  user  on  the  current  screen  until  the  Phone  Number  is  corrected.  
AT2  Role  Selection  Failure  At  Step  5  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  selected  role  is  invalid  or  mandatory  fields  are  left  empty:  
 228  |  Page  
--- PAGE 229 ---
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System  Displays  an  inline  validation  error  message  (e.g.,  "Tr ườ ng  b ắ t  bu ộ c  không  đượ c  để  tr ố ng."  or  "Please  select  a  valid  role.").   
5.2  System  Blocks  the  update  process.   
AT3  Avatar  Upload  Failure  At  Step  6  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  uploaded  Avatar  image  fails  (e.g.,  image  compression  fails  or  size  limit  exceeded  -  implied  by  BR-10  of  UC05):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
6.1  System  Displays  an  error  message  (e.g.,  "Image  size  exceeds  the  limit.  Please  choose  a  smaller  file.").   
6.2  System  Continues  saving  non-image  fields  (Name,  Phone,  Roles)  but  retains  the  existing  avatar  URL  (or  null)  for  the  image  field.   
AT4  Role  Change  With  Active  Assignments:  At  Step  5,  if  the  Manager /Landlord  changes  a  staff  member's  role  while  that  member  still  has  tickets  in  status  ASSIGNED  or  IN_PROGRESS:  
Sub  step  Actor  
Action  
5.1  System  
Warns  that  changing  the  role  may  orphan  tickets  assigned  under  the  previous  role  type  and  asks  for  conﬁrmation.  
5.2  Manager /Landlord  
Either  conﬁrms  (the  role  is  changed  and  affected  tickets  are  ﬂagged  for  reassignment)  or  cancels  the  role  change.  
AT5  Database  Error  On  Save:  At  Step  6,  if  a  database  error  occurs  while  updating  the  staff  record:  
Sub  step  Actor  
Action  
6.1  System  
Aborts  the  update  without  persisting  partial  changes  and  displays:  "Unable  to  save  changes.  Please  try  again."  
6.2  System  
Retains  the  entered  values  on  the  form  so  the  update  can  be  retried.  
AT6  Cancel  With  Unsaved  Changes:  At  any  step,  if  the  Manager /Landlord  leaves  the  screen  after  modifying  fields  without  saving:  
Sub  step  Actor  
Action  
 229  |  Page  
--- PAGE 230 ---
 
1.1  System  
Displays  a  conﬁrmation  dialog  warning  that  unsaved  changes  will  be  lost  before  leaving  the  screen.  
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Access  Control:  Only  users  with  the  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  role  are  authorized  to  view  and  modify  staff  accounts.  
BR-02  Uniqueness:  The  ```  phone_number  ```  field  used  as  the  login  username  must  remain  unique  across  all  existing  user  records  in  the  database  (BR-09  under  UC05).1 
BR-03  Password  Reset  Exclusion:  This  screen  is  for  profile  updates  only.  Changing  the  staff  member's  password  must  be  handled  by  a  dedicated,  secure  flow  (e.g.,  a  function  similar  to  Forgot  Password  UC03).  
BR-04  Audit  Logging:  Any  change  made  to  critical  fields  (Phone  Number,  Roles,  or  Status)  must  be  logged  in  the  system's  audit  trail,  recording  the  Manager /Landlord  ID,  the  timestamp,  and  the  original  and  new  values.

4  Confirm  Deactivation  Button  
Button  Yes  -  Executes  the  database  update  to  change  the  account  status  to  'INACTIVE'.  
5  Cancel  Button  Button  -  -  Closes  the  modal  and  cancels  the  deactivation  process,  returning  the  user  to  the  Staff  Detail  screen.  
3.6.11.2  Use  Case  Description  
 231  |  Page  
--- PAGE 232 ---
 
Use  Case  ID  UC40  Use  Case  Name  Deactivate  Staff  Account  
Author  Đ ào  Chí  C ườ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Manager,  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Manager  and  Landlord  to  deactivate  the  account  of  a  resigned  staff  member  to  prevent  further  system  access.  
Precondition  PRE-01:  Manager/Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
 PRE-02:  The  target  staff  account  currently  has  the  status  'ACTIVE'.  PRE-03:  The  target  staff  account  has  no  tickets  in  status  ASSIGNED  or  IN_PROGRESS,  in  accordance  with  BR-50. 
Trigger  TRG-01:  Manager/Landlord  clicks  "Deactivate"  on  a  staff  record  from  the  Staff  Detail  screen  (UC37).  
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  staff  member's  account  status  is  permanently  changed  to  'INACTIVE'  in  the  database.  POS-02:  The  staff  member  is  immediately  prevented  from  logging  into  the  system.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Manager/Landlord  Clicks  "Deactivate"  on  a  staff  record.  
2  System  Displays  the  confirmation  dialog.  
3  Manager/Landlord  Confirms  the  action.  
4  System  Changes  the  staff  account  status  to  'INACTIVE'  and  immediately  invalidates  any  active  JWT  sessions  for  that  account,  forcing  logout  on  all  devices. 
5  System  Displays  a  success  message  and  redirects/returns  to  the  Staff  Detail  screen  (UC37)  showing  the  updated  status.   
Alternative  flows  
 232  |  Page  
--- PAGE 233 ---
 
AT1  Cancellation  by  User  At  Step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  Manager/Landlord  cancels  the  action:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  Manager/Landlord  
Clicks  the  "Cancel"  button.  
3.2  System   Closes  the  dialog,  and  the  staff  record  status  remains  'ACTIVE'.  
AT2  Account  Already  Inactive  At  Step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  system  checks  the  account  status  and  finds  it  is  already  'INACTIVE':  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Displays  an  informational  message:  "This  account  is  already  inactive  and  cannot  be  deactivated  again."   
2.2  System  Blocks  the  "Confirm  Deactivation"  button  and  remains  on  the  screen.  Note:  reactivating  a  deactivated  account  is  not  currently  supported  by  any  defined  use  case.  
AT3  Database  Update  Failure  At  Step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  database  fails  to  update  the  user  status  (e.g.,  connection  error):  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Displays  an  error  message:  "Failed  to  deactivate  account  due  to  a  system  error.  Please  try  again."   
4.2  System  Retains  the  original  status  ('ACTIVE')  and  waits  for  the  Manager  to  retry  the  action.   
AT4  Open  Assigned  Tickets  Exist:  At  Step  2,  if  the  target  staff  member  still  has  tickets  in  status  ASSIGNED  or  IN_PROGRESS,  per  BR-50:  
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System  Blocks  the  deactivation,  informs  the  Manager/Landlord  that  open  tickets  must  be  reassigned  first,  and  surfaces  the  "Reassign  Tickets  First"  action  from  the  Staff  Detail  screen  (UC37).  
 233  |  Page  
--- PAGE 234 ---
 
2.2  Manager /Landlord  
Reassigns  the  open  tickets,  then  re-attempts  the  deactivation.  
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Access  Control:  The  deactivation  function  is  strictly  restricted  to  users  possessing  the  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  role.  
BR-02  Inactivation:  Upon  successful  execution,  the  staff  account's  status field  in  the  users table  must  be  updated  to  the  literal  value  'INACTIVE'.1 
BR-03  Login  Prohibition:  Accounts  flagged  as  'INACTIVE'  must  be  prevented  from  completing  the  login  process  (UC01),  ensuring  immediate  revocation  of  access  upon  deactivation.  (Implied  by  BR-04  in  UC01  which  states  'Inactive  Account  accounts  with  status  INACTIVE  are  not  permitted  to  log  into  the  system').1 
BR-04  Audit  Logging:  The  system  must  record  the  Manager /Landlord  ID,  the  staff  account  ID,  the  timestamp,  and  the  action  taken  ('Deactivate')  in  a  dedicated  audit  log  table  to  maintain  an  immutable  history  of  staff  management  actions.  (Implied  by  BR-04  in  UC39  and  BR-03  in  UC12  related  to  auditing  critical  changes).

3.6.12.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC35  Use  Case  Name  Dashboard  &  Statistics  Report   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord,  Manager   
Description  Landlord  or  Manager  views  statistical  reports  about  apartment  operations,  including  monthly  revenue  collection  status,  unpaid  invoices,  unresolved  repair  tickets,  and  recent  management  activities.  This  use  case  supports  high-level  monitoring  of  financial  and  operational  performance  without  modifying  business  data.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  User  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  User  account  has  the  role  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER.  
PRE-03:  Invoice,  payment,  apartment,  and  repair  ticket  data  are  available  in  the  database.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Landlord  or  Manager  accesses  the  Dashboard  screen  from  the  main  menu  after  login.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  Dashboard  statistics  and  charts  are  displayed  successfully.  
POS-02:  Dashboard  data  is  refreshed  according  to  the  selected  reporting  month.  
POS-03:  No  business  data  is  modified  by  this  use  case.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Landlord  /  Manager   Opens  the  Dashboard  screen.   
 238  |  Page  
--- PAGE 239 ---
 
2  System  Requests  dashboard  data  from  the  backend.   
3  System  Calculates  the  monthly  revenue  collection  status,  including  paid  bills,  unpaid  bills,  and  total  collected  amount.   
4  System  Calculates  the  number  of  unresolved  repair  tickets,  such  as  tickets  with  status  “PENDING”,  “ASSIGNED”,  or  “PROCESSING”.   
5  System  Retrieves  recent  operational  activities,  such  as  payment  received,  ticket  assigned,  apartment  check-in,  apartment  checkout,  or  news  posted.   
6  System  Displays  summary  cards  for  revenue,  unpaid  bills,  and  unresolved  tickets.   
7  System   Displays  visual  charts  for  revenue  collection  and  ticket  status  overview.   
8  System   Displays  quick  action  shortcuts  based  on  the  logged-in  user’s  role  and  permission.   
9  Landlord  /  Manager   Selects  a  different  month  or  filter  option  if  available.   
10  System   Refreshes  the  dashboard  data  based  on  the  selected  filter.   
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  3  of  the  main  flow,  if  there  is  no  invoice  or  payment  data  for  the  selected  month.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System   Displays  zero  values  for  revenue-related  statistics.   
3.2  System   Displays  an  empty  chart  or  a  message  such  as  “No  billing  data  available  for  this  month.”   
 239  |  Page  
--- PAGE 240 ---
 
 
AT2  At  step  4  of  the  main  flow,  if  there  are  no  unresolved  repair  tickets:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System   Displays  the  unresolved  ticket  count  as  0.   
4.2  System   Displays  a  normal  dashboard  state  without  an  error  message.   
 
AT3  At  step  2  of  the  main  flow,  if  dashboard  data  cannot  be  loaded  because  of  a  network  or  server  error:   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System   Displays  an  error  message,  such  as  “Unable  to  load  dashboard  data.”   
2.2  System   Provides  a  “Retry”  option  for  the  user.   
 
AT4  At  step  9  of  the  main  flow,  if  the  user  selects  an  invalid  or  unsupported  date  range.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
9.1  System   Displays  a  validation  message,  such  as  “Invalid  date  range.”   
9.2  System   Keeps  the  previous  dashboard  data  unchanged.   
 
AT5  At  step  1  of  the  main  flow,  if  a  non-authorized  user  tries  to  access  the  Dashboard  &  Statistics  Report  screen.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System   Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  access  this  function.”   
 240  |  Page  
--- PAGE 241 ---
 
1.2  System   Blocks  access  to  the  dashboard  screen.   
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  Content  Authorization:  Only  users  with  role  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  are  allowed  to  access  the  Dashboard  &  Statistics  Report  screen.  Non-authorized  users  must  not  be  allowed  to  access  this  function.  
BR-02  Read-Only  Statistics:  Dashboard  statistics  must  be  read-only.  Viewing  dashboard  data  must  not  directly  modify  invoices,  payments,  users,  apartments,  contracts,  repair  tickets,  tasks,  news,  or  notifications.  
BR-03  Revenue  Calculation:  Total  revenue  and  collected  revenue  must  be  calculated  from  invoice  and  payment  records  in  the  selected  reporting  period.  Collected  revenue  must  only  include  invoices  or  payments  with  successful  paid  status.  
BR-04  Unpaid  Invoice  Calculation:  Unpaid  invoice  count  must  only  include  invoices  with  status  “UNPAID”  in  the  selected  reporting  period.  
BR-05  Ticket  Statistics  Calculation:  Unresolved  ticket  statistics  must  include  repair  tickets  that  still  require  operational  handling,  such  as  “PENDING”,  “ASSIGNED”,  or  “PROCESSING”.  Tickets  with  status  “RESOLVED”  or  “CANCELLED”  must  not  be  counted  as  unresolved  tickets.  
BR-06  Role-Based  Quick  Actions:  Quick  action  buttons  must  be  displayed  according  to  the  logged-in  user’s  role  and  permission.  A  user  must  not  see  or  execute  actions  that  are  not  allowed  for  their  role.  
BR-07  Reporting  Month  Filter:  When  a  reporting  month  is  selected,  all  dashboard  cards  and  charts  must  be  recalculated  based  on  that  selected  month.  
BR-08  Data  Consistency:  Dashboard  values  must  reflect  the  latest  available  data  from  invoices,  payments,  repair  tickets,  apartments,  and  tasks  at  the  time  the  dashboard  is  loaded  or  refreshed.  
BR-09  Empty  Data  Handling:  If  no  data  exists  for  the  selected  month,  the  system  must  display  zero  values  or  empty-state  messages  instead  of  showing  errors.

3.6.13.2  Use  Case  Description  
 244  |  Page  
--- PAGE 245 ---
 
Use  Case  ID  UC41  Use  Case  Name  View  Manager  List   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord  
Description  Allows  the  Landlord  to  view  a  centralized  list  of  all  current  Manager  accounts  in  the  system,  including  their  active  or  inactive  status,  basic  contact  information,  and  recent  management  activity  summary.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  The  logged-in  account  has  the  LANDLORD  role.  
PRE-03:  Manager  account  records  exist  in  the  database.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Landlord  navigates  to  the  Manager  Management  module  from  the  Dashboard  or  Statistics  &  Administration  menu.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  retrieves  and  displays  the  list  of  Manager  accounts.  
POS-02:  Manager  accounts  are  displayed  according  to  the  selected  search  keyword  or  status  filter.  
POS-03:  Landlord  can  select  an  Manager  account  to  view  detailed  profile  and  management  history.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Landlord   Navigates  to  Manager  Management.   
2  System   Retrieves  Manager  account  records  from  the  database,  including  name,  contact  information,  account  status,  and  latest  activity  summary.   
3  System   Displays  the  Manager  Management  screen  with  summary  cards,  search  bar,  status  filters,  and  Manager  account  list.   
 245  |  Page  
--- PAGE 246 ---
 
4  Landlord   Searches  by  manager  name,  phone  number,  or  email,  or  filters  Manager  accounts  by  status  such  as  All,  Active,  or  Inactive.   
5  System   Queries  matching  Manager  accounts  based  on  the  entered  keyword  or  selected  filter.   
6  System   Displays  the  matching  Manager  account  list.   
7  Landlord   Reviews  summarized  Manager  information  such  as  name,  contact  information,  status,  and  latest  management  activity.   
8  Landlord   Selects  an  Manager  account  from  the  list  to  view  more  detailed  information.   
9  System   Redirects  the  Landlord  to  the  Manager  Detail  screen  for  the  selected  Manager  account.   
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  2,  if  no  Manager  account  exists  in  the  database.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System   System  displays  an  empty-state  message  such  as  “No  manager  account  found.”   
 
AT2  At  step  5,  if  no  Manager  account  matches  the  search  keyword  or  selected  filter.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
5.1  System   System  displays  “No  matching  manager  found”.  
5.2  System   Keeps  the  search  bar  and  filters  available.   
 
AT3  At  step  2,  if  the  system  cannot  load  Manager  data  due  to  server  or  database  error.   
 246  |  Page  
--- PAGE 247 ---
 
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System   System  displays  an  error  message  such  as  “Unable  to  load  manager  list.  Please  try  again.”.   
2.2  System   Provides  a  retry  option.  
 
AT4  At  step  1,  if  the  logged-in  user  is  not  Landlord.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System   Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  access  this  function.”   
1.2  System   Blocks  access  to  Manager  Management.   
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-60   Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  users  with  the  role  LANDLORD  or  MANAGER  are  allowed  to  access  and  view  the  Manager  Management  list.   
BR-61   UI  Display  &  Indicators :  The  Manager  list  must  dynamically  display  essential  system  information  (full  name,  contact  details,  account  status)  and  reflect  the  latest  account  status  stored  in  the  database.   
BR-62   UI  Navigation  &  Read-Only  Control :  Searching  Manager  accounts  (by  name,  phone,  email),  filtering  by  status,  or  selecting  an  Manager  to  view  details  are  strictly  read-only  and  must  not  modify  any  data.   
BR-49   Data  Retention  Policy :  Inactive  Manager  accounts  must  not  be  hard-deleted  from  the  system;  they  must  remain  visible  and  stored  in  the  database  for  audit  and  historical  tracking  purposes.

3.6.14.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC42  Use  Case  Name  View  Manager  Detail   
Author  V ũ  H ả i  Nam  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord   
Description  Allows  the  Landlord  to  view  the  detailed  profile,  contact  information,  account  status,  assigned  permissions,  and  management  history  of  a  specific  Manager  account.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  Landlord  has  successfully  logged  into  the  system.  
PRE-02:  The  logged-in  account  has  the  LANDLORD  role.  
PRE-03:  The  selected  Manager  account  exists  in  the  database.  
PRE-04:  Landlord  has  accessed  the  Manager  Management  screen.   
Trigger  TRG-01:  Landlord  selects  an  Manager  account  from  the  Manager  Management  screen.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  system  successfully  displays  the  selected  Manager’s  detailed  profile.  
POS-02:  The  Landlord  can  review  the  Manager’s  contact  information,  account  status,  permissions,  and  management  history.  
POS-03:  No  Manager  data  is  modified  by  viewing  the  detail  screen.   
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  Landlord   Clicks  on  an  Manager  account  from  the  Manager  Management  screen.   
 251  |  Page  
--- PAGE 252 ---
 
2  System   Retrieves  detailed  Manager  information  from  the  database,  including  profile,  contact  information,  account  status,  assigned  permissions,  and  management  history.   
3  System   Displays  the  Manager  Detail  screen  with  the  selected  Manager’s  profile  and  history.   
4  Landlord   Reviews  the  Manager’s  personal  information,  contact  details,  account  status,  and  access  permissions.   
5  Landlord   Reviews  the  Manager’s  management  history,  including  recent  actions  or  handled  records.   
6  Landlord   Returns  to  the  Manager  Management  screen  or  selects  an  available  action  such  as  Edit  Manager  or  Deactivate  Account  if  needed.   
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  2,  if  the  selected  Manager  account  no  longer  exists.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System   System  displays  “Manager   account  not  found”.  
2.2  System   Redirects  the  Landlord  back  to  the  Manager  Management  screen.   
 
AT2  At  step  2,  if  the  selected  Manager  has  no  recorded  management  activity.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System   System  displays  the  Manager  profile  normally.  
2.2  System   Shows  “No  management  history  available”  in  the  history  section.   
 
 252  |  Page  
--- PAGE 253 ---
 
AT3  At  step  2,  if  the  system  cannot  retrieve  Manager  details  due  to  server  or  database  error.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
2.1  System   System  displays  “Unable  to  load  manager  details.  Please  try  again.”.  
2.2  System   Provides  a  “Retry”  option  for  the  user.   
 
AT4  At  step  3,  if  the  selected  Manager  account  is  inactive.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System   System  displays  the  profile  and  history  in  read-only  mode  with  an  Inactive  status  badge.   
 
AT5  At  step  1,  if  the  logged-in  user  is  not  Landlord.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  System   Displays  an  access  denied  message,  such  as  “You  do  not  have  permission  to  access  this  function.”   
1.2  System   Blocks  access  to  the  Manager  Detail  screen.   
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access :  Only  users  with  authorized  roles  (LANDLORD,  MANAGER)  can  access  and  view  the  detailed  profile  of  an  Manager  account.   
BR-62  UI  Navigation  &  Read-Only  Control :  Viewing  the  Manager  detailed  profile  screen  functions  strictly  as  a  read-only  process  and  triggers  no  data  updates.   
 253  |  Page  
--- PAGE 254 ---
 
BR-08   Profile  View  Data  Masking :  Sensitive  identity  or  configuration  credentials  within  the  Manager  detail  profile  must  be  properly  masked  or  hidden  depending  on  the  viewing  user's  specific  permission  level.

3.6.15.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC43  Use  Case  Name  Create  Manager  Account  
Author  Võ  Chi ế n  Th ắ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord   
Description  Allows  the  Landlord  to  create  and  provision  a  new  Manager  account  to  help  manage  the  building's  operations.   
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  Landlord  has  successfully  authenticated  and  logged  into  the  Manager  Dashboard.  
PRE-02:  The  Landlord  is  currently  on  the  "Manager  Management  Screen".  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  Landlord  clicks  the  "Create  Manager"  button  on  the  interface.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  A  new  Manager  account  is  successfully  saved  in  the  PostgreSQL  database  with  an  initial  default  password.  
POS-02:  The  new  Manager  record  appears  on  the  Manager  Management  list.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
 256  |  Page  
--- PAGE 257 ---
 
1  Landlord  Enters  the  new  Manager's  details  (e.g.,  Full  Name,  Phone  Number,  Email,  and  specific  system  permissions)  into  the  creation  form.  
2  Landlord  Clicks  the  "Save"  or  "Create"  button.  
3  Backend  Validates  the  input  data,  ensuring  the  phone  number  is  unique  across  the  system  (BR-02).  
4  Backend  Generates  the  account  with  a  default  password,  encrypts  the  password  (BR-03),  and  commits  the  new  user  record  to  the  database.  
5  System  Refreshes  the  Manager  Management  Screen  and  displays  a  success  toast  notification:  "Manager  account  created  successfully."  
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  3,  the  entered  phone  number  already  exists  in  the  system.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
3.1  System  Detects  the  duplicate  entry  and  rejects  the  creation  request.  
3.2  System  Keeps  the  form  open,  highlights  the  Phone  Number  field,  and  displays  an  error  message:  "This  phone  number  is  already  registered  to  another  account."  
3.3  Landlord  Enters  a  different  phone  number  and  returns  to  step  2.  
  257  |  Page  
--- PAGE 258 ---
 
Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-01  The  system  does  not  allow  users  to  freely  register.  Accounts  are  exclusively  provisioned  by  the  Management  Board  (Manager/Landlord)  with  a  default  password.  This  rule  applies  to  Resident  Owner  accounts  created  during  apartment  check-in  and  staff  accounts  created  during  staff  management.  Users  must  change  their  default  password  upon  their  first  login.  
BR-02  The  phone  number  used  as  the  login  username  must  be  unique  across  all  user  accounts  in  the  entire  system,  even  when  performing  a  profile  update.  
BR-03  User  passwords  must  be  one-way  encrypted  using  the  bcrypt  algorithm  before  being  saved  to  the  PostgreSQL  database.  
BR-57  Only  system  accounts  explicitly  assigned  the  highest  administrative  role  (e.g.,  LANDLORD)  hold  the  permissions  required  to  access  the  Manager  Management  Screen  to  create,  update,  or  deactivate  other  Manager  accounts.

3.6.16.2  Use  Case  Description  
Use  Case  ID  UC44  Use  Case  Name  Update  Manager  Account  
Author  Võ  Chi ế n  Th ắ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord   
Description  Allows  the  Landlord  to  modify  the  profile  details,  contact  information,  or  access  permissions  of  an  existing  Manager  account.    
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  Landlord  is  logged  in  and  accessing  the  "Manager  Management  Screen".  
PRE-02:  At  least  one  existing  Manager  account  is  present  in  the  system.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  Landlord  selects  an  existing  Manager  from  the  list  and  clicks  the  "Edit"  or  "Update"  button.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  selected  Manager's  details  are  successfully  updated  in  the  database.  
POS-02:  The  updated  information  is  immediately  reflected  on  the  Manager  Management  Screen.  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Displays  an  update  form  pre-filled  with  the  selected  Manager's  current  information.  
 261  |  Page  
--- PAGE 262 ---
 
2  Landlord  Modifies  the  necessary  fields  (e.g.,  updating  the  email,  name,  or  toggling  specific  permissions).  
3  Landlord  Clicks  the  "Update"  button  to  confirm  changes.  
4  Backend  Validates  the  payload  data  for  the  specific  Manager  record.  
5  System  Closes  the  form,  refreshes  the  data  list,  and  displays  a  success  notification:  "Manager  account  updated  successfully."  
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  4,  the  entered  data  does  not  meet  validation  rules   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
4.1  System  Rejects  the  update  payload.  
4.2  System  Highlights  the  invalid  field(s)  with  an  error  message  (e.g.,  "Please  enter  a  valid  email  address.").  
4.3  Landlord  Corrects  the  formatting  and  returns  to  step  3.  
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-02  The  phone  number  used  as  the  login  username  must  be  unique  across  all  user  accounts  in  the  entire  system,  even  when  performing  a  profile  update.  
 262  |  Page  
--- PAGE 263 ---
 
BR-11  For  security  purposes,  any  modification  to  a  user's  phone  number  or  key  Manageristrative  actions  (such  as  approvals/rejections)  must  be  logged  in  the  system's  audit  trail,  recording  the  actor's  ID,  timestamp,  and  details  of  the  action  taken.  
BR-57  Only  system  accounts  explicitly  assigned  the  highest  administrative  role  (e.g.,  LANDLORD  or  SUPER_ADMIN)  hold  the  permissions  required  to  access  the  Manager  Management  Screen  to  create,  update,  or  deactivate  other  Manager  accounts.

9  Cancel  Button  Button  Yes  -  A  secondary  action  button  that  dismisses  the  confirmation  dialog  and  returns  the  Landlord  to  the  previous  screen  without  making  any  changes.  
 
3.6.17.2  Use  Case  Description  
 265  |  Page  
--- PAGE 266 ---
 
Use  Case  ID  UC45  Use  Case  Name  Deactivate  Manager  Account  
Author  Võ  Chi ế n  Th ắ ng  Version  1.0  
Date  02/06/2026  Actor  Landlord   
Description  Allows  the  Landlord  to  safely  disable  an  Manager's  access  to  the  system  without  hard-deleting  their  historical  data  or  audit  trails.    
Precondition  PRE-01:  The  Landlord  is  on  the  "Manager  Management  Screen".  
PRE-02:  The  target  Manager  account  is  currently  in  an  ACTIVE  state.  
Trigger  TRG-01:  The  Landlord  clicks  the  "Deactivate"  (or  Block)  button  associated  with  a  specific  Manager  account.   
Post-Condition  POS-01:  The  target  Manager's  status  is  updated  to  INACTIVE  in  the  database.  
POS-02:  The  target  Manager  can  no  longer  log  into  the  system  (BR-04).  
Main  flows  
Step  Actor  Action  
1  System  Prompts  a  warning  confirmation  dialog:  "Are  you  sure  you  want  to  deactivate  this  Manager  account?  They  will  lose  all  access  to  the  system."  
2  Landlord  Clicks  "Confirm"  to  proceed  with  the  deactivation.  
3  Backend  Updates  the  target  user's  record,  changing  the  account  status  from  ACTIVE  to  INACTIVE.  
 266  |  Page  
--- PAGE 267 ---
 
4  Backend  Instantly  invalidates  any  active  JWT  sessions  associated  with  the  deactivated  Manager  (BR-05).  
5  System  Updates  the  UI  to  show  the  Manager's  status  as  "Inactive"  and  displays  a  success  toast  message.  
 Alternative  flows  
AT1  At  step  1,  the  Landlord  decides  not  to  proceed.   
Sub  step  Actor  Action  
1.1  Landlord  Clicks  "Cancel"  or  clicks  outside  the  confirmation  dialog.  
1.2  System  Closes  the  dialog  without  making  any  changes  to  the  database.  The  flow  terminates.  
 Business  Rules  
#  Rule  Description  
BR-04  Only  user  accounts  with  ACTIVE  status  are  permitted  to  log  in  or  request  a  Password  Reset  OTP.  Accounts  with  status  INACTIVE  (e.g.,  following  a  checkout  process  or  staff  deactivation)  are  strictly  blocked  from  logging  into  the  system.  
BR-05  The  logout  process  must  explicitly  invalidate  the  user's  active  JSON  Web  Token  (JWT)  on  the  server  side  to  prevent  unauthorized  reuse.  
BR-11  For  security  purposes,  any  modification  to  a  user's  phone  number  or  key  administrative  actions  (such  as  approvals/rejections)  must  be  logged  in  the  system's  audit  trail,  recording  the  actor's  ID,  timestamp,  and  details  of  the  action  taken.  
 267  |  Page  
--- PAGE 268 ---
 
BR-58  The  system  must  strictly  prevent  the  currently  logged-in  Landlord/Super  Admin  from  deactivating  their  own  account.  The  deactivation  button/action  must  be  disabled  or  hidden  for  the  active  user's  own  row  to  avoid  locking  the  management  system  entirely. 
BR-59  When  an  Manager  account  is  deactivated  (status  set  to  INACTIVE),  all  historical  actions,  tickets  handled,  audit  logs,  and  system  changes  previously  performed  by  that  account  must  remain  fully  intact  and  visible  in  the  database.  Hard  deletion  is  strictly  prohibited. 
 4.  Non-Functional  Requirements  4.1  External  Interfaces  
●  The  Backend  system  (Next.js)  communicates  with  the  PayOS  payment  gateway  via  standard  
HTTP
 
RESTful
 
APIs
 
to
 
process
 
real
 
financial
 
transactions.
 ●  The  mobile  application  interacts  with  Firebase  Cloud  Messaging  (FCM)  via  the  official  SDK  to  
receive
 
Push
 
Notifications.
 ●  The  system  uses  the  Pusher  SDK  to  establish  continuous  WebSocket  connections,  serving  the  
online
 
Live
 
Chat
 
feature.
 ●  The  mobile  application  uploads  files  directly  to  the  Cloudinary  server  via  API  to  retrieve  
HTTPS-standard
 
URLs
 
for
 
Database
 
storage.
 
4.2  Quality  Attributes  
4.2.1  Security:  
●  Transaction  Integrity  (PayOS):  Because  the  system  handles  real  financial  transactions,  the  
Webhook
 
endpoint
 
receiving
 
payment
 
results
 
from
 
PayOS
 
must
 
strictly
 
verify
 
the
 
data
 
signature
 
(Checksum/HMAC)
 
to
 
prevent
 
payment
 
spoofing
 
or
 
unauthorized
 
status
 
updates.
 ●  All  connections  from  the  Mobile  App  to  the  Backend  must  transmit  a  JWT  Token  in  the  HTTP  
Header.
 ●  User  passwords  must  be  one-way  encrypted  using  the  bcrypt  algorithm  before  being  saved  
to
 
the
 
PostgreSQL
 
database.
 ●  Apply  "Soft  Delete"  and  "Data  Anonymization"  principles  when  users  move  out  of  the  
building
 
to
 
comply
 
with
 
identity
 
data
 
(ID
 
Card)
 
protection
 
while
 
maintaining
 
an
 
intact
 
financial
 
audit
 
history.

guarantee
 
an
 
uptime
 
of
 
99.9%,
 
ensuring
 
that
 
residents
 
can
 
access
 
billing
 
information,
 
report
 
critical
 
issues,
 
or
 
communicate
 
via
 
Live
 
Chat
 
at
 
any
 
time
 
of
 
the
 
day.
 ●  Mean  Time  Between  Failures  (MTBF):  The  system  should  operate  stably  without  any  major  
crashes
 
or
 
service
 
disruptions
 
for
 
a
 
minimum
 
of
 
720
 
hours
 
(approximately
 
1
 
month).
 ●  Mean  Time  To  Repair  (MTTR):  In  the  event  of  a  critical  system  failure  or  database  downtime,  
the
 
maximum
 
allowable
 
time
 
to
 
restore
 
full
 
operational
 
status
 
is
 
2
 
hours.
 ●  Accuracy:  Absolute  precision  is  required  for  the  billing  module.  Financial  data  (total  amounts,

records)

access
 
to

(ID
