5.  Requirement  Appendix  5.1  Business  Rules   
ID  Rule  Definition  
BR-01  Account  Provisioning:  The  system  does  not  allow  users  to  freely  register.  Accounts  are  exclusively  provisioned  by  the  Management  Board  (Manager/Landlord)  with  a  default  password.  This  rule  applies  to  Resident  Owner  accounts  created  during  apartment  check-in  and  staff  accounts  created  during  staff  management.  Users  must  change  their  default  password  upon  their  first  login.  
BR-02  Phone  Number  Uniqueness:  The  phone  number  used  as  the  login  username  must  be  unique  across  all  user  accounts  in  the  entire  system,  even  when  performing  a  profile  update.  
BR-03  Password  Encryption:  User  passwords  must  be  one-way  encrypted  using  the  bcrypt  algorithm  before  being  saved  to  the  PostgreSQL  database.  
BR-04  Active  Account  Requirement:  Only  user  accounts  with  ACTIVE  status  are  permitted  to  log  in  or  request  a  Password  Reset  OTP.  Accounts  with  status  INACTIVE  (e.g.,  following  a  checkout  process  or  staff  deactivation)  are  strictly  blocked  from  logging  into  the  system.  
BR-05  Session  Invalidation:  The  logout  process  must  explicitly  invalidate  the  user's  active  JSON  Web  Token  (JWT)  on  the  server  side  to  prevent  unauthorized  reuse.  
BR-06  FCM  Token  Lifecycle  (FCM  Disassociation):  The  system  must  capture  and  store  the  device's  unique  FCM  Token  upon  successful  login  and  strictly  destroy  this  token  entry  in  the  database  upon  logout  to  prevent  cross-account  notification  leaks.  
BR-07  OTP  Expiration:  The  One-Time  Password  (OTP)  generated  by  the  system  (via  Firebase  Auth  SMS  Gateway)  must  be  valid  for  a  limited  time  (e.g.,  5  minutes)  to  ensure  security  and  prevent  replay  attacks.  
BR-08  Profile  View  Data  Masking:  The  profile  view  must  strictly  hide  sensitive  fields,  such  as  the  password_hash  and  internal  id.  If  a  Citizen  ID  (CCCD)  number  is  displayed,  it  must  be  partially  masked  (e.g.,  only  the  last  4  digits  visible)  for  security  and  privacy.  
 270  |  Page  
--- PAGE 271 ---
 
BR-09  Profile  Edit  Access:  Only  users  with  the  role  RESIDENT  are  authorized  to  view  and  update  their  personal  profiles  through  this  functionality.  Manager/Staff  profiles  are  managed  separately.  
BR-10  Resource  Optimization  (Image  Compression):  All  media  files  (e.g.,  avatars,  roommate  CCCD  photos,  incident  scene  photos,  completion  proof  photos)  captured  or  selected  by  users  must  be  compressed  on  the  client  side  (Mobile  Application)  to  a  maximum  defined  threshold  (e.g.,  <  500KB  or  max  5MB  for  after  photos)  before  being  transmitted  to  Cloudinary  or  Firebase  Storage.  
BR-11  Change  Auditing:  For  security  purposes,  any  modification  to  a  user's  phone  number  or  key  administrative  actions  (such  as  approvals/rejections)  must  be  logged  in  the  system's  audit  trail,  recording  the  actor's  ID,  timestamp,  and  details  of  the  action  taken.  
BR-12  Active  Lease  Requirement:  The  system  shall  only  calculate  and  display  stay  duration  for  contracts  with  an  ACTIVE  status  in  the  database.  
BR-13  Dynamic  Stay  Calculation:  Remaining  days  of  tenancy  must  be  calculated  dynamically  on  each  load  by  subtracting  the  current  system  date  from  the  contract  end_date.  
BR-14  Extension  Date  Validation:  The  proposed  new  end  date  in  a  stay  extension  request  must  be  in  the  future  and  strictly  after  the  current  lease's  end_date.  
BR-15  Extension  Request  Reason  Constraint:  The  "Reason"  input  for  requesting  a  stay  extension  must  be  strictly  mandatory,  containing  a  minimum  of  10  characters  and  a  maximum  of  500  characters.  
BR-16  Extension  Request  Access  Control:  Only  users  with  the  role  Manager  or  LANDLORD  are  allowed  to  view,  approve,  or  reject  stay  extension  requests.  
BR-17  ACID  Transaction  on  Extension  Approval:  The  stay  extension  approval  process  must  execute  within  a  single  database  transaction  block.  If  updating  either  stay_extensions.status  to  APPROVED  or  contracts.end_date  to  proposed_end_date  fails,  the  entire  transaction  must  rollback.  
 271  |  Page  
--- PAGE 272 ---
 
BR-18  FCM  Notification  Requirement:  Upon  successful  approval  or  rejection  of  a  stay  extension  or  roommate  request,  the  system  must  immediately  dispatch  a  Firebase  FCM  push  notification  to  the  resident's  registered  device  token.  
BR-19  No  Account  Creation  for  Roommates:  Room  members  (roommates)  are  registered  as  demographic  data  only  in  the  database.  The  system  must  not  create  a  corresponding  user  account  (User  entity)  or  provide  login  credentials.  
BR-20  Data  Privacy  on  Checkout:  When  an  apartment  checkout  is  performed,  sensitive  roommate  data  must  be  anonymized,  including  permanently  deleting  physical  ID  card  images  from  Cloudinary  storage  and  masking  identity  numbers  in  the  database.  
BR-21  Roommate  Status  Flow  &  Strict  Workflow:  The  initial  status  of  a  roommate  registration  request  must  be  PENDING.  Only  the  Manager  or  LANDLORD  is  authorized  to  change  the  status  to  APPROVED  or  REJECTED.  Roommates  cannot  gain  access  to  digital  building  amenities  or  receive  gate  pass  permissions  until  their  status  is  APPROVED.  
BR-22  Sensitive  PII  Data  Handling:  The  roommate  ID  Card  images  displayed  in  the  verification  panel  are  classified  as  highly  sensitive  PII.  The  system  must  load  these  images  via  secure,  expiring  HTTPS  URLs  and  prevent  caching  on  the  Manager's  local  device  to  comply  with  data  protection  standards.  
BR-23  Data  Isolation:  A  Resident  can  only  view  the  profiles,  roommates,  and  contract  details  associated  with  their  exact  apartment_id.  Cross-apartment  querying  is  strictly  prohibited.  
BR-24  Primary  Owner  Occupancy  Count:  The  primary  resident  (the  account  holder)  must  be  counted  in  the  "Total  Residents"  calculation  to  accurately  reflect  the  total  physical  occupancy  of  the  apartment.  
BR-25  Index  Validation:  The  input  current  electricity  and  water  indices  must  be  non-negative  real  numbers  and  must  be  greater  than  or  equal  to  their  respective  previous  month's  indices  (curr  >=  prev).  
 272  |  Page  
--- PAGE 273 ---
 
BR-26  Positive  Utility  Consumption  Logic:  The  calculated  consumption  (New  Index  -  Old  Index)  for  both  water  and  electricity  must  be  strictly  $\ge  0$.  The  system  must  reject  any  row  violating  this  mathematical  rule.  
BR-27  Billing  Duplication  Prevention:  The  system  enforces  a  unique  composite  constraint  on  apartment_id  and  month_year.  It  must  reject  the  creation  of  a  bill  if  an  invoice  for  that  exact  month  and  apartment  already  exists.  
BR-28  ACID  Transaction  on  Billing  Generation:  The  bulk  insert  process  for  monthly  billing  must  run  within  a  database  transaction.  If  one  row  fails  critical  validation,  the  entire  batch  must  roll  back  to  prevent  partial  billing  states.  
BR-29  Read-Only  Transaction  History:  The  Transaction  History  module  is  strictly  read-only.  Managers  cannot  manually  add,  edit,  or  delete  any  transaction  records  from  this  interface  to  ensure  absolute  financial  transparency  and  auditability.  
BR-30  Transaction  History  Sorting  &  Pagination:  Upon  initial  page  load,  the  system  must  query  and  display  transaction  records  in  descending  order  based  on  payment_date.  The  system  must  enforce  a  pagination  limit  (e.g.,  10  rows  per  page)  to  optimize  performance.  
BR-31  Billing  History  Isolation:  The  billing  history  of  the  previous  owner  must  not  be  visible  to  the  new  owner.  A  new  owner  must  not  be  able  to  view  the  previous  owner’s  bills,  tickets,  roommate  data,  or  personal  information.  
BR-32  Payment  Integrity:  The  mobile  application  does  not  have  the  authority  to  directly  change  a  bill's  status  to  PAID.  This  status  can  only  be  updated  by  the  Backend  after  receiving  and  successfully  verifying  the  Webhook  from  PayOS  (including  HMAC  hash  signature  verification).  
BR-33  VietQR  Live  Transaction  Precaution:  Because  the  system  handles  live  VietQR  transactions,  all  evaluation  and  defense  demonstration  scenarios  must  use  minimal  denominations  (under  50,000  VND)  to  ensure  financial  safety.  
BR-34  Receipt  Immutability:  The  data  displayed  on  the  digital  receipt  (Total  Amount,  Timestamp,  Reference  ID)  must  be  strictly  read-only  and  immutable,  reflecting  the  exact  payload  confirmed  by  the  payment  gateway  webhook.  
 273  |  Page  
--- PAGE 274 ---
 
BR-35  Receipt  Access  Security:  If  a  receipt  is  accessed  via  a  URL  or  deep  link,  the  system  must  verify  that  the  requesting  user's  resident_id  matches  the  resident  linked  to  the  invoice  to  prevent  unauthorized  access.  
BR-36  Mandatory  Report  Fields:  A  repair  ticket  cannot  be  submitted  without  a  selected  category  and  description  text  (minimum  10  characters  and  maximum  500  characters).  
BR-37  Report  Image  Limits:  The  Resident  can  attach  at  most  3  images  showing  the  issue  before  repair.  Each  image  must  be  in  JPG/PNG  format,  must  not  exceed  5MB,  and  must  undergo  background  compression.  
BR-38  Image  Purge  on  Ticket  Rejection:  If  a  ticket  is  rejected  or  deleted,  all  associated  image  URLs  must  be  purged  from  Cloudinary  storage.  
BR-39  Role-Based  Ticket  Filtering:  The  database  query  must  restrict  ticket  results  based  on  roles:  Residents  can  only  see  tickets  associated  with  their  apartment_id;  Security  Guards  can  only  see  tickets  where  assigned_guard_id  matches  their  ID;  Managers  have  read  access  to  all  tickets.  
BR-40  Ticket  Assignment  Constraints:  Only  users  with  the  Manager  role  are  authorized  to  assign  or  reassign  repair  tickets.  A  ticket  can  only  be  assigned  if  its  current  status  is  PENDING.  
BR-41  Workload  Indicator:  The  system  must  fetch  real-time  workload  counts  (number  of  currently  active  tickets  in  status  PROCESSING)  for  all  technicians  and  display  correct  status  tags  (e.g.,  Available  /  Busy  X  task(s))  to  aid  Manager  decision-making.  
BR-42  Technician  Task  Access:  Only  the  specific  technician  assigned  to  the  ticket  (or  an  Manager)  is  authorized  to  log  repair  results  and  update  task  progress.  The  task  list  must  only  display  tickets  that  have  been  explicitly  assigned  to  the  logged-in  guard.  
BR-43  Resolution  Proof  Requirement:  A  technician  cannot  mark  a  task  status  as  RESOLVED  (or  transition  to  WAITING_FOR_RATING)  without  uploading  at  least  one  photo  under  the  "Proof  Upload  Box"  to  prevent  fraudulent  completion  logs.  
BR-44  Notification  Accuracy:  The  system  must  capture  and  store  the  device's  unique  FCM  Token  upon  a  successful  user  login  and  strictly  destroy  this  token  entry  in  the  PostgreSQL  database  upon  logout.  This  ensures  sensitive  notifications  are  not  pushed  to  
 274  |  Page  
--- PAGE 275 ---
 
the  wrong  recipient  after  an  account  or  resident  profile  switch  on  the  same  physical  smartphone  device.   
BR-45  Chat  Scope  &  Isolation:  Each  chat  room  is  isolated.  Only  residents  linked  directly  to  that  room  and  authenticated  users  under  the  Manager  role  are  granted  authorization  to  read  or  subscribe  to  that  specific  Pusher  channel.  
BR-46  Dashboard  Statistics  Integrity:  Dashboard  statistics  must  be  calculated  from  reliable  system  records  and  must  be  read-only.  Revenue  collection  statistics  must  be  calculated  from  bill  records  in  the  selected  reporting  period,  where  collected  revenue  is  based  only  on  bills  with  status  PAID  and  unpaid  bill  count  is  based  only  on  bills  with  status  UNPAID.  Unresolved  ticket  statistics  must  include  tickets  in  status  PENDING  and  PROCESSING,  and  must  exclude  tickets  that  are  WAITING_FOR_RATING,  CLOSED,  or  REJECTED.  
BR-47  Apartment  Status  Constraints:  An  apartment  can  only  be  assigned  to  a  maximum  of  one  (01)  Resident  (Owner)  at  any  given  time.  A  new  owner  can  only  be  assigned  to  an  apartment  whose  status  is  EMPTY.  Checkout  can  only  be  performed  on  an  apartment  whose  status  is  OCCUPIED  and  has  a  valid  current  owner.  
BR-48  Historical  Billing  Rent  Protection:  Updating  the  base  rent  of  an  apartment  must  not  modify  old  invoices  or  old  contract  rent  snapshots  that  were  already  created  before  the  update.  
BR-49  Data  Retention  Policy:  User  accounts  and  billing  records  must  never  be  hard-deleted  from  the  database.  When  an  owner  moves  out,  their  account  status  is  strictly  marked  as  INACTIVE  (Soft  Delete)  to  maintain  the  integrity  of  financial  audit  trails.  
BR-50  Staff  Deactivation  Constraint:  A  staff  account  must  not  be  deactivated  while  unresolved  assigned  tickets  still  exist;  the  Manager  must  reassign  or  resolve  those  tickets  before  deactivation.  
BR-51  Notification  Pagination:  To  optimize  database  queries  and  client  rendering  performance,  the  notification  list  must  implement  pagination  (e.g.,  fetching  20  records  per  request)  utilizing  lazy  loading  or  infinite  scrolling  as  the  user  scrolls  down  the  list.  
BR-52  Notification  Sorting  Priority:  Notifications  must  always  be  sorted  by  their  creation  timestamp  in  descending  order,  ensuring  the  most  recent  alerts  appear  at  the  top.  
 275  |  Page  
--- PAGE 276 ---
 
BR-53  Notification  Data  Isolation:  Users  can  only  retrieve  and  view  notifications  that  are  explicitly  addressed  to  their  specific user_id  or  broadcasted  to  their  assigned  role/apartment.  Cross-user  notification  querying  is  strictly  prohibited.  
BR-54  Asynchronous  Read  Update:  The  action  of  marking  a  notification  as  'Read'  must  be  processed  asynchronously  by  the  backend  to  ensure  it  does  not  block  or  delay  the  UI  rendering  of  the  detail  screen  for  the  user.  
BR-55  Notification  Immutability:  The  content  of  a  notification  displayed  in  the  detail  view  (Title,  Body,  Timestamp,  Sender)  is  strictly  read-only  for  the  receiving  user.  Users  cannot  edit,  reply  directly,  or  forward  the  system  notification.  
BR-56  Actionable  Deep  Linking:  If  a  notification  is  linked  to  a  specific  system  entity,  the  Notification  Detail  screen  must  provide  an  actionable  button  or  deep  link  that  routes  the  user  directly  to  the  corresponding  entity's  detail  screen.  
BR-57  ManagerManagement  Authorization:  Only  system  accounts  explicitly  assigned  the  highest  administrative  role  (e.g.,  LANDLORD)  hold  the  permissions  required  to  access  the  Manager  Management  Screen  to  create,  update,  or  deactivate  other  Manager  accounts.  
BR-58  Self-Deactivation  Prevention:  The  system  must  strictly  prevent  the  currently  logged-in  Landlord/Super  Manager  from  deactivating  their  own  account.  The  deactivation  button/action  must  be  disabled  or  hidden  for  the  active  user's  own  row  to  avoid  locking  the  management  system  entirely.  
BR-59  Manager  Audit  Immutability:  When  an  Manager  account  is  deactivated  (status  set  to  INACTIVE),  all  historical  actions,  tickets  handled,  audit  logs,  and  system  changes  previously  performed  by  that  account  must  remain  fully  intact  and  visible  in  the  database.  Hard  deletion  is  strictly  prohibited.  
BR-60  Role-Based  Screen  Access:  The  system  must  strictly  enforce  role-based  authorization  before  granting  access  to  any  management  or  operational  screen.  If  a  user  attempts  to  access  a  screen  or  feature  that  is  not  permitted  for  their  assigned  role  (e.g.,  Manager,  LANDLORD,  RESIDENT,  SECURITY_GUARD),  the  system  must  block  the  request  and  display  an  appropriate  "Access  Denied"  error  message.  
BR-61  Apartment  UI  Display  &  Indicators:  All  apartment  lists,  grids,  and  detail  screens  must  dynamically  reflect  the  real-time  operational  indicators  and  life-cycle  statuses  (e.g.,  EMPTY,  OCCUPIED,  INACTIVE)  directly  fetched  from  the  database.  This  includes  displaying  accurate,  up-to-date  counters  for  pending  actions,  such  as  the  total  number  of  unpaid  invoices  or  unresolved  maintenance  tickets  associated  with  each  apartment.   
 276  |  Page  
--- PAGE 277 ---
 
BR-62  UI  Navigation  &  Read-Only  Control:  Basic  user  interface  operations—including  screen  navigation,  searching,  filtering,  sorting,  and  viewing  details—must  function  strictly  as  read-only  processes.  Under  no  circumstances  shall  performing  these  navigation  or  query  actions  trigger  any  state  changes,  modifications,  insertions,  or  deletions  within  the  system  database.   
BR-63  Form  Data  Validation  &  Integrity:  The  system  must  perform  comprehensive  data  validation  on  all  creation  and  update  forms  before  processing  any  business  logic  or  persisting  data.  Validation  rules  dictate  that  all  mandatory  fields  must  not  be  null,  numeric  parameters  (such  as  room  area  size  and  monthly  base  rent)  must  be  strictly  positive  values  greater  than  zero,  and  unique  identifiers  (such  as  Room  Number)  must  not  conflict  with  any  existing  records  in  the  database.   
BR-64  Lifecycle  Separation:  The  system  must  ensure  that  updating  the  basic  physical  or  structural  information  of  an  apartment  (such  as  floor,  room  number,  or  area  size)  is  strictly  isolated  from  its  operational  lifecycle.  Executing  such  updates  must  not  alter,  reset,  or  affect  the  current  apartment  status  (e.g.,  EMPTY,  OCCUPIED),  active  resident/owner  assignments,  or  any  ongoing  rental  contracts.   
BR-65  Transaction  Safety:  The  system  must  execute  critical  multi-step  operations  (such  as  apartment  check-in  and  check-out  processing)  as  a  single,  atomic  ACID  transaction.  If  any  sub-step  fails  during  the  process  (e.g.,  account  provisioning,  contract  creation,  or  database  status  updates),  the  entire  transaction  must  be  completely  rolled  back  to  maintain  absolute  data  integrity  and  prevent  partial  corruption.

5.4  Other  Requirements  5.4.1  Development  &  Testing  Requirements  -  Git  Architecture:  The  repository  must  be  separated  by  feature  folders  (e.g.,  /features/billing)  
to
 
avoid
 
code
 
conflicts
 
among
 
the
 
5
 
team
 
members.
 
-  State  Management:  The  app  must  strictly  use  Riverpod  to  enforce  a  Unidirectional  Data  
Flow,
 
decoupling
 
UI
 
components
 
from
 
business
 
logic.
 
-  Mandatory  Automated  Tests:  Every  pull  request  requires  at  least  one  (01)  Unit  Test  for  
validation
 
logic
 
and
 
one
 
(01)
 
Widget
 
Test
 
for
 
core
 
UI
 
behavior.
 
5.4.2  Operational  &  Deployment  Constraints  -  Demo  Safety:  Because  the  system  handles  live  VietQR  transactions,  all  evaluation  and  
defense
 
demos
 
must
 
use
 
minimal
 
denominations
 
(under
 
50,000
 
VND).
 
-  Database  Seeding:  The  Supabase  PostgreSQL  database  must  be  pre-populated  with  baseline  
data
 
(1
 
Landlord,
 
10
 
Managers,
 
1
 
Staff,
 
140
 
residents
 
and
 
70
 
apartments)
 
before
 
the
 
project
 
defense.
 
5.4.3  Third-Party  Service  Integrity  -  FCM  Token  Lifecycle:  Firebase  tokens  must  be  explicitly  cleared  from  the  database  during  
logout
 
to
 
prevent
 
cross-account
 
notification
 
leaks.
 
-  Real-time  Infrastructure:  The  system  must  utilize  Pusher  for  Live  Chat  events  to  bypass  the  
connection
 
limits
 
of
 
a
 
Serverless
 
Next.js
 
backend.
